# Chandogya Upanishad

### 1.1.0

ॐ आप्यायन्तु ममाङ्गानि वाक्प्राणश्चक्षुः  
श्रोत्रमथो बलमिन्द्रियाणि च सर्वाणि ।  
सर्वं ब्रह्मौपनिषदं माहं ब्रह्म निराकुर्यां मा मा ब्रह्म  
निराकरोदनिराकरणमस्त्वनिराकरणंं मेऽस्तु ।  
तदात्मनि निरते य उपनिषत्सु धर्मास्ते  
मयि सन्तु ते मयि सन्तु ॥  
॥ ॐ शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिः ॥

*oṃ āpyāyantu mamāṅgāni vākprāṇaścakṣuḥ  
śrotramatho balamindriyāṇi ca sarvāṇi .  
sarvaṃ brahmaupaniṣadaṃ māhaṃ brahma nirākuryāṃ mā mā brahma  
nirākarodanirākaraṇamastvanirākaraṇaṃṃ me'stu .  
tadātmani nirate ya upaniṣatsu dharmāste  
mayi santu te mayi santu ..  
.. oṃ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ ..*

Oṃ! May my limbs and organs, my speech, my breath, my strength, and my senses such as the ears and eyes be well nourished.
All is Brahman, as the Upanishads clearly teach.
May I never deny Brahman. May Brahman never deny me. Let there be no denial of Brahman at all. Let there be no denial of Brahman on my part.
I pray also that the virtues described in the Upanishads, belonging to the wise who dwell in the Ātma (the Soul), may become part of me and live in me.
Oṃ, peace, peace, peace.

### 1.1.1

॥ प्रथमोऽध्यायः ॥ ओमित्येतदक्षरमुद्गीथमुपासीत । ओमिति ह्युद्गायति तस्योपव्याख्यानम् ॥ १.१.१ ॥

*|| prathamo'dhyāyaḥ || omityetadakṣaramudgīthamupāsīta | omiti hyudgāyati tasyopavyākhyānam || 1.1.1 ||*

No word comes nearer to Brahman than Om. Chant this Om as an act of worship of Brahman. (In other words, take Om as Brahman's symbol and focus on the idea that the two are one.) The way to chant this Om is explained next.

### 1.1.2

एषां भूतानां पृथिवी रसः पृथिव्या अपो रसः । अपामोषधयो रस ओषधीनां पुरुषो रसः पुरुषस्य वाग्रसो वाच ऋग्रस ऋचः साम रसः साम्न उद्गीथो रसः ॥ १.१.२ ॥

*eṣāṃ bhūtānāṃ pṛthivī rasaḥ pṛthivyā apo rasaḥ | apāmoṣadhayo rasa oṣadhīnāṃ puruṣo rasaḥ puruṣasya vāgraso vāca ṛgrasa ṛcaḥ sāma rasaḥ sāmna udgītho rasaḥ || 1.1.2 ||*

All things, living and non-living, have the earth as their essence. Water is the earth's essence, and plants are the essence of water. Human beings are the essence of plants, and speech is the essence of human beings. The Ṛg Veda is the essence of speech, the Sāma Veda is the essence of the Ṛg Veda, and the udhītha is the essence of the Sāma Veda.

### 1.1.3

स एष रसानांरसतमः परमः परार्ध्योऽष्टमो यदुद्गीथः ॥ १.१.३ ॥

*sa eṣa rasānāṃrasatamaḥ paramaḥ parārdhyo'ṣṭamo yadudgīthaḥ || 1.1.3 ||*

Among all essences this udgītha (Om) is the finest. It surpasses everything that exists. It is the eighth, and it holds the highest rank.

### 1.1.4

कतमा कतमर्क्कतमत्कतमत्साम कतमः कतम उद्गीथ इति विमृष्टं भवति ॥ १.१.४ ॥

*katamā katamarkkatamatkatamatsāma katamaḥ katama udgītha iti vimṛṣṭaṃ bhavati || 1.1.4 ||*

What are the Ṛks? What are the Sāmas? What are the udgīthas? That is the question.

### 1.1.5

वागेवर्क्प्राणः सामोमित्येतदक्षरमुद्गीथः । तद्वा एतन्मिथुनं यद्वाक्च प्राणश्चर्क्च साम च ॥ १.१.५ ॥

*vāgevarkprāṇaḥ sāmomityetadakṣaramudgīthaḥ | tadvā etanmithunaṃ yadvākca prāṇaścarkca sāma ca || 1.1.5 ||*

In answer to that question: Ṛk is speech, Sāma is prāṇa (life), and udgītha (Brahman) itself is simply Om. These form pairs: speech and life, Ṛk and Sāma.

### 1.1.6

तदेतन्मिथुनमोमित्येतस्मिन्नक्षरे संसृज्यते यदा वै मिथुनौ समागच्छत आपयतो वै तावन्योन्यस्य कामम् ॥ १.१.६ ॥

*tadetanmithunamomityetasminnakṣare saṃsṛjyate yadā vai mithunau samāgacchata āpayato vai tāvanyonyasya kāmam || 1.1.6 ||*

This pair of speech and life blend into one another and become one in the syllable Om, much as a man and a woman come together and satisfy each other's desires.

### 1.1.7

आपयिता ह वै कामानां भवति य एतदेवं विद्वानक्षरमुद्गीथमुपास्ते ॥ १.१.७ ॥

*āpayitā ha vai kāmānāṃ bhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānakṣaramudgīthamupāste || 1.1.7 ||*

Whoever worships Om as the udgītha (Brahman), understanding it as what receives everything, in the end receives everything he wants.

### 1.1.8

तद्वा एतदनुज्ञाक्षरं यद्धि किंचानुजानात्योमित्येव तदाहैषो एव समृद्धिर्यदनुज्ञा समर्धयिता ह वै कामानां भवति य एतदेवं विद्वानक्षरमुद्गीथमुपास्ते ॥ १.१.८ ॥

*tadvā etadanujñākṣaraṃ yaddhi kiṃcānujānātyomityeva tadāhaiṣo eva samṛddhiryadanujñā samardhayitā ha vai kāmānāṃ bhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānakṣaramudgīthamupāste || 1.1.8 ||*

That akṣaram (Om) signals agreement: whenever someone wishes to say yes, he says Om. This Om is the key to progress. Whoever worships Om as the udgītha (Brahman), understanding it in this way as the fulfiller of all desires, has all his desires fulfilled.

### 1.1.9

तेनेयं त्रयीविद्या वर्तते ओमित्याश्रावयत्योमिति शंसत्योमित्युद्गायत्येतस्यैवाक्षरस्यापचित्यै महिम्ना रसेन ॥ १.१.९ ॥

*teneyaṃ trayīvidyā vartate omityāśrāvayatyomiti śaṃsatyomityudgāyatyetasyaivākṣarasyāpacityai mahimnā rasena || 1.1.9 ||*

The threefold Vedic ritual begins with Om. Recitation of the Vedas begins with Om. The singing of Vedic hymns begins with Om, and with Om the udgān from the Vedas is sung in praise of Om, or Brahman. All of this honors Om. And all of it is made possible by the essence drawn from Om, in the form of wheat and other food.

### 1.1.10

तेनोभौ कुरुतो यश्चैतदेवं वेद यश्च न वेद । नाना तु विद्या चाविद्या च यदेव विद्यया करोति श्रद्धयोपनिषदा तदेव वीर्यवत्तरं भवतीति खल्वेतस्यैवाक्षरस्योपव्याख्यानं भवति ॥ १.१.१० ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*tenobhau kuruto yaścaitadevaṃ veda yaśca na veda | nānā tu vidyā cāvidyā ca yadeva vidyayā karoti śraddhayopaniṣadā tadeva vīryavattaraṃ bhavatīti khalvetasyaivākṣarasyopavyākhyānaṃ bhavati || 1.1.10 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Both the one who knows about Om and the one who does not draw the strength for their work from Om. But knowledge and ignorance bring different results. Whatever is done with knowledge of Om, with faith in the teachers and scriptures, and in keeping with the principles of the Upaniṣads (or of yoga), yields more. This surely is the right way to honor Om.

### 1.2.1

देवासुरा ह वै यत्र संयेतिरे उभये प्राजापत्यास्तद्ध देवा उद्गीथमाजह्रुरनेनैनानभिभविष्याम इति ॥ १.२.१ ॥

*devāsurā ha vai yatra saṃyetire ubhaye prājāpatyāstaddha devā udgīthamājahruranenainānabhibhaviṣyāma iti || 1.2.1 ||*

The gods and goddesses and the demons were all children of Prajāpati, yet they fought one another. So the gods and goddesses took up the path of the udgītha, expecting that it would let them defeat the demons.

### 1.2.2

ते ह नासिक्यं प्राणमुद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तं हासुराः पाप्मना विविधुस्तस्मात्तेनोभयं जिघ्रति सुरभि च दुर्गन्धि च पाप्मना ह्येष विद्धः ॥ १.२.२ ॥

*te ha nāsikyaṃ prāṇamudgīthamupāsāṃcakrire taṃ hāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhustasmāttenobhayaṃ jighrati surabhi ca durgandhi ca pāpmanā hyeṣa viddhaḥ || 1.2.2 ||*

The gods and goddesses worshipped the prāṇa that governs the nostrils as udgītha. The demons misused it, since for them it was only a smelling organ. Because of this misuse, people smell both pleasant and foul odors through the nostrils.

### 1.2.3

अथ ह वाचमुद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तां हासुराः पाप्मना विविधुस्तस्मात्तयोभयं वदति सत्यं चानृतं च पाप्मना ह्येषा विद्धा ॥ १.२.३ ॥

*atha ha vācamudgīthamupāsāṃcakrire tāṃ hāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhustasmāttayobhayaṃ vadati satyaṃ cānṛtaṃ ca pāpmanā hyeṣā viddhā || 1.2.3 ||*

The gods and goddesses next worshipped speech as udgītha, using speech to praise Om. But the demons pierced it with evil, misusing it out of ignorance. So people use the organ of speech for both truth and falsehood, because speech was pierced with evil.

### 1.2.4

अथ ह चक्षुरुद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तद्धासुराः पाप्मना विविधुस्तस्मात्तेनोभयं पश्यति दर्शनीयं चादर्शनीयं च पाप्मना ह्येतद्विद्धम् ॥ १.२.४ ॥

*atha ha cakṣurudgīthamupāsāṃcakrire taddhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhustasmāttenobhayaṃ paśyati darśanīyaṃ cādarśanīyaṃ ca pāpmanā hyetadviddham || 1.2.4 ||*

The gods and goddesses next worshipped the eye as udgītha, as a praise to Om. But the demons pierced it with evil, misusing it out of ignorance. So people see both good and bad things with their eyes. Ignorance is why they see both.

### 1.2.5

अथ ह श्रोत्रमुद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तद्धासुराः पाप्मना विविधुस्तस्मात्तेनोभयं शृणोति श्रवणीयं चाश्रवणीयं च पाप्मना ह्येतद्विद्धम् ॥ १.२.५ ॥

*atha ha śrotramudgīthamupāsāṃcakrire taddhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhustasmāttenobhayaṃ śṛṇoti śravaṇīyaṃ cāśravaṇīyaṃ ca pāpmanā hyetadviddham || 1.2.5 ||*

The gods and goddesses next worshipped the faculty of hearing as udgītha, in order to praise Om. But the demons pierced it with evil, misusing it out of ignorance. As a result, people's ears take in both pleasant and unpleasant things. Ignorance is the reason.

### 1.2.6

अथ ह मन उद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तद्धासुराः पाप्मना विविधुस्तस्मात्तेनोभयंसंकल्पते संकल्पनीयंच चासंकल्पनीयं च पाप्मना ह्येतद्विद्धम् ॥ १.२.६ ॥

*atha ha mana udgīthamupāsāṃcakrire taddhāsurāḥ pāpmanā vividhustasmāttenobhayaṃsaṃkalpate saṃkalpanīyaṃca cāsaṃkalpanīyaṃ ca pāpmanā hyetadviddham || 1.2.6 ||*

The gods and goddesses next worshipped the mind, since the mind enables them to chant the udgītha, the praise to Om. But the demons corrupted even the mind. So the mind has both good and bad thoughts. Ignorance is the reason.

### 1.2.7

अथ ह य एवायं मुख्यः प्राणस्तमुद्गीथमुपासांचक्रिरे तंहासुरा ऋत्वा विदध्वंसुर्यथाश्मानमाखणमृत्वा विध्वंसेतैवम् ॥ १.२.७ ॥

*atha ha ya evāyaṃ mukhyaḥ prāṇastamudgīthamupāsāṃcakrire taṃhāsurā ṛtvā vidadhvaṃsuryathāśmānamākhaṇamṛtvā vidhvaṃsetaivam || 1.2.7 ||*

The gods and goddesses then worshipped the chief prāṇa as udgītha. The demons all perished against prāṇa, the way clumps of earth shatter when they strike a stone that cannot be broken.

### 1.2.8

यथाश्मानमाखणमृत्वा विध्वंसत एवं हैव स विध्वंसते य एवंविदि पापं कामयते यश्चैनमभिदासति स एषोऽश्माखणः ॥ १.२.८ ॥

*yathāśmānamākhaṇamṛtvā vidhvaṃsata evaṃ haiva sa vidhvaṃsate ya evaṃvidi pāpaṃ kāmayate yaścainamabhidāsati sa eṣo'śmākhaṇaḥ || 1.2.8 ||*

Clumps of earth thrown at an unbreakable stone are themselves turned to dust. In the same way, anyone who wishes ill to a knower of prāṇa, or harms such a person, brings about his own ruin. The knower of prāṇa cannot be hurt, like a piece of unbreakable stone.

### 1.2.9

नैवैतेन सुरभि न दुर्गन्धि विजानात्यपहतपाप्मा ह्येष तेन यदश्नाति यत्पिबति तेनेतरान्प्राणानवति एतमु एवान्ततोऽवित्त्वोत्क्रमति व्याददात्येवान्तत इति ॥ १.२.९ ॥

*naivaitena surabhi na durgandhi vijānātyapahatapāpmā hyeṣa tena yadaśnāti yatpibati tenetarānprāṇānavati etamu evāntato'vittvotkramati vyādadātyevāntata iti || 1.2.9 ||*

For the chief prāṇa there is no good or bad smell, because it is pure and is never touched by any evil born of egotism. Whatever this prāṇa eats and drinks, it does so only to keep the sense organs, such as the eyes and ears, alive. At death the chief prāṇa takes no food or drink, and so the sense organs collapse and seem to have left the body. Yet they still want to eat and drink in order to live, as is shown by the fact that a person dies with the mouth open.

### 1.2.10

तं हाङ्गिरा उद्गीथमुपासांचक्र एतमु एवाङ्गिरसं मन्यन्तेऽङ्गानां यद्रसः ॥ १.२.१० ॥

*taṃ hāṅgirā udgīthamupāsāṃcakra etamu evāṅgirasaṃ manyante'ṅgānāṃ yadrasaḥ || 1.2.10 ||*

The sage Aṅgirā worshipped the chief prāṇa as udgītha, that is, as Brahman, to whom the udgītha is addressed. The chief prāṇa is called āṅgirasa because it is the rasa (essence, or support) of all the aṅgas (organs).

### 1.2.11

तेन तं ह बृहस्पतिरुद्गीथमुपासांचक्र एतमु एव बृहस्पतिं मन्यन्ते वाग्घि बृहती तस्या एष पतिः ॥ १.२.११ ॥

*tena taṃ ha bṛhaspatirudgīthamupāsāṃcakra etamu eva bṛhaspatiṃ manyante vāgghi bṛhatī tasyā eṣa patiḥ || 1.2.11 ||*

For this reason Bṛhaspati worshipped prāṇa as udgītha. Prāṇa is considered Bṛhaspati because vāk (speech) is great (bṛhatī) and prāṇa is its lord (pati).

### 1.2.12

तेन तं हायास्य उद्गीथमुपासांचक्र एतमु एवायास्यं मन्यन्त आस्याद्यदयते ॥ १.२.१२ ॥

*tena taṃ hāyāsya udgīthamupāsāṃcakra etamu evāyāsyaṃ manyanta āsyādyadayate || 1.2.12 ||*

For this reason Āyāsya worshipped prāṇa as udgītha. They considered it Āyāsya because it comes (ayate) out of the mouth (āsyāt).

### 1.2.13

तेन तंह बको दाल्भ्यो विदांचकार । स ह नैमिशीयानामुद्गाता बभूव स ह स्मैभ्यः कामानागायति ॥ १.२.१३ ॥

*tena taṃha bako dālbhyo vidāṃcakāra | sa ha naimiśīyānāmudgātā babhūva sa ha smaibhyaḥ kāmānāgāyati || 1.2.13 ||*

The sage Baka, son of Dalbha, came to know prāṇa as it truly was. So the sages of the Naimiṣa forest chose him to sing their udgītha, and he in turn fulfilled their wishes.

### 1.2.14

आगाता ह वै कामानां भवति य एतदेवं विद्वानक्षरमुद्गीथमुपास्त इत्यध्यात्मम् ॥ १.२.१४ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*āgātā ha vai kāmānāṃ bhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānakṣaramudgīthamupāsta ityadhyātmam || 1.2.14 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Someone who knows the true meaning of prāṇa and worships it as udgītha akṣara, that is, as Akṣara Brahman, himself becomes Akṣara Brahman. He then worships everyone he wishes to worship, for in singing for prāṇa he sings for all, and he gains everything he desires. This is the attainment at the level of the body (adhyātma). The inner attainment is that he becomes one with prāṇa, which is Akṣara Brahman.

### 1.3.1

अथाधिदैवतं य एवासौ तपति तमुद्गीथमुपासीतोद्यन्वा एष प्रजाभ्य उद्गायति । उद्यंस्तमो भयमपहन्त्यपहन्ता ह वै भयस्य तमसो भवति य एवं वेद ॥ १.३.१ ॥

*athādhidaivataṃ ya evāsau tapati tamudgīthamupāsītodyanvā eṣa prajābhya udgāyati | udyaṃstamo bhayamapahantyapahantā ha vai bhayasya tamaso bhavati ya evaṃ veda || 1.3.1 ||*

Next comes worship from the standpoint of the forces of nature. There is the sun, rising to give us heat. Worship it as udgītha. The sun rises as if to pray for the good of all living beings, and in rising it removes the fear of darkness. Whoever knows this overcomes the fear of ignorance about birth and death.

### 1.3.2

समान उ एवायं चासौ चोष्णोऽयमुष्णोऽसौ स्वर इतीममाचक्षते स्वर इति प्रत्यास्वर इत्यमुं तस्माद्वा एतमिमममुं चोद्गीथमुपासीत ॥ १.३.२ ॥

*samāna u evāyaṃ cāsau coṣṇo'yamuṣṇo'sau svara itīmamācakṣate svara iti pratyāsvara ityamuṃ tasmādvā etamimamamuṃ codgīthamupāsīta || 1.3.2 ||*

This prāṇa and that sun are alike. Prāṇa is warm, and so is the sun. Prāṇa is called svara when it is 'going out' at death. The sun too is described as svara when it 'sets' and as pratyāsvara when it 'comes back.' So worship both prāṇa and the sun as udgītha.

### 1.3.3

अथ खलु व्यानमेवोद्गीथमुपासीत यद्वै प्राणिति स प्राणो यदपानिति सोऽपानः । अथ यः प्राणापानयोः संधिः स व्यानो यो व्यानः सा वाक् । तस्मादप्राणन्ननपानन्वाचमभिव्याहरति ॥ १.३.३ ॥

*atha khalu vyānamevodgīthamupāsīta yadvai prāṇiti sa prāṇo yadapāniti so'pānaḥ | atha yaḥ prāṇāpānayoḥ saṃdhiḥ sa vyāno yo vyānaḥ sā vāk | tasmādaprāṇannanapānanvācamabhivyāharati || 1.3.3 ||*

Worship vyāna, the breath held between prāṇa and apāna that makes speech possible, as udgītha. Prāṇa is the breath taken in and apāna is the breath let out. Vyāna is the breath held between them. Vyāna is also called vāk (speech), since a person must hold the breath in order to speak.

### 1.3.4

या वाक्सर्क्तस्मादप्राणन्ननपानन्नृचमभिव्याहरति यर्क्तत्साम तस्मादप्राणन्ननपानन्साम गायति यत्साम स उद्गीथस्तस्मादप्राणन्ननपानन्नुद्गायति ॥ १.३.४ ॥

*yā vāksarktasmādaprāṇannanapānannṛcamabhivyāharati yarktatsāma tasmādaprāṇannanapānansāma gāyati yatsāma sa udgīthastasmādaprāṇannanapānannudgāyati || 1.3.4 ||*

Whatever is vāk (speech) is also the Ṛk (part of the Ṛg Veda). So a person neither inhales nor exhales while reciting the Ṛk mantras. Whatever is the Ṛk is also the Sāma. So the Sāma is recited without inhaling or exhaling. What is the Sāma is also the udgītha. So when singing the udgītha one stops both inhaling and exhaling.

### 1.3.5

अतो यान्यन्यानि वीर्यवन्ति कर्माणि यथाग्नेर्मन्थनमाजेः सरणं दृढस्य धनुष आयमनमप्राणन्ननपानंस्तानि करोत्येतस्य हेतोर्व्यानमेवोद्गीथमुपासीत ॥ १.३.५ ॥

*ato yānyanyāni vīryavanti karmāṇi yathāgnermanthanamājeḥ saraṇaṃ dṛḍhasya dhanuṣa āyamanamaprāṇannanapānaṃstāni karotyetasya hetorvyānamevodgīthamupāsīta || 1.3.5 ||*

For this reason, during feats that demand great strength, such as making fire by rubbing one stick against another, running to a target, or bending a stiff bow, a person neither inhales nor exhales. Therefore this holding of the breath, called vyāna, should be worshipped as udgītha (Om).

### 1.3.6

अथ खलूद्गीथाक्षराण्युपासीतोद्गीथ इति प्राण एवोत्प्राणेन ह्युत्तिष्ठति वाग्गीर्वाचो ह गिर इत्याचक्षतेऽन्नं थमन्ने हीदंसर्वंस्थितम् ॥ १.३.६ ॥

*atha khalūdgīthākṣarāṇyupāsītodgītha iti prāṇa evotprāṇena hyuttiṣṭhati vāggīrvāco ha gira ityācakṣate'nnaṃ thamanne hīdaṃsarvaṃsthitam || 1.3.6 ||*

Next, the syllables ut, gī, and tha in the word udgītha should each be worshipped separately. Ut stands for prāṇa, for prāṇa is responsible for the

### 1.3.7

द्यौरेवोदन्तरिक्षं गीः पृथिवी थमादित्य एवोद्वायुर्गीरग्निस्थं सामवेद एवोद्यजुर्वेदो गीरृग्वेदस्थं दुग्धेऽस्मै वाग्दोहं यो वाचो दोहोऽन्नवानन्नादो भवति य एतान्येवं विद्वानुद्गीथाक्षराण्युपास्त उद्गीथ इति ॥ १.३.७ ॥

*dyaurevodantarikṣaṃ gīḥ pṛthivī thamāditya evodvāyurgīragnisthaṃ sāmaveda evodyajurvedo gīrṛgvedasthaṃ dugdhe'smai vāgdohaṃ yo vāco doho'nnavānannādo bhavati ya etānyevaṃ vidvānudgīthākṣarāṇyupāsta udgītha iti || 1.3.7 ||*

Heaven is ut, the space between heaven and earth is gī, and the earth is tha. The sun is ut, air is gī, and fire is tha. The Sāma Veda is ut, the Yajur Veda is gī, and the Ṛg Veda is tha. When a seeker knows all this, the scriptures disclose their meaning to him. Whoever worships ut-gī-tha in this way obtains abundant food and also eats abundant food. Such a person also gains enlightenment.

### 1.3.8

अथ खल्वाशीःसमृद्धिरुपसरणानीत्युपासीत येन साम्ना स्तोष्यन्स्यात्तत्सामोपधावेत् ॥ १.३.८ ॥

*atha khalvāśīḥsamṛddhirupasaraṇānītyupāsīta yena sāmnā stoṣyansyāttatsāmopadhāvet || 1.3.8 ||*

Here is guidance on how to reach the goals one wants. Keep meditating on those goals. Pray for them as well by singing the fitting Sāma, bearing in mind that the Sāma is the source of what you are requesting.

### 1.3.9

यस्यामृचि तामृचं यदार्षेयं तमृषिं यां देवतामभिष्टोष्यन्स्यात्तां देवतामुपधावेत् ॥ १.३.९ ॥

*yasyāmṛci tāmṛcaṃ yadārṣeyaṃ tamṛṣiṃ yāṃ devatāmabhiṣṭoṣyansyāttāṃ devatāmupadhāvet || 1.3.9 ||*

Meditate on the Ṛk from which this Sāma comes, on the sage who conceived this Sāma prayer, and on the deity to whom the prayer is directed.

### 1.3.10

येन च्छन्दसा स्तोष्यन्स्यात्तच्छन्द उपधावेद्येन स्तोमेन स्तोष्यमाणः स्यात्तंस्तोममुपधावेत् ॥ १.३.१० ॥

*yena cchandasā stoṣyansyāttacchanda upadhāvedyena stomena stoṣyamāṇaḥ syāttaṃstomamupadhāvet || 1.3.10 ||*

When praying, one may use the Gāyatrī or another hymn, but one should meditate on it. One may also use several Sāma mantras in prayer, but one must meditate on them along with the deities they address.

### 1.3.11

यां दिशमभिष्टोष्यन्स्यात्तां दिशमुपधावेत् ॥ १.३.११ ॥

*yāṃ diśamabhiṣṭoṣyansyāttāṃ diśamupadhāvet || 1.3.11 ||*

Whichever direction one faces in prayer, one should meditate on that direction along with its presiding deities.

### 1.3.12

आत्मानमन्तत उपसृत्य स्तुवीत कामं ध्यायन्नप्रमत्तोऽभ्याशो ह यदस्मै स कामः समृध्येत यत्कामः स्तुवीतेति यत्कामः स्तुवीतेति ॥ १.३.१२ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*ātmānamantata upasṛtya stuvīta kāmaṃ dhyāyannapramatto'bhyāśo ha yadasmai sa kāmaḥ samṛdhyeta yatkāmaḥ stuvīteti yatkāmaḥ stuvīteti || 1.3.12 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Finally, on ending his prayer, a person should ask for whatever he wants, taking care to pronounce correctly. He should also reflect on himself, including his name, lineage, and caste. Then any desire he holds while praying is quickly and surely fulfilled.

### 1.4.1

ओमित्येतदक्षरमुद्गीथमुपासीतोमिति ह्युद्गायति तस्योपव्याख्यानम् ॥ १.४.१ ॥

*omityetadakṣaramudgīthamupāsītomiti hyudgāyati tasyopavyākhyānam || 1.4.1 ||*

No word comes nearer to Brahman than Om. Chant this Om as an act of worship of Brahman. (In other words, take Om as Brahman's symbol and focus on the idea that the two are one.) The way to chant this Om is explained next.

### 1.4.2

देवा वै मृत्योर्बिभ्यतस्त्रयीं विद्यां प्राविशंस्ते छन्दोभिरच्छादयन्यदेभिरच्छादयंस्तच्छन्दसां छन्दस्त्वम् ॥ १.४.२ ॥

*devā vai mṛtyorbibhyatastrayīṃ vidyāṃ prāviśaṃste chandobhiracchādayanyadebhiracchādayaṃstacchandasāṃ chandastvam || 1.4.2 ||*

Fearing death, the gods and goddesses sought shelter in the rites and rituals of the three Vedas. They wrapped themselves, so to speak, in mantras. Because of this covering, the mantras came to be called chandas.

### 1.4.3

तानु तत्र मृत्युर्यथा मत्स्यमुदके परिपश्येदेवं पर्यपश्यदृचि साम्नि यजुषि । ते नु विदित्वोर्ध्वा ऋचः साम्नो यजुषः स्वरमेव प्राविशन् ॥ १.४.३ ॥

*tānu tatra mṛtyuryathā matsyamudake paripaśyedevaṃ paryapaśyadṛci sāmni yajuṣi | te nu viditvordhvā ṛcaḥ sāmno yajuṣaḥ svarameva prāviśan || 1.4.3 ||*

A fish swimming in shallow water can be seen by anyone and is at risk of being caught. In the same way, Death could see the gods and goddesses while they relied on Vedic rituals, and they were within his easy reach. Realizing this, they turned to reciting Om instead.

### 1.4.4

यदा वा ऋचमाप्नोत्योमित्येवातिस्वरत्येवंसामैवं यजुरेष उ स्वरो यदेतदक्षरमेतदमृतमभयं तत्प्रविश्य देवा अमृता अभया अभवन् ॥ १.४.४ ॥

*yadā vā ṛcamāpnotyomityevātisvaratyevaṃsāmaivaṃ yajureṣa u svaro yadetadakṣarametadamṛtamabhayaṃ tatpraviśya devā amṛtā abhayā abhavan || 1.4.4 ||*

People begin reciting the Ṛk with Om, saying it with great enthusiasm, and they do likewise with the Sāma and Yajus. This Om is akṣara and also svara. It is a symbol of immortality and fearlessness. By taking refuge in it, the gods and goddesses gained immortality and fearlessness.

### 1.4.5

स य एतदेवं विद्वानक्षरं प्रणौत्येतदेवाक्षरं स्वरममृतमभयं प्रविशति तत्प्रविश्य यदमृता देवास्तदमृतो भवति ॥ १.४.५ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya etadevaṃ vidvānakṣaraṃ praṇautyetadevākṣaraṃ svaramamṛtamabhayaṃ praviśati tatpraviśya yadamṛtā devāstadamṛto bhavati || 1.4.5 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Even today, anyone who knows this Om and worships it in this way can gain the fearlessness and immortality of Om, which is akṣara, or svara. By becoming one with Om, a person can reach immortality, as the gods and goddesses did.

### 1.5.1

अथ खलु य उद्गीथः स प्रणवो यः प्रणवः स उद्गीथ इत्यसौ वा आदित्य उद्गीथ एष प्रणव ओमिति ह्येष स्वरन्नेति ॥ १.५.१ ॥

*atha khalu ya udgīthaḥ sa praṇavo yaḥ praṇavaḥ sa udgītha ityasau vā āditya udgītha eṣa praṇava omiti hyeṣa svaranneti || 1.5.1 ||*

What is udgītha is also praṇava (Om), and what is praṇava is udgītha. That sun is both udgītha and praṇava, because when it appears it seems to say Om, or to hold the word Om in its mind.

### 1.5.2

एतमु एवाहमभ्यगासिषं तस्मान्मम त्वमेकोऽसीति ह कौषीतकिः पुत्रमुवाच रश्मींस्त्वं पर्यावर्तयाद्बहवो वै ते भविष्यन्तीत्यधिदैवतम् ॥ १.५.२ ॥

*etamu evāhamabhyagāsiṣaṃ tasmānmama tvameko'sīti ha kauṣītakiḥ putramuvāca raśmīṃstvaṃ paryāvartayādbahavo vai te bhaviṣyantītyadhidaivatam || 1.5.2 ||*

The sage Kauṣītaki told his son, 'I worshipped the sun and its rays as one, and so I had only one son, you. If you worship the sun and its rays as separate, you will have many children.' This is the worship of the forces of nature.

### 1.5.3

अथाध्यात्मं य एवायं मुख्यः प्राणस्तमुद्गीथमुपासीतोमिति ह्येष स्वरन्नेति ॥ १.५.३ ॥

*athādhyātmaṃ ya evāyaṃ mukhyaḥ prāṇastamudgīthamupāsītomiti hyeṣa svaranneti || 1.5.3 ||*

Next, worship concerning the physical body is done in this way: the chief prāṇa should be worshipped as udgītha, for it seems to say Om as it sets the organs of perception and action to work.

### 1.5.4

एतमु एवाहमभ्यगासिषं तस्मान्मम त्वमेकोऽसीति ह कौषीतकिः पुत्रमुवाच प्राणांस्त्वं भूमानमभिगायताद्बहवो वै मे भविष्यन्तीति ॥ १.५.४ ॥

*etamu evāhamabhyagāsiṣaṃ tasmānmama tvameko'sīti ha kauṣītakiḥ putramuvāca prāṇāṃstvaṃ bhūmānamabhigāyatādbahavo vai me bhaviṣyantīti || 1.5.4 ||*

The sage Kauṣītaki told his son, 'I worshipped prāṇa as just one entity, and so I had only one son. I advise you to worship prāṇa as one having many qualities and many forms, while thinking, "May I have many children."'

### 1.5.5

अथ खलु य उद्गीथः स प्रणवो यः प्रणवः स उद्गीथ इति होतृषदनाद्धैवापि दुरुद्गीथमनुसमाहरतीत्यनुसमाहरतीति ॥ १.५.५ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*atha khalu ya udgīthaḥ sa praṇavo yaḥ praṇavaḥ sa udgītha iti hotṛṣadanāddhaivāpi durudgīthamanusamāharatītyanusamāharatīti || 1.5.5 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

It is certain that what is udgītha is praṇava, and what is praṇava is also udgītha. If the person performing a sacrifice makes errors of pronunciation, they can be corrected once he knows that udgītha and praṇava are the same.

### 1.6.1

इयमेवर्गग्निः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढ़ं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढंसाम गीयत इयमेव साग्निरमस्तत्साम ॥ १.६.१ ॥

*iyamevargagniḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍha़ṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma gīyata iyameva sāgniramastatsāma || 1.6.1 ||*

This earth is like the Ṛg Veda and fire is like the Sāma Veda. The Sāma rests on the Ṛg Veda, which is why the Sāṃa is sung on the basis of the Ṛg Veda. The earth is sā and fire is ama. Joined, sā and ama make Sāma.

### 1.6.2

अन्तरिक्षमेवर्ग्वायुः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढं साम गीयतेऽन्तरिक्षमेव सा वायुरमस्तत्साम ॥ १.६.२ ॥

*antarikṣamevargvāyuḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma gīyate'ntarikṣameva sā vāyuramastatsāma || 1.6.2 ||*

The space between heaven and earth is the Ṛk, and air is the Sāma. This Sāma, air, rests on the Ṛk, the space between heaven and earth. So the Sāma is sung on the basis of the Ṛk. The space between heaven and earth is sā, and earth is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.6.3

द्यौरेवर्गादित्यः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढं साम गीयते द्यौरेव सादित्योऽमस्तत्साम ॥ १.६.३ ॥

*dyaurevargādityaḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma gīyate dyaureva sādityo'mastatsāma || 1.6.3 ||*

Heaven is the Ṛk, and the sun is the Sāma. This Sāma, the sun, rests on the Ṛk, heaven. So Sāma scholars sing songs based on the Ṛk. Heaven is sā and the sun is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.6.4

नक्षत्रान्येवर्क्चन्द्रमाः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढं साम गीयते नक्षत्राण्येव सा चन्द्रमा अमस्तत्साम ॥ १.६.४ ॥

*nakṣatrānyevarkcandramāḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma gīyate nakṣatrāṇyeva sā candramā amastatsāma || 1.6.4 ||*

The stars are the Ṛk, and the moon is the Sāma. This Sāma, the moon, rests on the Ṛk, the stars. So Sāma scholars sing songs based on the Ṛk. The stars are sā and the moon is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.6.5

अथ यदेतदादित्यस्य शुक्लं भाः सैवर्गथ यन्नीलं परः कृष्णं तत्साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढं साम गीयते ॥ १.६.५ ॥

*atha yadetadādityasya śuklaṃ bhāḥ saivargatha yannīlaṃ paraḥ kṛṣṇaṃ tatsāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma gīyate || 1.6.5 ||*

The sun's white glow is the Ṛk, and its deep blue glow is the Sāma. The black glow, the Sāma, rests on the white glow, the Ṛk. So the Sāma is sung on the basis of the Ṛk.

### 1.6.6

अथ यदेवैतदादित्यस्य शुक्लं भाः सैव साथ यन्नीलं परः कृष्णं तदमस्तत्सामाथ य एषोऽन्तरादित्ये हिरण्मयः पुरुषो दृश्यते हिरण्यश्मश्रुर्हिरण्यकेश आप्रणस्वात्सर्व एव सुवर्णः ॥ १.६.६ ॥

*atha yadevaitadādityasya śuklaṃ bhāḥ saiva sātha yannīlaṃ paraḥ kṛṣṇaṃ tadamastatsāmātha ya eṣo'ntarāditye hiraṇmayaḥ puruṣo dṛśyate hiraṇyaśmaśrurhiraṇyakeśa āpraṇasvātsarva eva suvarṇaḥ || 1.6.6 ||*

Then comes worship of the shining being in the sun. The sun's white glow is sā, and its dark bluish-black glow is ama. Together these make the word Sāma. Within the sun's orb is a deity whom yogīs see. His whole body gleams like gold, down to his toenails, and his beard and hair are bright gold.

### 1.6.7

तस्य यथा कप्यासं पुण्डरीकमेवमक्षिणी तस्योदिति नाम स एष सर्वेभ्यः पाप्मभ्य उदित उदेति ह वै सर्वेभ्यः पाप्मभ्यो य एवं वेद ॥ १.६.७ ॥

*tasya yathā kapyāsaṃ puṇḍarīkamevamakṣiṇī tasyoditi nāma sa eṣa sarvebhyaḥ pāpmabhya udita udeti ha vai sarvebhyaḥ pāpmabhyo ya evaṃ veda || 1.6.7 ||*

His eyes are like lotuses opened by the sun. He is called ut because he stands above all weakness. Whoever knows this truth also stands above all weakness.

### 1.6.8

तस्यर्क्च साम च गेष्णौ तस्मादुद्गीथस्तस्मात्त्वेवोद्गातैतस्य हि गाता स एष ये चामुष्मात्पराञ्चो लोकास्तेषां चेष्टे देवकामानां चेत्यधिदैवतम् ॥ १.६.८ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*tasyarkca sāma ca geṣṇau tasmādudgīthastasmāttvevodgātaitasya hi gātā sa eṣa ye cāmuṣmātparāñco lokāsteṣāṃ ceṣṭe devakāmānāṃ cetyadhidaivatam || 1.6.8 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

The Ṛk and the Sāma are the two singers who praise this god. That is why he is called udgītha, and why one who sings the udgītha is called an udgātā. There are worlds above the solar region, but the god of the solar region rules and supports them. He also determines the wishes of the gods and goddesses. This is from the standpoint of the gods and goddesses.

### 1.7.1

अथाध्यात्मं वागेवर्क्प्राणः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढं साम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढंसाम गीयते। वागेव सा प्राणोऽमस्तत्साम ॥ १.७.१ ॥

*athādhyātmaṃ vāgevarkprāṇaḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃ sāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma gīyate| vāgeva sā prāṇo'mastatsāma || 1.7.1 ||*

Now an explanation in terms of the body. Speech is Ṛk and prāṇa is Sāma. This Sāma, prāṇa, rests on the Ṛk, speech. So Sāma scholars sing songs based on the Ṛk. Speech is sā and prāṇa is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.7.2

चक्षुरेवर्गात्मा साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढंसाम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढंसाम गीयते । चक्षुरेव सात्मामस्तत्साम ॥ १.७.२ ॥

*cakṣurevargātmā sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma gīyate | cakṣureva sātmāmastatsāma || 1.7.2 ||*

The eyes are like the Ṛg Veda, and the self, the form seen in the eyes, is like the Sāma, which rests on the Ṛk. So the Sāma is sung on the basis of the Ṛk. The eyes are sā and the self, the form in the eyes, is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.7.3

श्रोत्रमेवर्ङ्मनः साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढंसाम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढंसाम गीयते । श्रोत्रमेव सा मनोऽमस्तत्साम ॥ १.७.३ ॥

*śrotramevarṅmanaḥ sāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma gīyate | śrotrameva sā mano'mastatsāma || 1.7.3 ||*

The organ of hearing is the Ṛk and the mind is Sāma. This Sāma, the mind, rests on the Ṛk, the organ of hearing. So Sāma scholars sing songs based on the Ṛk. The organ of hearing is sā and the mind is ama. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.7.4

अथ यदेतदक्ष्णः शुक्लं भाः सैवर्गथ यन्नीलं परः कृष्णं तत्साम तदेतदेतस्यामृच्यध्यूढंसाम तस्मादृच्यध्यूढंसाम गीयते । अथ यदेवैतदक्ष्णः शुक्लं भाः सैव साथ यन्नीलं परः कृष्णं तदमस्तत्साम ॥ १.७.४ ॥

*atha yadetadakṣṇaḥ śuklaṃ bhāḥ saivargatha yannīlaṃ paraḥ kṛṣṇaṃ tatsāma tadetadetasyāmṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma tasmādṛcyadhyūḍhaṃsāma gīyate | atha yadevaitadakṣṇaḥ śuklaṃ bhāḥ saiva sātha yannīlaṃ paraḥ kṛṣṇaṃ tadamastatsāma || 1.7.4 ||*

There is also a white glow in the eyes, which is likened to the Ṛg Veda, and a similar glow of deep, dark blue, which is likened to the Sāma Veda. The dark glow rests on the white glow, which is why the Sāma is said to rest on the Ṛk. The sā of Sāma stands for the white glow in the eye, and ama for the deep blue glow. Joined, they are Sāma.

### 1.7.5

अथ य एषोऽन्तरक्षिणि पुरुषो दृश्यते सैवर्क्तत्साम तदुक्थं तद्यजुस्तद्ब्रह्म तस्यैतस्य तदेव रूपं यदमुष्य रूपं यावमुष्य गेष्णौ तौ गेष्णौ यन्नाम तन्नाम ॥ १.७.५ ॥

*atha ya eṣo'ntarakṣiṇi puruṣo dṛśyate saivarktatsāma tadukthaṃ tadyajustadbrahma tasyaitasya tadeva rūpaṃ yadamuṣya rūpaṃ yāvamuṣya geṣṇau tau geṣṇau yannāma tannāma || 1.7.5 ||*

The person seen in the eye is the Ṛk, the Sāma, the uktha (a part of the Sāma), and the Yajus. He is also the three Vedas. The person in the sun and the person in the eye are one and the same. The same two singers, the Ṛk and the Sāma, praise each of them, and they bear the same names.

### 1.7.6

स एष ये चैतस्मादर्वाञ्चो लोकास्तेषां चेष्टे मनुष्यकामानां चेति तद्य इमे वीणायां गायन्त्येतं ते गायन्ति तस्मात्ते धनसनयः ॥ १.७.६ ॥

*sa eṣa ye caitasmādarvāñco lokāsteṣāṃ ceṣṭe manuṣyakāmānāṃ ceti tadya ime vīṇāyāṃ gāyantyetaṃ te gāyanti tasmātte dhanasanayaḥ || 1.7.6 ||*

The person in the eye governs the world beneath him as well as the desires of the human mind. People who sing to the vīṇā are really singing to honor him, and that is why such musicians grow wealthy.

### 1.7.7

अथ य एतदेवं विद्वान्साम गायत्युभौ स गायति सोऽमुनैव स एष चामुष्मात्पराञ्चो लोकास्तांश्चाप्नोति देवकामांश्च ॥ १.७.७ ॥

*atha ya etadevaṃ vidvānsāma gāyatyubhau sa gāyati so'munaiva sa eṣa cāmuṣmātparāñco lokāstāṃścāpnoti devakāmāṃśca || 1.7.7 ||*

[Here is what a worshipper gains:] Someone who knows the Truth described above [the Truth about Āditya, the sun] sings the Sāma to honor both Āditya and the person in the eye. He becomes one with Āditya. He also gains mastery over the planes above Āditya and obtains all that the gods and goddesses wish for. [In other words, such a person becomes divine.]

### 1.7.8

अथानेनैव ये चैतस्मादर्वाञ्चो लोकास्तांश्चाप्नोति मनुष्यकामांश्च तस्मादु हैवंविदुद्गाता ब्रूयात् ॥ १.७.८ ॥

*athānenaiva ye caitasmādarvāñco lokāstāṃścāpnoti manuṣyakāmāṃśca tasmādu haivaṃvidudgātā brūyāt || 1.7.8 ||*

[That same worshipper], by the grace of the Lord in the eye, also gains all the worlds beneath that Lord, along with everything human beings can desire. That is why the singer will ask:

### 1.7.9

कं ते काममागायानीत्येष ह्येव कामागानस्येष्टे य एवं विद्वान्साम गायति साम गायति ॥ १.७.९ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*kaṃ te kāmamāgāyānītyeṣa hyeva kāmāgānasyeṣṭe ya evaṃ vidvānsāma gāyati sāma gāyati || 1.7.9 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

[A learned udgātā singing the Sāma will ask the person he is singing for:] "What shall I request for you through my songs?" He asks [because he knows that the Lord in the eye presides over the Sāma and can grant whatever the person wants], and he sings the Sāma, he sings the Sāma.

### 1.8.1

त्रयो होद्गीथे कुशला बभूवुः शिलकः शालावत्यश्चैकितायनो दाल्भ्यः प्रवाहणो जैवलिरिति ते होचुरुद्गीथे वै कुशलाः स्मो हन्तोद्गीथे कथां वदाम इति ॥ १.८.१ ॥

*trayo hodgīthe kuśalā babhūvuḥ śilakaḥ śālāvatyaścaikitāyano dālbhyaḥ pravāhaṇo jaivaliriti te hocurudgīthe vai kuśalāḥ smo hantodgīthe kathāṃ vadāma iti || 1.8.1 ||*

Long ago there were three men skilled in the art of the udgītha: Śilaka, son of Salāvat; Caikitāyana, son of Cikitāyana of the Dalbha clan; and Pravāhaṇa, son of Jīvala. They said, "We are masters of the art of the udgītha. If you like, we can have a discussion about it."

### 1.8.2

तथेति ह समुपविविशुः स ह प्रावहणो जैवलिरुवाच भगवन्तावग्रे वदतां ब्राह्मणयोर्वदतोर्वाचं श्रोष्यामीति ॥ १.८.२ ॥

*tatheti ha samupaviviśuḥ sa ha prāvahaṇo jaivaliruvāca bhagavantāvagre vadatāṃ brāhmaṇayorvadatorvācaṃ śroṣyāmīti || 1.8.2 ||*

They agreed and sat down. Pravāhaṇa, son of Jīvala, said, "Please, you two start the debate. I would like to hear the two of you brāhmins argue it out."

### 1.8.3

स ह शिलकः शालावत्यश्चैकितायनं दाल्भ्यमुवाच हन्त त्वा पृच्छानीति पृच्छेति होवाच ॥ १.८.३ ॥

*sa ha śilakaḥ śālāvatyaścaikitāyanaṃ dālbhyamuvāca hanta tvā pṛcchānīti pṛccheti hovāca || 1.8.3 ||*

Śilaka, son of Śalāvat, said to the sage Caikitāyana Dālbhya, "With your permission, I would like to put a question to you." Dālbhya answered, "Go ahead."

### 1.8.4

का साम्नो गतिरिति स्वर इति होवाच स्वरस्य का गतिरिति प्राण इति होवाच प्राणस्य का गतिरित्यन्नमिति होवाचान्नस्य का गतिरित्याप इति होवाच ॥ १.८.४ ॥

*kā sāmno gatiriti svara iti hovāca svarasya kā gatiriti prāṇa iti hovāca prāṇasya kā gatirityannamiti hovācānnasya kā gatirityāpa iti hovāca || 1.8.4 ||*

Śilaka asked, "What does the Sāma rest on?" "The voice," Dālbhya answered. "What does the voice rest on?" "The vital breath," Dālbhya said. "And the vital breath?" Śilaka asked. "Food," Dālbhya replied. Śilaka then asked, "What does food rest on?" Dālbhya said, "Water."

### 1.8.5

अपां का गतिरित्यसौ लोक इति होवाचामुष्य लोकस्य का गतिरिति न स्वर्गं लोकमिति नयेदिति होवाच स्वर्गं वयं लोकं सामाभिसंस्थापयामः स्वर्गसंस्तावंहि सामेति ॥ १.८.५ ॥

*apāṃ kā gatirityasau loka iti hovācāmuṣya lokasya kā gatiriti na svargaṃ lokamiti nayediti hovāca svargaṃ vayaṃ lokaṃ sāmābhisaṃsthāpayāmaḥ svargasaṃstāvaṃhi sāmeti || 1.8.5 ||*

[Śilaka:] "What does water rest on?" [Dālbhya:] "That world, heaven." [Śilaka:] "And what does that world rest on?" [Dālbhya:] "Do not go past heaven. We know the Sāma to be in heaven, and for that reason the Sāma is honored as heaven."

### 1.8.6

तं ह शिलकः शालावत्यश्चैकितायनं दाल्भ्यमुवाचाप्रतिष्ठितं वै किल ते दाल्भ्य साम यस्त्वेतर्हि ब्रूयान्मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति मूर्धा ते विपतेदिति ॥ १.८.६ ॥

*taṃ ha śilakaḥ śālāvatyaścaikitāyanaṃ dālbhyamuvācāpratiṣṭhitaṃ vai kila te dālbhya sāma yastvetarhi brūyānmūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti mūrdhā te vipatediti || 1.8.6 ||*

Śilaka Śālāvatya said to Caikitāyana Dālbhya, "Dālbhya, your Sāma has no foundation, then. If someone who knows the Sāma were to speak up now [declaring you wrong and cursing you with the words] 'Your head will fall [if what you say proves false],' your head really would fall."

### 1.8.7

हन्ताहमेतद्भगवतो वेदानीति विद्धीति होवाचामुष्य लोकस्य का गतिरित्ययं लोक इति होवाचास्य लोकस्य का गतिरिति न प्रतिष्ठां लोकमिति नयेदिति होवाच प्रतिष्ठां वयं लोकं सामाभिसंस्थापयामः प्रतिष्ठासंस्तावं हि सामेति ॥ १.८.७ ॥

*hantāhametadbhagavato vedānīti viddhīti hovācāmuṣya lokasya kā gatirityayaṃ loka iti hovācāsya lokasya kā gatiriti na pratiṣṭhāṃ lokamiti nayediti hovāca pratiṣṭhāṃ vayaṃ lokaṃ sāmābhisaṃsthāpayāmaḥ pratiṣṭhāsaṃstāvaṃ hi sāmeti || 1.8.7 ||*

Dālbhya said, "Sir, with your permission, I would like to ask you about this." Śilaka answered, "Go ahead." Dālbhya asked, "What does that heaven rest on?" "This earth," said Śilaka. "What holds up this earth?" Dālbhya asked. Śilaka replied, "Do not suppose the Sāma's foundation lies beyond this earth. We hold that the Sāma is founded on this earth, and we honor it accordingly."

### 1.8.8

तं ह प्रवाहणो जैवलिरुवाचान्तवद्वै किल ते शालावत्य साम यस्त्वेतर्हि ब्रूयान्मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति मूर्धा ते विपतेदिति हन्ताहमेतद्भगवतो वेदानीति विद्धीति होवाच ॥ १.८.८ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*taṃ ha pravāhaṇo jaivaliruvācāntavadvai kila te śālāvatya sāma yastvetarhi brūyānmūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti mūrdhā te vipatediti hantāhametadbhagavato vedānīti viddhīti hovāca || 1.8.8 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Pravāhaṇa Jaivali said, "Śālāvatya, your Sāma has an end. If someone were to say now that your head will fall off, it would fall off." Śilaka Śālāvatya then said, "O Lord, I want you to teach me [the truth about the Sāma]." Jaivali answered, "Yes, learn it from me."

### 1.9.1

अस्य लोकस्य का गतिरित्याकाश इति होवाच सर्वाणि ह वा इमानि भूतान्याकाशादेव समुत्पद्यन्त आकाशं प्रत्यस्तं यन्त्याकाशो ह्येवैभ्यो ज्यायानकाशः परायणम् ॥ १.९.१ ॥

*asya lokasya kā gatirityākāśa iti hovāca sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtānyākāśādeva samutpadyanta ākāśaṃ pratyastaṃ yantyākāśo hyevaibhyo jyāyānakāśaḥ parāyaṇam || 1.9.1 ||*

Śilaka Śālāvatya asked Pravāhaṇa, "Where does this earth end?" Pravāhaṇa said, "In space. Everything that exists comes out of space and returns into space. Space is greater than everything. Space is the highest goal."

### 1.9.2

स एष परोवरीयानुद्गीथः स एषोऽनन्तः परोवरीयो हास्य भवति परोवरीयसो ह लोकाञ्जयति य एतदेवं विद्वान्परोवरीयांसमुद्गीथमुपास्ते ॥ १.९.२ ॥

*sa eṣa parovarīyānudgīthaḥ sa eṣo'nantaḥ parovarīyo hāsya bhavati parovarīyaso ha lokāñjayati ya etadevaṃ vidvānparovarīyāṃsamudgīthamupāste || 1.9.2 ||*

It was said earlier that the udgītha is the best and is also endless. Someone who understands this and worships the udgītha in that light goes on reaching higher and higher worlds and keeps becoming a better person.

### 1.9.3

तं हैतमतिधन्वा शौनक उदरशाण्डिल्यायोक्त्वोवाच यावत्त एनं प्रजायामुद्गीथं वेदिष्यन्ते परोवरीयो हैभ्यस्तावदस्मिँल्लोके जीवनं भविष्यति ॥ १.९.३ ॥

*taṃ haitamatidhanvā śaunaka udaraśāṇḍilyāyoktvovāca yāvatta enaṃ prajāyāmudgīthaṃ vediṣyante parovarīyo haibhyastāvadasmim̐lloke jīvanaṃ bhaviṣyati || 1.9.3 ||*

[In ancient times there lived a sage named Atidhanvā, son of Śunaka, who knew the science of the udgītha well.] Once, teaching it to his disciple Udaraśāṇḍilya, he declared, "As long as you and your family keep this knowledge alive, life in your family's world will be of a higher quality than that of ordinary people."

### 1.9.4

तथामुष्मिँल्लोके लोक इति स य एतमेवं विद्वानुपास्ते परोवरीय एव हास्यास्मिँल्लोके जीवनं भवति तथामुष्मिँल्लोके लोक इति लोके लोक इति ॥ १.९.४ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*tathāmuṣmim̐lloke loka iti sa ya etamevaṃ vidvānupāste parovarīya eva hāsyāsmim̐lloke jīvanaṃ bhavati tathāmuṣmim̐lloke loka iti loke loka iti || 1.9.4 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

What holds in this world holds in the other as well. Someone who knows the place of the udgītha and worships it accordingly enjoys the best of life here, and [after death] enjoys the best of life in the other world too.

### 1.10.1

मटचीहतेषु कुरुष्वाटिक्या सह जाययोषस्तिर्ह चाक्रायण इभ्यग्रामे प्रद्राणक उवास ॥ १.१०.१ ॥

*maṭacīhateṣu kuruṣvāṭikyā saha jāyayoṣastirha cākrāyaṇa ibhyagrāme pradrāṇaka uvāsa || 1.10.1 ||*

Once a severe thunderstorm struck the land of the Kurus, and a young man who lived there, Uṣasti, son of Cakra, fell into great hardship. Together with his child-wife he left home and settled in a prosperous village.

### 1.10.2

स हेभ्यं कुल्माषान्खादन्तं बिभिक्षे तं होवाच । नेतोऽन्ये विद्यन्ते यच्च ये म इम उपनिहिता इति ॥ १.१०.२ ॥

*sa hebhyaṃ kulmāṣānkhādantaṃ bibhikṣe taṃ hovāca | neto'nye vidyante yacca ye ma ima upanihitā iti || 1.10.2 ||*

Uṣasti came upon an elephant-driver eating some poor-quality pulses and begged him for a share. The driver answered, "What is in my bowl is all I have to eat. I have nothing else. [What should I do?]"

### 1.10.3

एतेषां मे देहीति होवाच तानस्मै प्रददौ हन्तानुपानमित्युच्छिष्टं वै मे पीतंस्यादिति होवाच ॥ १.१०.३ ॥

*eteṣāṃ me dehīti hovāca tānasmai pradadau hantānupānamityucchiṣṭaṃ vai me pītaṃsyāditi hovāca || 1.10.3 ||*

Uṣasti said to him, "Please give me some [of the pulses]." The driver handed them over and said, "Here is some water." [Uṣasti refused it, saying,] "That would mean drinking unclean water."

### 1.10.4

न स्विदेतेऽप्युच्छिष्टा इति न वा अजीविष्यमिमानखादन्निति होवाच कामो म उदपानमिति ॥ १.१०.४ ॥

*na svidete'pyucchiṣṭā iti na vā ajīviṣyamimānakhādanniti hovāca kāmo ma udapānamiti || 1.10.4 ||*

The driver asked, "Aren't the pulses unclean as well?" Uṣasti answered, "Without these grains to eat, I would die. Drinking water [is not as important]. I can get that whenever I like."

### 1.10.5

स ह खादित्वातिशेषाञ्जायाया आजहार साग्र एव सुभिक्षा बभूव तान्प्रतिगृह्य निदधौ ॥ १.१०.५ ॥

*sa ha khāditvātiśeṣāñjāyāyā ājahāra sāgra eva subhikṣā babhūva tānpratigṛhya nidadhau || 1.10.5 ||*

After eating part of the food, Uṣasti carried what remained back to his wife. She, however, had in the meantime received good alms. She took the food [from her husband] and set it aside [to use later].

### 1.10.6

स ह प्रातः संजिहान उवाच यद्बतान्नस्य लभेमहि लभेमहि धनमात्रांराजासौ यक्ष्यते स मा सर्वैरार्त्विज्यैर्वृणीतेति ॥ १.१०.६ ॥

*sa ha prātaḥ saṃjihāna uvāca yadbatānnasya labhemahi labhemahi dhanamātrāṃrājāsau yakṣyate sa mā sarvairārtvijyairvṛṇīteti || 1.10.6 ||*

Getting out of bed the next morning, Uṣasti said to his wife, "Oh, if only I could get something to eat, I could earn some money. The king over there is about to hold a sacrifice, and he would quite likely have given me all the work of a ṛtvik [a priest at a sacrifice]."

### 1.10.7

तं जायोवाच हन्त पत इम एव कुल्माषा इति तान्खादित्वामुं यज्ञं विततमेयाय ॥ १.१०.७ ॥

*taṃ jāyovāca hanta pata ima eva kulmāṣā iti tānkhāditvāmuṃ yajñaṃ vitatameyāya || 1.10.7 ||*

His wife said to him, "Dear husband, here are the pulses you gave me." After eating them, Uṣasti set off for the place where the sacrifice was going on [the other priests having already begun it].

### 1.10.8

तत्रोद्गातॄनास्तावे स्तोष्यमाणानुपोपविवेश स ह प्रस्तोतारमुवाच ॥ १.१०.८ ॥

*tatrodgātṝnāstāve stoṣyamāṇānupopaviveśa sa ha prastotāramuvāca || 1.10.8 ||*

At the sacrifice he found those [the udgātṛs] singing the [Sāma] hymns and sat down among them. Turning to the one singing the prastāva, he said:

### 1.10.9

प्रस्तोतर्या देवता प्रस्तावमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रस्तोष्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति ॥ १.१०.९ ॥

*prastotaryā devatā prastāvamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānprastoṣyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti || 1.10.9 ||*

"Prastotā, if you recite the prastāva knowing nothing about the god this hymn concerns, your head will fall."

### 1.10.10

एवमेवोद्गातारमुवाचोद्गातर्या देवतोद्गीथमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वानुद्गास्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति ॥ १.१०.१० ॥

*evamevodgātāramuvācodgātaryā devatodgīthamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānudgāsyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti || 1.10.10 ||*

Uṣasti then said the same to the one singing the udgītha: "Udgātā, if you sing the udgītha knowing nothing about the god connected with it, your head will fall."

### 1.10.11

एवमेव प्रतिहर्तारमुवाच प्रतिहर्तर्या देवता प्रतिहारमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रतिहरिष्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति ते ह समारतास्तूष्णीमासांचक्रिरे ॥ १.१०.११ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*evameva pratihartāramuvāca pratihartaryā devatā pratihāramanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānpratihariṣyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti te ha samāratāstūṣṇīmāsāṃcakrire || 1.10.11 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

And he said the same again to the one singing the pratihāra: "Pratihartā, if you sing the pratihāra knowing nothing about its deity, your head will fall." At this they all broke off their hymns and fell silent.

### 1.11.1

अथ हैनं यजमान उवाच भगवन्तं वा अहं विविदिषाणीत्युषस्तिरस्मि चाक्रायण इति होवाच ॥ १.११.१ ॥

*atha hainaṃ yajamāna uvāca bhagavantaṃ vā ahaṃ vividiṣāṇītyuṣastirasmi cākrāyaṇa iti hovāca || 1.11.1 ||*

The prince conducting the sacrifice then said, "Sir, I would like to know who you are." He [Uṣasti] answered, "I am Uṣasti, son of Cakra."

### 1.11.2

स होवाच भगवन्तं वा अहमेभिः सर्वैरार्त्विज्यैः पर्यैषिषं भगवतो वा अहमवित्त्यान्यानवृषि ॥ १.११.२ ॥

*sa hovāca bhagavantaṃ vā ahamebhiḥ sarvairārtvijyaiḥ paryaiṣiṣaṃ bhagavato vā ahamavittyānyānavṛṣi || 1.11.2 ||*

He [the prince] said, "Revered sir, I searched for you so that I could give you all the priestly work. When I could not find you, I gave it to other [brāhmins]."

### 1.11.3

भगवांस्त्वेव मे सर्वैरार्त्विज्यैरिति तथेत्यथ तर्ह्येत एव समतिसृष्टाः स्तुवतां यावत्त्वेभ्यो धनं दद्यास्तावन्मम दद्या इति तथेति ह यजमान उवाच ॥ १.११.३ ॥

*bhagavāṃstveva me sarvairārtvijyairiti tathetyatha tarhyeta eva samatisṛṣṭāḥ stuvatāṃ yāvattvebhyo dhanaṃ dadyāstāvanmama dadyā iti tatheti ha yajamāna uvāca || 1.11.3 ||*

[The prince said,] "O Lord, please take on all the priestly work for me." [Uṣasti answered:] "Very well. Let the priests you have already hired recite as I direct. But you must pay me as much as you promised to pay them." "So it shall be," said [the prince].

### 1.11.4

अथ हैनं प्रस्तोतोपससाद प्रस्तोतर्या देवता प्रस्तावमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रस्तोष्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति मा भगवानवोचत्कतमा सा देवतेति ॥ १.११.४ ॥

*atha hainaṃ prastotopasasāda prastotaryā devatā prastāvamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānprastoṣyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti mā bhagavānavocatkatamā sā devateti || 1.11.4 ||*

The Prastotā then came to [Uṣasti and said:] "Revered sir, you told me, 'Prastotā, if you sing the prastāva knowing nothing about the deity the hymn is addressed to, your head will fall.' Please tell me who that deity is."

### 1.11.5

प्राण इति होवाच सर्वाणि ह वा इमानि भूतानि प्राणमेवाभिसंविशन्ति प्राणमभ्युज्जिहते सैषा देवता प्रस्तावमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रास्तोष्यो मूर्धा ते व्यपतिष्यत्तथोक्तस्य मयेति ॥ १.११.५ ॥

*prāṇa iti hovāca sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtāni prāṇamevābhisaṃviśanti prāṇamabhyujjihate saiṣā devatā prastāvamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānprāstoṣyo mūrdhā te vyapatiṣyattathoktasya mayeti || 1.11.5 ||*

Uṣasti said, "It is prāṇa [the vital force]. All the things we see around us [moving or unmoving] vanish into prāṇa [when they are destroyed, and when they appear] they appear out of prāṇa. Prāṇa is the deity the prastāva is addressed to. Had you sung the hymn without knowing its deity, after my warning, your head would certainly have fallen."

### 1.11.6

अथ हैनमुद्गातोपससादोद्गातर्या देवतोद्गीथमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वानुद्गास्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति मा भगवानवोचत्कतमा सा देवतेति ॥ १.११.६ ॥

*atha hainamudgātopasasādodgātaryā devatodgīthamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānudgāsyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti mā bhagavānavocatkatamā sā devateti || 1.11.6 ||*

The udgātā next came to [Uṣasti and said:] "Revered sir, you told me, 'Udgātā, if you sing the udgītha knowing nothing about the deity the hymn is addressed to, your head will fall.' Please tell me who that deity is."

### 1.11.7

आदित्य इति होवाच सर्वाणि ह वा इमानि भूतान्यादित्यमुच्चैः सन्तं गायन्ति सैषा देवतोद्गीथमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वानुदगास्यो मूर्धा ते व्यपतिष्यत्तथोक्तस्य मयेति ॥ १.११.७ ॥

*āditya iti hovāca sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtānyādityamuccaiḥ santaṃ gāyanti saiṣā devatodgīthamanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānudagāsyo mūrdhā te vyapatiṣyattathoktasya mayeti || 1.11.7 ||*

Uṣasti said, "It is āditya [the sun], since all these beings pay homage to the sun high above them. Āditya is the deity the udgītha is addressed to. Had you sung the udgītha without knowing its deity, your head would certainly have fallen, as I told you."

### 1.11.8

अथ हैनं प्रतिहर्तोपससाद प्रतिहर्तर्या देवता प्रतिहारमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रतिहरिष्यसि मूर्धा ते विपतिष्यतीति मा भगवानवोचत्कतमा सा देवतेति ॥ १.११.८ ॥

*atha hainaṃ pratihartopasasāda pratihartaryā devatā pratihāramanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānpratihariṣyasi mūrdhā te vipatiṣyatīti mā bhagavānavocatkatamā sā devateti || 1.11.8 ||*

After that the pratihartā came to [Uṣasti and said:] "Revered sir, you told me, 'Pratihartā, if you sing the pratihāra knowing nothing about the deity the hymn is addressed to, your head will fall.' Please tell me who that deity is."

### 1.11.9

अन्नमिति होवाच सर्वाणि ह वा इमानि भूतन्यन्नमेव प्रतिहरमाणानि जीवन्ति सैषा देवता प्रतिहारमन्वायत्ता तां चेदविद्वान्प्रत्यहरिष्यो मूर्धा ते व्यपतिष्यत्तथोक्तस्य मयेति तथोक्तस्य मयेति ॥ १.११.९ ॥ ॥ इति एकादशः खण्डः ॥

*annamiti hovāca sarvāṇi ha vā imāni bhūtanyannameva pratiharamāṇāni jīvanti saiṣā devatā pratihāramanvāyattā tāṃ cedavidvānpratyahariṣyo mūrdhā te vyapatiṣyattathoktasya mayeti tathoktasya mayeti || 1.11.9 || || iti ekādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Uṣasti said, "It is anna [food], since all these beings keep themselves alive by eating food. Anna is the deity the pratihāra is addressed to. Had you sung the pratihāra without knowing its deity, your head would certainly have fallen, as I told you."

### 1.12.1

अथातः शौव उद्गीथस्तद्ध बको दाल्भ्यो ग्लावो वा मैत्रेयः स्वाध्यायमुद्वव्राज ॥ १.१२.१ ॥

*athātaḥ śauva udgīthastaddha bako dālbhyo glāvo vā maitreyaḥ svādhyāyamudvavrāja || 1.12.1 ||*

Next comes an udgītha [to food] sung by dogs. Here is the story: Baka Dālbhya, also called Glāva Maitreya, went one day to a quiet spot to study the scriptures [the udgītha].

### 1.12.2

तस्मै श्वा श्वेतः प्रादुर्बभूव तमन्ये श्वान उपसमेत्योचुरन्नं नो भगवानागायत्वशनायामवा इति ॥ १.१२.२ ॥

*tasmai śvā śvetaḥ prādurbabhūva tamanye śvāna upasametyocurannaṃ no bhagavānāgāyatvaśanāyāmavā iti || 1.12.2 ||*

A white dog appeared in front of him, as though it wished to do the sage a kindness. Several smaller dogs then came up to the white dog and said, "O Lord, please sing for us. We are hungry and want some food."

### 1.12.3

तान्होवाचेहैव मा प्रातरुपसमीयातेति तद्ध बको दाल्भ्यो ग्लावो वा मैत्रेयः प्रतिपालयांचकार ॥ १.१२.३ ॥

*tānhovācehaiva mā prātarupasamīyāteti taddha bako dālbhyo glāvo vā maitreyaḥ pratipālayāṃcakāra || 1.12.3 ||*

[The white dog] answered, "All of you come and meet me here tomorrow morning." Baka Dālbhya, also called Glāva Maitreya, waited there as well [for the white dog].

### 1.12.4

ते ह यथैवेदं बहिष्पवमानेन स्तोष्यमाणाः संरब्धाः सर्पन्तीत्येवमाससृपुस्ते ह समुपविश्य हिं चक्रुः ॥ १.१२.४ ॥

*te ha yathaivedaṃ bahiṣpavamānena stoṣyamāṇāḥ saṃrabdhāḥ sarpantītyevamāsasṛpuste ha samupaviśya hiṃ cakruḥ || 1.12.4 ||*

In the way that those reciting the hymn called bahiṣpavamāna move forward touching one another, the dogs did likewise. Then they sat down and uttered him.

### 1.12.5

ओ३मदा३मों३पिबा३मों३ देवो वरुणः प्रजपतिः सविता२न्नमिहा२हरदन्नपते३ऽन्नमिहा २हरा२हरो३मिति ॥ १.१२.५ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*o3madā3moṃ3pibā3moṃ3 devo varuṇaḥ prajapatiḥ savitā2nnamihā2haradannapate3'nnamihā 2harā2haro3miti || 1.12.5 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

"Om, we will eat. Om, we will drink. Om, Deva [the sun], Varuṇa, Prajāpati, Savitā, bring food to us here. O Lord of food [the sun], bring food to us here. Bring food to us here. Om."

### 1.13.1

अयं वाव लोको हाउकारः वायुर्हाइकारश्चन्द्रमा अथकारः । आत्मेहकारोऽग्निरीकारः ॥ १.१३.१ ॥

*ayaṃ vāva loko hāukāraḥ vāyurhāikāraścandramā athakāraḥ | ātmehakāro'gnirīkāraḥ || 1.13.1 ||*

The syllable hāu stands for this planet, the earth; the syllable hāi stands for air; the moon

### 1.13.2

आदित्य ऊकारो निहव एकारो विश्वे देवा औहोयिकारः प्रजपतिर्हिंकारः प्राणः स्वरोऽन्नं या वाग्विराट् ॥ १.१३.२ ॥

*āditya ūkāro nihava ekāro viśve devā auhoyikāraḥ prajapatirhiṃkāraḥ prāṇaḥ svaro'nnaṃ yā vāgvirāṭ || 1.13.2 ||*

The stobha ū stands for Āditya, the sun; the stobha e for nihava, the welcoming hymn; the stobha auhoyi for the Viśvadeva gods; the stobha hiṃ for Prajāpati; the stobha svara for prāṇa; the stobha yā for food; and the stobha vāk for Virāṭ.

### 1.13.3

अनिरुक्तस्त्रयोदशः स्तोभः संचरो हुंकारः ॥ १.१३.३ ॥

*aniruktastrayodaśaḥ stobhaḥ saṃcaro huṃkāraḥ || 1.13.3 ||*

The thirteenth stobha, huṃ, has no clear definition, and different scholars have explained it differently. [So people are free to meditate on it however they like.]

### 1.13.4

दुग्धेऽस्मै वाग्दोहं यो वाचो दोहोऽन्नवानन्नादो भवति य एतामेवंसाम्नामुपनिषदं वेदोपनिषदं वेदेति ॥ १.१३.४ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमोऽध्यायः ॥

*dugdhe'smai vāgdohaṃ yo vāco doho'nnavānannādo bhavati ya etāmevaṃsāmnāmupaniṣadaṃ vedopaniṣadaṃ vedeti || 1.13.4 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti prathamo'dhyāyaḥ ||*

The organ of speech gives its very best to someone who knows the Sāma with its stobhas as described above. Such a person has abundant food and is also able to eat a great deal.

### 2.1.1

॥ द्वितीयोऽध्यायः ॥ समस्तस्य खलु साम्न उपासनं साधु यत्खलु साधु तत्सामेत्याचक्षते यदसाधु तदसामेति ॥ २.१.१ ॥

*|| dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ || samastasya khalu sāmna upāsanaṃ sādhu yatkhalu sādhu tatsāmetyācakṣate yadasādhu tadasāmeti || 2.1.1 ||*

It is good to worship the Sāma with all of its parts. Scholars call everything good sāma, and likewise everything bad asāma.

### 2.1.2

तदुताप्याहुः साम्नैनमुपागादिति साधुनैनमुपागादित्येव तदाहुरसाम्नैनमुपागादित्यसाधुनैनमुपगादित्येव तदाहुः ॥ २.१.२ ॥

*tadutāpyāhuḥ sāmnainamupāgāditi sādhunainamupāgādityeva tadāhurasāmnainamupāgādityasādhunainamupagādityeva tadāhuḥ || 2.1.2 ||*

So when people say, "He gained access to that distinguished person by virtue of sāma," they mean he reached that person by honest and proper means. And when they say, "He went to that distinguished person by virtue of asāma," they mean he got there by unethical means.

### 2.1.3

अथोताप्याहुः साम नो बतेति यत्साधु भवति साधु बतेत्येव तदाहुरसाम नो बतेति यदसाधु भवत्यसाधु बतेत्येव तदाहुः ॥ २.१.३ ॥

*athotāpyāhuḥ sāma no bateti yatsādhu bhavati sādhu batetyeva tadāhurasāma no bateti yadasādhu bhavatyasādhu batetyeva tadāhuḥ || 2.1.3 ||*

Again, when something good happens, people say, "It is sāma for us," meaning it is good for them. And when something bad happens, they say, "It is asāma for us," meaning it is bad for them.

### 2.1.4

स य एतदेवं विद्वानसाधु सामेत्युपास्तेऽभ्याशो ह यदेनं साधवो धर्मा आ च गच्छेयुरुप च नमेयुः ॥ २.१.४ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya etadevaṃ vidvānasādhu sāmetyupāste'bhyāśo ha yadenaṃ sādhavo dharmā ā ca gaccheyurupa ca nameyuḥ || 2.1.4 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

When a person knows the Sāma in this way and worships it mindful of the great qualities it has, those qualities quickly appear in him and become a source of happiness.

### 2.2.1

लोकेषु पञ्चविधं सामोपासीत पृथिवी हिंकारः । अग्निः प्रस्तावोऽन्तरिक्षमुद्गीथ आदित्यः प्रतिहारो द्यौर्निधनमित्यूर्ध्वेषु ॥ २.२.१ ॥

*lokeṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāsīta pṛthivī hiṃkāraḥ | agniḥ prastāvo'ntarikṣamudgītha ādityaḥ pratihāro dyaurnidhanamityūrdhveṣu || 2.2.1 ||*

One should worship the Sāma in five ways, taking its different parts as symbols of the worlds.

### 2.2.2

अथावृत्तेषु द्यौर्हिंकार आदित्यः प्रस्तावोऽन्तरिक्षमुद्गीथोऽग्निः प्रतिहारः पृथिवी निधनम् ॥ २.२.२ ॥

*athāvṛtteṣu dyaurhiṃkāra ādityaḥ prastāvo'ntarikṣamudgītho'gniḥ pratihāraḥ pṛthivī nidhanam || 2.2.2 ||*

Here is the fivefold worship from the highest world to the lowest: heaven is the hiṃkāra, the sun the prastāva, the sky the udgītha, fire the pratihāra, and the earth the nidhana.

### 2.2.3

कल्पन्ते हास्मै लोका ऊर्ध्वाश्चावृत्ताश्च य एतदेवं विद्वाँल्लोकेषु पञ्चविधं सामोपास्ते ॥ २.२.३ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*kalpante hāsmai lokā ūrdhvāścāvṛttāśca ya etadevaṃ vidvām̐llokeṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāste || 2.2.3 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All these worlds, from lowest to highest and from highest to lowest, are there for the enjoyment of the one who has this knowledge and worships the Sāma in the fivefold way described.

### 2.3.1

वृष्टौ पञ्चविधं सामोपासीत पुरोवातो हिंकारो मेघो जायते स प्रस्तावो वर्षति स उद्गीथो विद्योतते स्तनयति स प्रतिहार उद्गृह्णाति तन्निधनम् ॥ २.३.१ ॥

*vṛṣṭau pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāsīta purovāto hiṃkāro megho jāyate sa prastāvo varṣati sa udgītho vidyotate stanayati sa pratihāra udgṛhṇāti tannidhanam || 2.3.1 ||*

The fivefold Sāma worship can be performed during the rain. Regard the wind that comes ahead of the rain as the hiṃkāra. The gathering clouds are the prastāva, and the rain that comes next is the udgītha. When lightning flashes and thunder roars, that is the pratihāra.

### 2.3.2

वर्षति हास्मै वर्षयति ह य एतदेवं विद्वान्वृष्टौ पञ्चविधंसामोपास्ते ॥ २.३.२ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*varṣati hāsmai varṣayati ha ya etadevaṃ vidvānvṛṣṭau pañcavidhaṃsāmopāste || 2.3.2 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

When the rain ends, that is the nidhana. For a person who performs the fivefold Sāma worship with all this in mind, the clouds bring rain as he wishes when it is due, and they may do him this favor even when it is not.

### 2.4.1

सर्वास्वप्सु पञ्चविधंसामोपासीत मेघो यत्सम्प्लवते स हिंकारो यद्वर्षति स प्रस्तावो याः प्राच्यः स्यन्दन्ते स उद्गीथो याः प्रतीच्यः स प्रतिहारः समुद्रो निधनम् ॥ २.४.१ ॥

*sarvāsvapsu pañcavidhaṃsāmopāsīta megho yatsamplavate sa hiṃkāro yadvarṣati sa prastāvo yāḥ prācyaḥ syandante sa udgītho yāḥ pratīcyaḥ sa pratihāraḥ samudro nidhanam || 2.4.1 ||*

The fivefold Sāma worship can be performed in every kind of water. When scattered clouds come together, that is the hiṃkāra. What pours down rain is the prastāva. The [river] flowing east is the udgītha, what flows west is the pratihāra, and the sea is the nidhana.

### 2.4.2

न हाप्सु प्रैत्यप्सुमान्भवति य एतदेवं विद्वान्सर्वास्वप्सु पञ्चविधंसामोपास्ते ॥ २.४.२ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*na hāpsu praityapsumānbhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānsarvāsvapsu pañcavidhaṃsāmopāste || 2.4.2 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows this and performs the fivefold Sāma worship in all forms of water will never drown unless he wants to, and he will have all the water he wants.

### 2.5.1

ऋतुषु पञ्चविधं सामोपासीत वसन्तो हिंकारः ग्रीष्मः प्रस्तावो वर्षा उद्गीथः शरत्प्रतिहारो हेमन्तो निधनम् ॥ २.५.१ ॥

*ṛtuṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāsīta vasanto hiṃkāraḥ grīṣmaḥ prastāvo varṣā udgīthaḥ śaratpratihāro hemanto nidhanam || 2.5.1 ||*

The same fivefold Sāma worship can be applied to the seasons. Take spring as the hiṃkāra, summer as the prastāva, the rainy season as the udgītha, autumn as the pratihāra, and winter as the nidhana.

### 2.5.2

कल्पन्ते हास्मा ऋतव ऋतुमान्भवति य एतदेवं विद्वानृतुषु पञ्चविधं सामोपास्ते ॥ २.५.२ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*kalpante hāsmā ṛtava ṛtumānbhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānṛtuṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāste || 2.5.2 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For the person who knows this principle of the fivefold Sāma worship and applies it to the seasons in this way, the seasons become a source of enjoyment, and the best that each has to give comes to him.

### 2.6.1

पशुषु पञ्चविधं सामोपासीताजा हिंकारोऽवयः प्रस्तावो गाव उद्गीथोऽश्वाः प्रतिहारः पुरुषो निधनम् ॥ २.६.१ ॥

*paśuṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāsītājā hiṃkāro'vayaḥ prastāvo gāva udgītho'śvāḥ pratihāraḥ puruṣo nidhanam || 2.6.1 ||*

Here is how the fivefold Sāma worship can be performed in animals. Regard goats as the hiṃkāra, sheep as the prastāva, cows as the udgītha, horses as the pratihāra, and human beings as the nidhana.

### 2.6.2

भवन्ति हास्य पशवः पशुमान्भवति य एतदेवं विद्वान्पशुषु पञ्चविधं सामोपास्ते ॥ २.६.२ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*bhavanti hāsya paśavaḥ paśumānbhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānpaśuṣu pañcavidhaṃ sāmopāste || 2.6.2 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows this and performs the fivefold Sāma worship in animals gains many animals for his enjoyment, and also owns a large number of them as his personal wealth.

### 2.7.1

प्राणेषु पञ्चविधं परोवरीयः सामोपासीत प्राणो हिंकारो वाक्प्रस्तावश्चक्षुरुद्गीथः श्रोत्रं प्रतिहारो मनो निधनं परोवरीयांसि वा एतानि ॥ २.७.१ ॥

*prāṇeṣu pañcavidhaṃ parovarīyaḥ sāmopāsīta prāṇo hiṃkāro vākprastāvaścakṣurudgīthaḥ śrotraṃ pratihāro mano nidhanaṃ parovarīyāṃsi vā etāni || 2.7.1 ||*

The fivefold worship of Sāma should be carried out in the organs, rising higher step by step. Smell is the hiṃkāra, speech the prastāva, the eyes the udgītha, the ears the pratihāra, and the mind the nidhana. Worship these organs, giving each more respect than the one before it.

### 2.7.2

परोवरीयो हास्य भवति परोवरीयसो ह लोकाञ्जयति य एतदेवं विद्वान्प्राणेषु पञ्चविधं परोवरीयः सामोपास्त इति तु पञ्चविधस्य ॥ २.७.२ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*parovarīyo hāsya bhavati parovarīyaso ha lokāñjayati ya etadevaṃ vidvānprāṇeṣu pañcavidhaṃ parovarīyaḥ sāmopāsta iti tu pañcavidhasya || 2.7.2 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Someone who knows the fivefold Sāma worship and practises it in the organs, honouring each more than the last, finds his life growing ever more excellent, and he reaches better and better worlds as well.

### 2.8.1

अथ सप्तविधस्य वाचि सप्तविध्ं सामोपासीत यत्किंच वाचो हुमिति स हिंकारो यत्प्रेति स प्रस्तावो यदेति स आदिः ॥ २.८.१ ॥

*atha saptavidhasya vāci saptavidhṃ sāmopāsīta yatkiṃca vāco humiti sa hiṃkāro yatpreti sa prastāvo yadeti sa ādiḥ || 2.8.1 ||*

Now the sevenfold Sāma worship is taken up. It can be performed in speech. Wherever the syllable huṃ occurs in speech, that is hiṃkāra. In the same way, wherever pra occurs, take that as the prastāva. And wherever ā occurs, that is ādi (the beginning).

### 2.8.2

यदुदिति स उद्गीथो यत्प्रतीति स प्रतिहारो यदुपेति स उपद्रवो यन्नीति तन्निधनम् ॥ २.८.२ ॥

*yaduditi sa udgītho yatpratīti sa pratihāro yadupeti sa upadravo yannīti tannidhanam || 2.8.2 ||*

Wherever the syllable ut occurs, that is the udgītha. Where prati appears, that is the pratihāra. Where upa is found, that is the upadrava. And where ni is found, that is the nidhana.

### 2.8.3

दुग्धेऽस्मै वाग्दोहं यो वाचो दोहोऽन्नवानन्नादो भवति य एतदेवं विद्वान्वाचि सप्तविधं सामोपास्ते ॥ २.८.३ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*dugdhe'smai vāgdohaṃ yo vāco doho'nnavānannādo bhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānvāci saptavidhaṃ sāmopāste || 2.8.3 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows Sāma in this way and performs the sevenfold Sāma worship in speech receives from speech every good thing it can give. He also has abundant food to eat, and he eats it and so looks radiant with health.

### 2.9.1

अथ खल्वमुमादित्यंसप्तविधं सामोपासीत सर्वदा समस्तेन साम मां प्रति मां प्रतीति सर्वेण समस्तेन साम ॥ २.९.१ ॥

*atha khalvamumādityaṃsaptavidhaṃ sāmopāsīta sarvadā samastena sāma māṃ prati māṃ pratīti sarveṇa samastena sāma || 2.9.1 ||*

Next, be sure to worship the sevenfold Sāma in the sun. The sun is the Sāma because it never varies. The sun also makes each of us think, "It is looking at me, it is looking at me." Since it is the same to everyone, it is called Sāma.

### 2.9.2

तस्मिन्निमानि सर्वाणि भूतान्यन्वायत्तानीति विद्यात्तस्य यत्पुरोदयात्स हिंकारस्तदस्य पशवोऽन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्ते हिं कुर्वन्ति हिंकारभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.२ ॥

*tasminnimāni sarvāṇi bhūtānyanvāyattānīti vidyāttasya yatpurodayātsa hiṃkārastadasya paśavo'nvāyattāstasmātte hiṃ kurvanti hiṃkārabhājino hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.2 ||*

Know that all beings depend on the sun. Before rising, the sun has a particular look, and that look is its hiṃkāra. Animals, being dependent on the sun, share in this hiṃkāra, and that is why they make the sound hiṃ.

### 2.9.3

अथ यत्प्रथमोदिते स प्रस्तावस्तदस्य मनुष्या अन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्ते प्रस्तुतिकामाः प्रशंसाकामाः प्रस्तावभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.३ ॥

*atha yatprathamodite sa prastāvastadasya manuṣyā anvāyattāstasmātte prastutikāmāḥ praśaṃsākāmāḥ prastāvabhājino hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.3 ||*

Then the form the sun takes shortly after it rises is the prastāva. This form charms human beings. Because they take part in the praise and adoration of the prastāva, they seek adoration and praise for themselves.

### 2.9.4

अथ यत्संगववेलायां स आदिस्तदस्य वयांस्यन्वायत्तानि तस्मात्तान्यन्तरिक्षेऽनारम्बणान्यादायात्मानं परिपतन्त्यादिभाजीनि ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.४ ॥

*atha yatsaṃgavavelāyāṃ sa ādistadasya vayāṃsyanvāyattāni tasmāttānyantarikṣe'nārambaṇānyādāyātmānaṃ paripatantyādibhājīni hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.4 ||*

Next, when the sun's rays spread everywhere a little after sunrise, that form of the sun is the ādi of the Sāma. Birds are linked with this form. Somehow they feel safely sheltered then, and so they can fly freely through the sky with no support. They also act as though they were joining in the ādi offered to the Sāma.

### 2.9.5

अथ यत्सम्प्रतिमध्यंदिने स उद्गीथस्तदस्य देवा अन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्ते सत्तमाः प्राजापत्यानामुद्गीथभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.५ ॥

*atha yatsampratimadhyaṃdine sa udgīthastadasya devā anvāyattāstasmātte sattamāḥ prājāpatyānāmudgīthabhājino hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.5 ||*

Next, the form of the sun at exactly noon is the udgītha. Gods and goddesses are linked with this form. So of all Prajāpati's children the gods and goddesses are held to be the best, since they share in singing the udgītha of the Sāma.

### 2.9.6

अथ यदूर्ध्वं मध्यंदिनात्प्रागपराह्णात्स प्रतिहारस्तदस्य गर्भा अन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्ते प्रतिहृतानावपद्यन्ते प्रतिहारभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.६ ॥

*atha yadūrdhvaṃ madhyaṃdinātprāgaparāhṇātsa pratihārastadasya garbhā anvāyattāstasmātte pratihṛtānāvapadyante pratihārabhājino hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.6 ||*

Next, the appearance of the sun between noon and afternoon is the pratihāra. Foetuses in the womb are attached to this pratihāra. That is why they stay held up and do not drop down, and why they have a right to share in the pratihāra addressed to the Sāma.

### 2.9.7

अथ यदूर्ध्वमपराह्णात्प्रागस्तमयात्स उपद्रवस्तदस्यारण्या अन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्ते पुरुषं दृष्ट्वा कक्षंश्वभ्रमित्युपद्रवन्त्युपद्रवभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्नः ॥ २.९.७ ॥

*atha yadūrdhvamaparāhṇātprāgastamayātsa upadravastadasyāraṇyā anvāyattāstasmātte puruṣaṃ dṛṣṭvā kakṣaṃśvabhramityupadravantyupadravabhājino hyetasya sāmnaḥ || 2.9.7 ||*

Next, the form of the sun between afternoon and sunset is called the upadrava. Wild animals favour this form, for when the sun stands there and they catch sight of a human being, they can scurry off into the forest or into their holes. These animals can also share in the upadrava to the Sāma.

### 2.9.8

अथ यत्प्रथमास्तमिते तन्निधनं तदस्य पितरोऽन्वायत्तास्तस्मात्तान्निदधति निधनभाजिनो ह्येतस्य साम्न एवं खल्वमुमादित्यं सप्तविधं सामोपास्ते ॥ २.९.८ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*atha yatprathamāstamite tannidhanaṃ tadasya pitaro'nvāyattāstasmāttānnidadhati nidhanabhājino hyetasya sāmna evaṃ khalvamumādityaṃ saptavidhaṃ sāmopāste || 2.9.8 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Next, the nidhana is the form of the sun as it sets. The ancestors love this form, and that is why offerings are made to them as the sun goes down (or are placed on straws in their honour at the time of the śrāddha rites). So the ancestors share in the nidhana in honour of the Sāma. This is how the sevenfold Sāma worship is offered to the sun.

### 2.10.1

अथ खल्वात्मसंमितमतिमृत्यु सप्तविधं सामोपासीत हिंकार इति त्र्यक्षरं प्रस्ताव इति त्र्यक्षरं तत्समम् ॥ २.१०.१ ॥

*atha khalvātmasaṃmitamatimṛtyu saptavidhaṃ sāmopāsīta hiṃkāra iti tryakṣaraṃ prastāva iti tryakṣaraṃ tatsamam || 2.10.1 ||*

Having worshipped the Sāma as the sun, one should next perform the sevenfold Sāma worship using words with the same number of syllables. In this way one overcomes death. The word hiṃkāra has three syllables; so also,

### 2.10.2

आदिरिति द्व्यक्षरं प्रतिहार इति चतुरक्षरं तत इहैकं तत्समम् ॥ २.१०.२ ॥

*ādiriti dvyakṣaraṃ pratihāra iti caturakṣaraṃ tata ihaikaṃ tatsamam || 2.10.2 ||*

The word ādi has two syllables and the word pratihāra has four. Take one syllable from pratihāra and add it to ādi, and the two will have an equal count.

### 2.10.3

उद्गीथ इति त्र्यक्षरमुपद्रव इति चतुरक्षरं त्रिभिस्त्रिभिः समं भवत्यक्षरमतिशिष्यते त्र्यक्षरं तत्समम् ॥ २.१०.३ ॥

*udgītha iti tryakṣaramupadrava iti caturakṣaraṃ tribhistribhiḥ samaṃ bhavatyakṣaramatiśiṣyate tryakṣaraṃ tatsamam || 2.10.3 ||*

The word udgītha has three syllables and the word upadrava has four. Counted as three-syllabled, they are equal. The syllable va in upadrava is then left over. As far as their three syllables go, they are equal.

### 2.10.4

निधनमिति त्र्यक्षरं तत्सममेव भवति तानि ह वा एतानि द्वाविंशतिरक्षराणि ॥ २.१०.४ ॥

*nidhanamiti tryakṣaraṃ tatsamameva bhavati tāni ha vā etāni dvāviṃśatirakṣarāṇi || 2.10.4 ||*

The word nidhana has three syllables. All words of three syllables each are alike when used in praise of the Sāma. Together they amount to twenty-two syllables.

### 2.10.5

एकविंशत्यादित्यमाप्नोत्येकविंशो वा इतोऽसावादित्यो द्वाविंशेन परमादित्याज्जयति तन्नाकं तद्विशोकम् ॥ २.१०.५ ॥

*ekaviṃśatyādityamāpnotyekaviṃśo vā ito'sāvādityo dvāviṃśena paramādityājjayati tannākaṃ tadviśokam || 2.10.5 ||*

With these twenty-one syllables one can reach the status of the sun (which is also Death). The sun holds the twenty-first place after the things that lie between earth and sun, namely the twelve months, the five seasons, and the three worlds. Knowing the twenty-second syllable, one can pass beyond the sun, to a world full of joy and free of every sorrow.

### 2.10.6

आप्नोती हादित्यस्य जयं परो हास्यादित्यजयाज्जयो भवति य एतदेवं विद्वानात्मसंमितमतिमृत्यु सप्तविधं सामोपास्ते सामोपास्ते ॥ २.१०.६ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*āpnotī hādityasya jayaṃ paro hāsyādityajayājjayo bhavati ya etadevaṃ vidvānātmasaṃmitamatimṛtyu saptavidhaṃ sāmopāste sāmopāste || 2.10.6 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

A person who knows all about the Sāma and performs the sevenfold Sāma worship, regarding the Sāma as himself and as something beyond death, gains the state of the sun and then a place even higher than the sun.

### 2.11.1

मनो हिंकारो वाक्प्रस्तावश्चक्षुरुद्गीथः श्रोत्रं प्रतिहारः प्राणो निधनमेतद्गायत्रं प्राणेषु प्रोतम् ॥ २.११.१ ॥

*mano hiṃkāro vākprastāvaścakṣurudgīthaḥ śrotraṃ pratihāraḥ prāṇo nidhanametadgāyatraṃ prāṇeṣu protam || 2.11.1 ||*

The mind is hiṃkāra, speech the prastāva, the eyes the udgītha, the ears the pratihāra, and the vital breath, in its fine (five?) forms, the nidhana. The vital breath governs the Gāyatrī prayer.

### 2.11.2

स एवमेतद्गायत्रं प्राणेषु प्रोतं वेद प्राणी भवति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या महामनाः स्यात्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.११.२ ॥ ॥ इति एकदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa evametadgāyatraṃ prāṇeṣu protaṃ veda prāṇī bhavati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā mahāmanāḥ syāttadvratam || 2.11.2 || || iti ekadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

This Gāyatra Sāma has its root in the prāṇas. One who knows this becomes full of vitality and lives long and brilliantly. He is fortunate in his children and has many domestic animals. He is among the most famous of people. His aim in life is to be noble-minded.

### 2.12.1

अभिमन्थति स हिंकारो धूमो जायते स प्रस्तावो ज्वलति स उद्गीथोऽङ्गारा भवन्ति स प्रतिहार उपशाम्यति तन्निधनं संशाम्यति तन्निधनमेतद्रथंतरमग्नौ प्रोतम् ॥ २.१२.१ ॥

*abhimanthati sa hiṃkāro dhūmo jāyate sa prastāvo jvalati sa udgītho'ṅgārā bhavanti sa pratihāra upaśāmyati tannidhanaṃ saṃśāmyati tannidhanametadrathaṃtaramagnau protam || 2.12.1 ||*

Rubbing two pieces of wood together to kindle a fire is the hiṃkāra. The smoke that comes from it is the prastāva. The flame when it appears is the udgītha. The resulting charcoals are the pratihāra, and when the fire starts to die, that is the nidhana. When the flame is fully out, that too is the nidhana. This Sāma, called Rathantara, has its root in fire.

### 2.12.2

स य एवमेतद्रथंतरमग्नौ प्रोतं वेद ब्रह्मवर्चस्यन्नादो भवति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या न प्रत्यङ्ङग्निमाचामेन्न निष्ठीवेत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१२.२ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadrathaṃtaramagnau protaṃ veda brahmavarcasyannādo bhavati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā na pratyaṅṅagnimācāmenna niṣṭhīvettadvratam || 2.12.2 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that this Rathantara Sāma has its root in fire gains the kind of glow that Vedic learning brings. He enjoys his food. He lives his full span, and his life is brilliant. He is well known for his children and his wealth in animals, and society holds him in great respect. His vow is never to eat with fire in front of him and never to spit.

### 2.13.1

उपमन्त्रयते स हिंकारो ज्ञपयते स प्रस्तावः स्त्रिया सह शेते स उद्गीथः प्रति स्त्रीं सह शेते स प्रतिहारः कालं गच्छति तन्निधनं पारं गच्छति तन्निधनमेतद्वामदेव्यं मिथुने प्रोतम् ॥ २.१३.१ ॥ स य एवमेतद्वामदेव्यं मिथुने प्रोतं वेद मिथुनी भवति मिथुनान्मिथुनात्प्रजायते सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या न कांचन परिहरेत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१३.२ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥

*upamantrayate sa hiṃkāro jñapayate sa prastāvaḥ striyā saha śete sa udgīthaḥ prati strīṃ saha śete sa pratihāraḥ kālaṃ gacchati tannidhanaṃ pāraṃ gacchati tannidhanametadvāmadevyaṃ mithune protam || 2.13.1 || sa ya evametadvāmadevyaṃ mithune protaṃ veda mithunī bhavati mithunānmithunātprajāyate sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā na kāṃcana pariharettadvratam || 2.13.2 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

A man beckoning to a woman is the syllable Him; his gratifying her is the Prastava; his lying with her is the Pratihara; his spending time with her is the Nidhana; and the end of the sexual act is also the Nidhana. This is the Vamadevya Saman as woven into sexual intercourse.
2.13.2 One who knows the Vamadevya Saman as woven into sexual intercourse does not suffer the pang of separation and procreates from every intercourse. He reaches the full length of life, lives brightly, and becomes great in children, cattle, and fame. The injunction for him is: "Do not reject a woman who comes to you seeking intercourse."

### 2.14.1

उद्यन्हिंकार उदितः प्रस्तावो मध्यंदिन उद्गीथोऽपराह्णः प्रतिहारोऽस्तं यन्निधनमेतद्बृहदादित्ये प्रोतम् ॥ २.१४.१ ॥

*udyanhiṃkāra uditaḥ prastāvo madhyaṃdina udgītho'parāhṇaḥ pratihāro'staṃ yannidhanametadbṛhadāditye protam || 2.14.1 ||*

The rising sun is the hiṃkāra, the sun already risen the prastāva, the midday sun the udgītha, the afternoon sun the pratihāra, and the setting sun the nidhana. This Sāma, called Bṛhat, is based on the sun.

### 2.14.2

स य एवमेतद्बृहदादित्ये प्रोतं वेद तेजस्व्यन्नादो भवति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या तपन्तं न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१४.२ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्दशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadbṛhadāditye protaṃ veda tejasvyannādo bhavati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā tapantaṃ na nindettadvratam || 2.14.2 || || iti caturdaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma called Bṛhat has its root in the sun is spirited and has a big appetite. He lives his full span, has a brilliant career, and is renowned for his children and his wealth in animals. His success brings him much fame. His vow is never to speak a word against the sun, which gives us heat.

### 2.15.1

अभ्राणि सम्प्लवन्ते स हिंकारो मेघो जायते स प्रस्तावो वर्षति स उद्गीथो विद्योतते स्तनयति स प्रतिहार उद्गृह्णाति तन्निधनमेतद्वैरूपं पर्जन्ये प्रोतम् ॥ २.१५.१ ॥

*abhrāṇi samplavante sa hiṃkāro megho jāyate sa prastāvo varṣati sa udgītho vidyotate stanayati sa pratihāra udgṛhṇāti tannidhanametadvairūpaṃ parjanye protam || 2.15.1 ||*

When light clouds gather together, that is the hiṃkāra. When clouds likely to bring rain collect, that is the prastāva. When the rain begins, that is the udgītha. Then come flashes of lightning and the roar of thunder, which are the pratihāra. When it all stops, that is the nidhana. This Sāma, called Vairūpa, has its root in the clouds.

### 2.15.2

स य एवमेतद्वैरूपं पर्जन्ये प्रोतं वेद विरूपांश्च सुरूपंश्च पशूनवरुन्धे सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या वर्षन्तं न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१५.२ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadvairūpaṃ parjanye protaṃ veda virūpāṃśca surūpaṃśca paśūnavarundhe sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā varṣantaṃ na nindettadvratam || 2.15.2 || || iti pañcadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that this Sāma called Vairūpa has its source in the clouds acquires many kinds of animals, all of them beautiful. His life is long and brilliant, and his children and animals make him famous. His achievements also mark him out as a great person.

### 2.16.1

वसन्तो हिंकारो ग्रीष्मः प्रस्तावो वर्षा उद्गीथः शरत्प्रतिहारो हेमन्तो निधनमेतद्वैराजमृतुषु प्रोतम् ॥ २.१६.१ ॥

*vasanto hiṃkāro grīṣmaḥ prastāvo varṣā udgīthaḥ śaratpratihāro hemanto nidhanametadvairājamṛtuṣu protam || 2.16.1 ||*

Spring is the hiṃkāra, summer the prastāva, the rainy season the udgītha, autumn the pratihāra, and winter the nidhana. This Sāma, known as Vairāja, has its root in the seasons.

### 2.16.2

स य एवमेतद्वैराजमृतुषु प्रोतं वेद विराजति प्रजया पशुभिर्ब्रह्मवर्चसेन सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्यर्तून्न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१६.२ ॥ ॥ इति षोडशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadvairājamṛtuṣu protaṃ veda virājati prajayā paśubhirbrahmavarcasena sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyartūnna nindettadvratam || 2.16.2 || || iti ṣoḍaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma called Vairāja has its root in the seasons is surrounded by his children and animals and carries a radiance born of Vedic learning. He lives long and brilliantly and is regarded as great for his children and his animals. He is also highly respected for his great deeds. His vow is never to criticize the seasons.

### 2.17.1

पृथिवी हिंकारोऽन्तरिक्षं प्रस्तावो द्यौरुद्गीथो दिशः प्रतिहारः समुद्रो निधनमेताः शक्वर्यो लोकेषु प्रोताः ॥ २.१७.१ ॥

*pṛthivī hiṃkāro'ntarikṣaṃ prastāvo dyaurudgītho diśaḥ pratihāraḥ samudro nidhanametāḥ śakvaryo lokeṣu protāḥ || 2.17.1 ||*

The earth is the hiṃkāra, the space between earth and heaven the prastāva, heaven the udgītha, the quarters the pratihāra, and the ocean the nidhana. The Sāma known as Śakvarī has its root in the earth and the other worlds.

### 2.17.2

स य एवमेताः शक्वर्यो लोकेषु प्रोता वेद लोकी भवति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या लोकान्न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१७.२ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametāḥ śakvaryo lokeṣu protā veda lokī bhavati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā lokānna nindettadvratam || 2.17.2 || || iti saptadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma known as Śakvarī is located in the worlds goes to the best world. His life is long and brilliant, and he is well known for his children and his wealth in animals. His great deeds lead people to regard him as a great person. He vows never to criticize the worlds.

### 2.18.1

अजा हिंकारोऽवयः प्रस्तावो गाव उद्गीथोऽश्वाः प्रतिहारः पुरुषो निधनमेता रेवत्यः पशुषु प्रोताः ॥ २.१८.१ ॥

*ajā hiṃkāro'vayaḥ prastāvo gāva udgītho'śvāḥ pratihāraḥ puruṣo nidhanametā revatyaḥ paśuṣu protāḥ || 2.18.1 ||*

Goats are the hiṃkāra, sheep the prastāva, cows the udgītha, horses the pratihāra, and a human being the nidhana. The Sāma called Revatī is established in animals.

### 2.18.2

स य एवमेता रेवत्यः पशुषु प्रोता वेद पशुमान्भवति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या पशून्न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.१८.२ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टादशः खण्डः ॥
