*sa ya evametā revatyaḥ paśuṣu protā veda paśumānbhavati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā paśūnna nindettadvratam || 2.18.2 || || iti aṣṭādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma called Revatī has its root in animals comes to own many animals. His life is long and brilliant, and he becomes well known for his children and his wealth in animals. His great deeds also make him a truly great person. His vow is never to say anything bad about animals.

### 2.19.1

लोम हिंकारस्त्वक्प्रस्तावो मांसमुद्गीथोस्थि प्रतिहारो मज्जा निधनमेतद्यज्ञायज्ञीयमङ्गेषु प्रोतम् ॥ २.१९.१ ॥

*loma hiṃkārastvakprastāvo māṃsamudgīthosthi pratihāro majjā nidhanametadyajñāyajñīyamaṅgeṣu protam || 2.19.1 ||*

Hair is the hiṃkāra, skin the prastāva, flesh the udgītha, bone the pratihāra, and marrow the nidhana. The Sāma called Yajñāyajñīya is spread throughout the limbs of the body.

### 2.19.2

स य एवमेतद्यज्ञायज्ञीयमङ्गेषु प्रोतं वेदाङ्गी भवति नाङ्गेन विहूर्छति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या संवत्सरं मज्ज्ञो नाश्नीयात्तद्व्रतं मज्ज्ञो नाश्नीयादिति वा ॥ २.१९.२ ॥ ॥ इति एकोनविंशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadyajñāyajñīyamaṅgeṣu protaṃ vedāṅgī bhavati nāṅgena vihūrchati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā saṃvatsaraṃ majjño nāśnīyāttadvrataṃ majjño nāśnīyāditi vā || 2.19.2 || || iti ekonaviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma known as Yajñāyajñīya is located in the different parts of the body gains a firm body, and none of his organs has any defect. His life is long and brilliant, and he becomes great through his children and his wealth in animals. His great deeds also bring him fame. His vow is not to eat meat for one whole year, or not to eat it at all.

### 2.20.1

अग्निर्हिंकारो वायुः प्रस्ताव आदित्य उद्गीथो नक्षत्राणि प्रतिहारश्चन्द्रमा निधनमेतद्राजनं देवतासु प्रोतम् ॥ २.२०.१ ॥

*agnirhiṃkāro vāyuḥ prastāva āditya udgītho nakṣatrāṇi pratihāraścandramā nidhanametadrājanaṃ devatāsu protam || 2.20.1 ||*

Fire is the hiṃkāra, air the prastāva, the sun the udgītha, the stars the pratihāra, and the moon the nidhana. This Sāma, known as Rājana, has its root in the gods and goddesses.

### 2.20.2

स य एवमेतद्राजनं देवतासु प्रोतं वेदैतासामेव देवतानांसलोकतांसर्ष्टितांसायुज्यं गच्छति सर्वमायुरेति ज्योग्जीवति महान्प्रजया पशुभिर्भवति महान्कीर्त्या ब्राह्मणान्न निन्देत्तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.२०.२ ॥ ॥ इति विंशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya evametadrājanaṃ devatāsu protaṃ vedaitāsāmeva devatānāṃsalokatāṃsarṣṭitāṃsāyujyaṃ gacchati sarvamāyureti jyogjīvati mahānprajayā paśubhirbhavati mahānkīrtyā brāhmaṇānna nindettadvratam || 2.20.2 || || iti viṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows that the Sāma known as Rājana is established in the gods and goddesses shares their worlds, has the same rights and privileges as they do, or has the same form. His life, too, is long and brilliant. He becomes great through his children and his wealth in animals, and through his great deeds as well. His vow is never to speak ill of the brāhmins.

### 2.21.1

त्रयी विद्या हिंकारस्त्रय इमे लोकाः स प्रस्तावोऽग्निर्वायुरादित्यः स उद्गीथो नक्षत्राणि वयांसि मरीचयः स प्रतिहारः सर्पा गन्धर्वाः पितरस्तन्निधनमेतत्साम सर्वस्मिन्प्रोतम् ॥ २.२१.१ ॥

*trayī vidyā hiṃkārastraya ime lokāḥ sa prastāvo'gnirvāyurādityaḥ sa udgītho nakṣatrāṇi vayāṃsi marīcayaḥ sa pratihāraḥ sarpā gandharvāḥ pitarastannidhanametatsāma sarvasminprotam || 2.21.1 ||*

The three vidyās (the Ṛk, the Yajuḥ, and the Sāma) together are the hiṃkāra. These three worlds (the earth, the space between earth and heaven, and heaven) together are the prastāva. Fire, air, and the sun together are the udgītha. The stars, the birds, and the rays together are the pratihāra. Serpents, gandharvas, and the ancestors together are the nidhana. This Sāma dwells in everything.

### 2.21.2

स य एवमेतत्साम सर्वस्मिन्प्रोतं वेद सर्वं ह भवति ॥ २.२१.२ ॥

*sa ya evametatsāma sarvasminprotaṃ veda sarvaṃ ha bhavati || 2.21.2 ||*

One who knows that this Sāma is in everything becomes one with everything (or becomes the Lord of everything).

### 2.21.3

तदेष श्लोको यानि पञ्चधा त्रीणी त्रीणि तेभ्यो न ज्यायः परमन्यदस्ति ॥ २.२१.३ ॥

*tadeṣa śloko yāni pañcadhā trīṇī trīṇi tebhyo na jyāyaḥ paramanyadasti || 2.21.3 ||*

A verse on this subject says: Sāma has five parts, and each of them is divided into three. Nothing is higher than these fifteen forms of Sāma.

### 2.21.4

यस्तद्वेद स वेद सर्वं सर्वा दिशो बलिमस्मै हरन्ति सर्वमस्मीत्युपासित तद्व्रतं तद्व्रतम् ॥ २.२१.४ ॥ ॥ इति एकविंशः खण्डः ॥

*yastadveda sa veda sarvaṃ sarvā diśo balimasmai haranti sarvamasmītyupāsita tadvrataṃ tadvratam || 2.21.4 || || iti ekaviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

One who knows Sāma knows everything, and gifts reach him from every quarter. His vow will be to tell himself constantly, "I am one with all."

### 2.22.1

विनर्दि साम्नो वृणे पशव्यमित्यग्नेरुद्गीथोऽनिरुक्तः प्रजापतेर्निरुक्तः सोमस्य मृदु श्लक्ष्णं वायोः श्लक्ष्णं बलवदिन्द्रस्य क्रौञ्चं बृहस्पतेरपध्वान्तं वरुणस्य तान्सर्वानेवोपसेवेत वारुणं त्वेव वर्जयेत् ॥ २.२२.१ ॥

*vinardi sāmno vṛṇe paśavyamityagnerudgītho'niruktaḥ prajāpaterniruktaḥ somasya mṛdu ślakṣṇaṃ vāyoḥ ślakṣṇaṃ balavadindrasya krauñcaṃ bṛhaspaterapadhvāntaṃ varuṇasya tānsarvānevopaseveta vāruṇaṃ tveva varjayet || 2.22.1 ||*

The vinardi voice for singing the Sāma is good for animals, and its presiding deity is Agni, the god of fire. Keeping this in mind, I pray to have this voice. The god Prajāpati presides over the udgītha sung in the anirukta (unclear) voice. The voice with Soma as its presiding deity is nirukta (clear). The voice of Vāyu, the god of air, is soft and pleasant, and that of Indra is strong. The voice presided over by Bṛhaspati resembles the call of the krauñca bird, and that of Varuṇa sounds like a broken metal pot. Cultivate all of these except the one belonging to Varuṇa.

### 2.22.2

अमृतत्वं देवेभ्य आगायानीत्यागायेत्स्वधां पितृभ्य आशां मनुष्येभ्यस्तृणोदकं पशुभ्यः स्वर्गं लोकं यजमानायान्नमात्मन आगायानीत्येतानि मनसा ध्यायन्नप्रमत्तः स्तुवीत ॥ २.२२.२ ॥

*amṛtatvaṃ devebhya āgāyānītyāgāyetsvadhāṃ pitṛbhya āśāṃ manuṣyebhyastṛṇodakaṃ paśubhyaḥ svargaṃ lokaṃ yajamānāyānnamātmana āgāyānītyetāni manasā dhyāyannapramattaḥ stuvīta || 2.22.2 ||*

One should sing with this resolve: "Through my singing I will obtain immortality for the gods and goddesses, food offerings for the ancestors, hope for humanity, food and water for the animals, heaven for those who perform sacrifices, and food for myself. All this I will obtain by singing." Thinking this way, one should sing without raising the voice too much (or without becoming too excited).

### 2.22.3

सर्वे स्वरा इन्द्रस्यात्मानः सर्व ऊष्माणः प्रजापतेरात्मानः सर्वे स्पर्शा मृत्योरात्मानस्तं यदि स्वरेषूपालभेतेन्द्रंशरणं प्रपन्नोऽभूवं स त्वा प्रति वक्ष्यतीत्येनं ब्रूयात् ॥ २.२२.३ ॥

*sarve svarā indrasyātmānaḥ sarva ūṣmāṇaḥ prajāpaterātmānaḥ sarve sparśā mṛtyorātmānastaṃ yadi svareṣūpālabhetendraṃśaraṇaṃ prapanno'bhūvaṃ sa tvā prati vakṣyatītyenaṃ brūyāt || 2.22.3 ||*

All the vowels are like the body of Indra with its many parts. The sibilants (śa, ṣa, and so on) are like the body of Prajāpati with its many parts. The consonants, beginning with ka, are the body and limbs of Death. If someone finds fault with how the udgātā pronounces the vowels, he may reply: "When I began to sing, I took refuge in Indra (that is, I sought Indra's blessings). Ask him, and he will give you the right answer to your criticism."

### 2.22.4

अथ यद्येनमूष्मसूपालभेत प्रजापतिंशरणं प्रपन्नोऽभूवं स त्वा प्रति पेक्ष्यतीत्येनं ब्रूयादथ यद्येनं स्पर्शेषूपालभेत मृत्युं शरणं प्रपन्नोऽभूवं स त्वा प्रति धक्ष्यतीत्येनं ब्रूयात् ॥ २.२२.४ ॥

*atha yadyenamūṣmasūpālabheta prajāpatiṃśaraṇaṃ prapanno'bhūvaṃ sa tvā prati pekṣyatītyenaṃ brūyādatha yadyenaṃ sparśeṣūpālabheta mṛtyuṃ śaraṇaṃ prapanno'bhūvaṃ sa tvā prati dhakṣyatītyenaṃ brūyāt || 2.22.4 ||*

Then, if someone faults his pronunciation of the sibilants, the singer will reply: "When I began to sing I sought Prajāpati's blessings, so he will crush you." And if someone faults his pronunciation of the consonants, the singer will reply: "I sought Death's protection as I sang, so he will burn you to ashes."

### 2.22.5

सर्वे स्वरा घोषवन्तो बलवन्तो वक्तव्या इन्द्रे बलं ददानीति सर्व ऊष्माणोऽग्रस्ता अनिरस्ता विवृता वक्तव्याः प्रजापतेरात्मानं परिददानीति सर्वे स्पर्शा लेशेनानभिनिहिता वक्तव्या मृत्योरात्मानं परिहराणीति ॥ २.२२.५ ॥ ॥ इति द्वाविंशः खण्डः ॥

*sarve svarā ghoṣavanto balavanto vaktavyā indre balaṃ dadānīti sarva ūṣmāṇo'grastā anirastā vivṛtā vaktavyāḥ prajāpaterātmānaṃ paridadānīti sarve sparśā leśenānabhinihitā vaktavyā mṛtyorātmānaṃ pariharāṇīti || 2.22.5 || || iti dvāviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Pronounce each vowel clearly and with force, thinking as you do, "I will give some of my strength to Indra." Pronounce the sibilants fully and distinctly too, without sounding as if you were swallowing part of them or spitting them out, thinking as you say them, "I surrender myself to Prajāpati." Then pronounce each consonant separately and clearly, thinking as you do, "I will save myself from death."

### 2.23.1

त्रयो धर्मस्कन्धा यज्ञोऽध्ययनं दानमिति प्रथमस्तप एव द्वितीयो ब्रह्मचार्याचार्यकुलवासी तृतीयोऽत्यन्तमात्मानमाचार्यकुलेऽवसादयन्सर्व एते पुण्यलोका भवन्ति ब्रह्मसंस्थोऽमृतत्वमेति ॥ २.२३.१ ॥

*trayo dharmaskandhā yajño'dhyayanaṃ dānamiti prathamastapa eva dvitīyo brahmacāryācāryakulavāsī tṛtīyo'tyantamātmānamācāryakule'vasādayansarva ete puṇyalokā bhavanti brahmasaṃstho'mṛtatvameti || 2.23.1 ||*

Religion has three divisions. The first is made up of sacrifices, study, and charity. The second consists of austerities such as fasting. The third is a life of celibacy, living in the teacher's house until death. People devoted to these three divisions of religion go to heaven when they die. But one who is devoted to Brahman reaches immortality.

### 2.23.2

प्रजापतिर्लोकानभ्यतपत्तेभ्योऽभितप्तेभ्यस्त्रयी विद्या सम्प्रास्रवत्तामभ्यतपत्तस्या अभितप्ताया एतान्यक्षराणि सम्प्रास्र्वन्त भूर्भुवः स्वरिति ॥ २.२३.२ ॥

*prajāpatirlokānabhyatapattebhyo'bhitaptebhyastrayī vidyā samprāsravattāmabhyatapattasyā abhitaptāyā etānyakṣarāṇi samprāsrvanta bhūrbhuvaḥ svariti || 2.23.2 ||*

Prajāpati (that is, Virāṭ) reflected on the worlds he would have, and from his reflection the three Vedas took shape. He then reflected on the Vedas, and as a result the Vedas gave birth to the three vyāhṛtis: bhūḥ, bhuvaḥ, and svaḥ.

### 2.23.3

तान्यभ्यतपत्तेभ्योऽभितप्तेभ्य ॐकारः सम्प्रास्रवत्तद्यथा शङ्कुना सर्वाणि पर्णानि संतृण्णान्येवमोंकारेण सर्वा वाक्संतृण्णोंकार एवेदं सर्वमोंकार एवेदं सर्वम् ॥ २.२३.३ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोविंशः खण्डः ॥

*tānyabhyatapattebhyo'bhitaptebhya ॐkāraḥ samprāsravattadyathā śaṅkunā sarvāṇi parṇāni saṃtṛṇṇānyevamoṃkāreṇa sarvā vāksaṃtṛṇṇoṃkāra evedaṃ sarvamoṃkāra evedaṃ sarvam || 2.23.3 || || iti trayoviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Prajāpati then meditated on those three vyāhṛtis (bhūḥ, bhuvaḥ, and svaḥ), and from them, as he reflected, Oṃkāra emerged. As a network of ribs runs through every part of a leaf, so Oṃkāra runs through every form of speech (or through everything). All this is Oṃkāra. All this is Oṃkāra.

### 2.24.1

ब्रह्मवादिनो वदन्ति यद्वसूनां प्रातः सवनं रुद्राणां माध्यंदिनं सवनमादित्यानां च विश्वेषां च देवानां तृतीयसवनम् ॥ २.२४.१ ॥

*brahmavādino vadanti yadvasūnāṃ prātaḥ savanaṃ rudrāṇāṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamādityānāṃ ca viśveṣāṃ ca devānāṃ tṛtīyasavanam || 2.24.1 ||*

Those who believe in Brahman say: The morning savana belongs to the Vasus, the midday savana to the Rudras, and the third, the evening savana, to the Ādityas and the Viśvadevas (that is, all the gods and goddesses).

### 2.24.2

क्व तर्हि यजमानस्य लोक इति स यस्तं न विद्यात्कथं कुर्यादथ विद्वान्कुर्यात् ॥ २.२४.२ ॥

*kva tarhi yajamānasya loka iti sa yastaṃ na vidyātkathaṃ kuryādatha vidvānkuryāt || 2.24.2 ||*

Where, then, is the place for the yajamāna (the one who performs a sacrifice)? How can anyone who does not know that place perform a sacrifice? Only the one who knows can perform it.

### 2.24.3

पुरा प्रातरनुवाकस्योपाकरणाज्जघनेन गार्हपत्यस्योदाङ्मुख उपविश्य स वासवं सामाभिगायति ॥ २.२४.३ ॥

*purā prātaranuvākasyopākaraṇājjaghanena gārhapatyasyodāṅmukha upaviśya sa vāsavaṃ sāmābhigāyati || 2.24.3 ||*

Before the morning chant begins, the sacrificer sits behind the Gārhapatya fire, facing north, and sings the Sāma about the Vasus.

### 2.24.4

लो३कद्वारमपावा३र्णू ३३ पश्येम त्वा वयं रा ३३३३३ हु ३ म् आ ३३ ज्या ३ यो ३ आ ३२१११ इति ॥ २.२४.४ ॥

*lo3kadvāramapāvā3rṇū 33 paśyema tvā vayaṃ rā 33333 hu 3 m ā 33 jyā 3 yo 3 ā 32111 iti || 2.24.4 ||*

O Fire, please open the door for us, that is, clear the path, so that we may see you and gain full control of the earth.

### 2.24.5

अथ जुहोति नमोऽग्नये पृथिवीक्षिते लोकक्षिते लोकं मे यजमानाय विन्दैष वै यजमानस्य लोक एतास्मि ॥ २.२४.५ ॥

*atha juhoti namo'gnaye pṛthivīkṣite lokakṣite lokaṃ me yajamānāya vindaiṣa vai yajamānasya loka etāsmi || 2.24.5 ||*

Then the sacrificer starts the offerings with this mantra: "O Agni, you are in this world. I salute you. Please obtain the right world for me, the one performing this sacrifice. I am ready to go to the world fitting for those who perform sacrifices."

### 2.24.6

अत्र यजमानः परस्तादायुषः स्वाहापजहि परिघमित्युक्त्वोत्तिष्ठति तस्मै वसवः प्रातःसवनं सम्प्रयच्छन्ति ॥ २.२४.६ ॥

*atra yajamānaḥ parastādāyuṣaḥ svāhāpajahi parighamityuktvottiṣṭhati tasmai vasavaḥ prātaḥsavanaṃ samprayacchanti || 2.24.6 ||*

"I, the yajamāna, have run the full course of my life in this world." Saying this, he will say "Svāhā" and make his oblation. Then he will stand, saying, "Please unbolt the door to the world for which I am destined." The Vasus then give him ownership of the earth, which is the fruit of the savana performed in the morning.

### 2.24.7

पुरा माध्यंदिनस्य सवनस्योपाकरणाज्जघनेनाग्नीध्रीयस्योदङ्मुख उपविश्य स रौद्रंसामाभिगायति ॥ २.२४.७ ॥

*purā mādhyaṃdinasya savanasyopākaraṇājjaghanenāgnīdhrīyasyodaṅmukha upaviśya sa raudraṃsāmābhigāyati || 2.24.7 ||*

Before the midday savana begins, the yajamāna sits behind the Dakṣiṇāgni fire, facing north. He then sings the Sāma addressed to the Rudras.

### 2.24.8

लो३कद्वारमपावा३र्णू३३ पश्येम त्वा वयं वैरा३३३३३ हु३म् आ३३ज्या ३यो३आ३२१११इति ॥ २.२४.८ ॥

*lo3kadvāramapāvā3rṇū33 paśyema tvā vayaṃ vairā33333 hu3m ā33jyā 3yo3ā32111iti || 2.24.8 ||*

O Fire, please open the door for us, that is, clear the path, so that we may see you and gain full control of the world of Virāṭ.

### 2.24.9

अथ जुहोति नमो वायवेऽन्तरिक्षक्षिते लोकक्षिते लोकं मे यजमानाय विन्दैष वै यजमानस्य लोक एतास्मि ॥ २.२४.९ ॥

*atha juhoti namo vāyave'ntarikṣakṣite lokakṣite lokaṃ me yajamānāya vindaiṣa vai yajamānasya loka etāsmi || 2.24.9 ||*

Then the sacrificer starts the offerings with this mantra: "O Vāyu, you are in the mid-region. I salute you. Please obtain the right world for me, the one performing this sacrifice. I am ready to go to the world fitting for those who perform sacrifices."

### 2.24.10

अत्र यजमानः परस्तादायुषः स्वाहापजहि परिघमित्युक्त्वोत्तिष्ठति तस्मै रुद्रा माध्यंदिनंसवनंसम्प्रयच्छन्ति ॥ २.२४.१० ॥

*atra yajamānaḥ parastādāyuṣaḥ svāhāpajahi parighamityuktvottiṣṭhati tasmai rudrā mādhyaṃdinaṃsavanaṃsamprayacchanti || 2.24.10 ||*

"I, the yajamāna, have run the full course of my life in this world." Saying this, he will say "Svāhā" and make his oblation. Then he will stand, saying, "Please unbolt the door to the world for which I am destined." The Rudras then give him ownership of the mid-region, which is the fruit of the savana performed at midday.

### 2.24.11

पुरा तृतीयसवनस्योपाकरणाज्जघनेनाहवनीयस्योदङ्मुख उपविश्य स आदित्यंस वैश्वदेवं सामाभिगायति ॥ २.२४.११ ॥

*purā tṛtīyasavanasyopākaraṇājjaghanenāhavanīyasyodaṅmukha upaviśya sa ādityaṃsa vaiśvadevaṃ sāmābhigāyati || 2.24.11 ||*

Before beginning the third, or evening, savana, the yajamāna sits behind the Āhavanīya fire and faces north. He then chants the Sāma addressed to the Ādityas and the Viśvadevas.

### 2.24.12

लो३कद्वारमपावा३र्णू३३पश्येम त्वा वयं स्वारा ३३३३३ हु३म् आ३३ ज्या३ यो३आ ३२१११ इति ॥ २.२४.१२ ॥

*lo3kadvāramapāvā3rṇū33paśyema tvā vayaṃ svārā 33333 hu3m ā33 jyā3 yo3ā 32111 iti || 2.24.12 ||*

Addressing the Ādityas: "O Fire, kindly open the door to the world, that is, heaven, for us, so that we may see you and gain sovereignty."

### 2.24.13

आदित्यमथ वैश्वदेवं लो३कद्वारमपावा३र्णू३३ पश्येम त्वा वयंसाम्रा३३३३३ हु३म् आ३३ ज्या३यो३आ ३२१११ इति ॥ २.२४.१३ ॥

*ādityamatha vaiśvadevaṃ lo3kadvāramapāvā3rṇū33 paśyema tvā vayaṃsāmrā33333 hu3m ā33 jyā3yo3ā 32111 iti || 2.24.13 ||*

The previous verse was directed to the Ādityas. Now the yajamāna turns to the Viśvadevas and says, "O Fire, kindly open the door to the world, that is, heaven, for us, so that we may see you and gain sovereignty."

### 2.24.14

अथ जुहोति नम आदित्येभ्यश्च विश्वेभ्यश्च देवेभ्यो दिविक्षिद्भ्यो लोकक्षिद्भ्यो लोकं मे यजमानाय विन्दत ॥ २.२४.१४ ॥

*atha juhoti nama ādityebhyaśca viśvebhyaśca devebhyo divikṣidbhyo lokakṣidbhyo lokaṃ me yajamānāya vindata || 2.24.14 ||*

The sacrificer then starts the offerings with this mantra: "Salutations to the Ādityas and the Viśvadevas, who dwell in heaven and the other worlds. May I gain the world of the yajamāna."

### 2.24.15

एष वै यजमानस्य लोक एतास्म्यत्र यजमानः परस्तादायुषः स्वाहापहत परिघमित्युक्त्वोत्तिष्ठति ॥ २.२४.१५ ॥

*eṣa vai yajamānasya loka etāsmyatra yajamānaḥ parastādāyuṣaḥ svāhāpahata parighamityuktvottiṣṭhati || 2.24.15 ||*

"I am prepared to go to a world fit for one who performs sacrifices. After my death I will live in this world." Uttering "Svāhā," he finishes the sacrifice. He then stands up and prays, "May the bolt be removed."

### 2.24.16

तस्मा आदित्याश्च विश्वे च देवास्तृतीयसवनं सम्प्रयच्छन्त्येष ह वै यज्ञस्य मात्रां वेद य एवं वेद य एवं वेद ॥ २.२४.१६ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्विंशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयोऽध्यायः ॥

*tasmā ādityāśca viśve ca devāstṛtīyasavanaṃ samprayacchantyeṣa ha vai yajñasya mātrāṃ veda ya evaṃ veda ya evaṃ veda || 2.24.16 || || iti caturviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti dvitīyo'dhyāyaḥ ||*

The Ādityas and the Viśvadevas then give the sacrificer the fruit of the third savana. Whoever knows this knows the real intent of the sacrifice.

### 3.1.1

॥ तृतीयोऽध्यायः ॥ असौ वा आदित्यो देवमधु तस्य द्यौरेव तिरश्चीनवंशोऽन्तरिक्षमपूपो मरीचयः पुत्राः ॥ ३.१.१ ॥

*|| tṛtīyo'dhyāyaḥ || asau vā ādityo devamadhu tasya dyaureva tiraścīnavaṃśo'ntarikṣamapūpo marīcayaḥ putrāḥ || 3.1.1 ||*

That sun up there is honey for the gods. Heaven is the crossbeam, the mid-region is the beehive, and the rays are the eggs.

### 3.1.2

तस्य ये प्राञ्चो रश्मयस्ता एवास्य प्राच्यो मधुनाड्यः । ऋच एव मधुकृत ऋग्वेद एव पुष्पं ता अमृता आपस्ता वा एता ऋचः ॥ ३.१.२ ॥

*tasya ye prāñco raśmayastā evāsya prācyo madhunāḍyaḥ | ṛca eva madhukṛta ṛgveda eva puṣpaṃ tā amṛtā āpastā vā etā ṛcaḥ || 3.1.2 ||*

The sun's eastern rays are the eastern honey-cells of the hive. The Ṛk mantras are the bees, and the Ṛg Veda is the flower. The water from the sacrifice, such as the soma juice and other things, is the flower's nectar. These Ṛk mantras—

### 3.1.3

एतमृग्वेदमभ्यतपंस्तस्याभितप्तस्य यशस्तेज इन्द्रियं वीर्यमन्नाद्यंरसोऽजायत ॥ ३.१.३ ॥

*etamṛgvedamabhyatapaṃstasyābhitaptasya yaśasteja indriyaṃ vīryamannādyaṃraso'jāyata || 3.1.3 ||*

—stimulated this Ṛg Veda. From the Ṛg Veda, thus stimulated, came fame, vitality, the power of the organs, energy, and the essence of food.

### 3.1.4

तद्व्यक्षरत्तदादित्यमभितोऽश्रयत्तद्वा एतद्यदेतदादित्यस्य रोहितंरूपम् ॥ ३.१.४ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*tadvyakṣarattadādityamabhito'śrayattadvā etadyadetadādityasya rohitaṃrūpam || 3.1.4 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All this, fame and the rest, flowed out to the sun and settled there. This is what gives the sun its red appearance.

### 3.2.1

अथ येऽस्य दक्षिणा रश्मयस्ता एवास्य दक्षिणा मधुनाड्यो यजूंष्येव मधुकृतो यजुर्वेद एव पुष्पं ता अमृत आपः ॥ ३.२.१ ॥

*atha ye'sya dakṣiṇā raśmayastā evāsya dakṣiṇā madhunāḍyo yajūṃṣyeva madhukṛto yajurveda eva puṣpaṃ tā amṛta āpaḥ || 3.2.1 ||*

The sun's southern rays, in turn, are the southern honey-cells of the hive. The Yajuh mantras are the bees, and the Yajur Veda is the flower. The water from the sacrifice, such as the soma juice and other things, is the flower's nectar.

### 3.2.2

तानि वा एतानि यजूंष्येतं यजुर्वेदमभ्यतपंस्तस्याभितप्तस्य यशस्तेज इन्द्रियं वीर्यमन्नाद्यंरसोजायत ॥ ३.२.२ ॥

*tāni vā etāni yajūṃṣyetaṃ yajurvedamabhyatapaṃstasyābhitaptasya yaśasteja indriyaṃ vīryamannādyaṃrasojāyata || 3.2.2 ||*

Those Yajuḥ mantras stimulated the Yajur Veda. From the Yajur Veda, thus stimulated, came fame, vitality, the power of the organs, energy, and the essence of food.

### 3.2.3

तद्व्यक्षरत्तदादित्यमभितोऽश्रयत्तद्वा एतद्यदेतदादित्यस्य शुक्लं रूपम् ॥ ३.२.३ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*tadvyakṣarattadādityamabhito'śrayattadvā etadyadetadādityasya śuklaṃ rūpam || 3.2.3 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All this, fame and the rest, flowed out to the sun and settled there. This is what gives the sun its white appearance.

### 3.3.1

अथ येऽस्य प्रत्यञ्चो रश्मयस्ता एवास्य प्रतीच्यो मधुनाड्यः सामान्येव मधुकृतः सामवेद एव पुष्पं ता अमृता आपः ॥ ३.३.१ ॥

*atha ye'sya pratyañco raśmayastā evāsya pratīcyo madhunāḍyaḥ sāmānyeva madhukṛtaḥ sāmaveda eva puṣpaṃ tā amṛtā āpaḥ || 3.3.1 ||*

The sun's western rays, in turn, are the western honey-cells of the hive. The Sāma mantras are the bees, and the Sāma Veda is the flower. The water from the sacrifice, such as the soma juice and other things, is the flower's nectar.

### 3.3.2

तानि वा एतानि सामान्येतं सामवेदमभ्यतपंस्तस्याभितप्तस्य यशस्तेज इन्द्रियं वीर्यमन्नाद्यंरसोऽजायत ॥ ३.३.२ ॥

*tāni vā etāni sāmānyetaṃ sāmavedamabhyatapaṃstasyābhitaptasya yaśasteja indriyaṃ vīryamannādyaṃraso'jāyata || 3.3.2 ||*

Those Sāma mantras stimulated the Sāma Veda. From the Sāma Veda, thus stimulated, came fame, vitality, the power of the organs, energy, and the essence of food.

### 3.3.3

तद्व्यक्षरत्तदादित्यमभितोऽश्रयत्तद्वा एतद्यदेतदादित्यस्य कृष्णंरूपम् ॥ ३.३.३ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*tadvyakṣarattadādityamabhito'śrayattadvā etadyadetadādityasya kṛṣṇaṃrūpam || 3.3.3 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All this, fame and the rest, flowed out to the sun and settled there. This is what produces the black spots in the sun.

### 3.4.1

अथ येऽस्योदञ्चो रश्मयस्ता एवास्योदीच्यो मधुनाड्योऽथर्वाङ्गिरस एव मधुकृत इतिहासपुराणं पुष्पं ता अमृता आपः ॥ ३.४.१ ॥

*atha ye'syodañco raśmayastā evāsyodīcyo madhunāḍyo'tharvāṅgirasa eva madhukṛta itihāsapurāṇaṃ puṣpaṃ tā amṛtā āpaḥ || 3.4.1 ||*

The sun's northern rays, in turn, are the northern honey-cells of the hive. The mantras seen by the sages Atharvā and Aṅgirā are the bees, and the Itihāsas and Purāṇas, that is, history and legends, are the flower. The water from the sacrifice, such as the soma juice and other things, is the flower's nectar.

### 3.4.2

ते वा एतेऽथर्वाङ्गिरस एतदितिहासपूराणमभ्यतपं स्तस्याभितप्तस्य यशस्तेज इन्द्रियां वीर्यमन्नाद्यंरसोऽजायत ॥ ३.४.२ ॥

*te vā ete'tharvāṅgirasa etaditihāsapūrāṇamabhyatapaṃ stasyābhitaptasya yaśasteja indriyāṃ vīryamannādyaṃraso'jāyata || 3.4.2 ||*

Those Atharvā-Aṅgirasā mantras stimulated the Itihāsas and Purāṇas, that is, the history and the legends. From these, thus stimulated, came fame, vitality, the power of the organs, energy, and the essence of food.

### 3.4.3

तद्व्यक्षरत्तदादित्यमभितोऽश्रयत्तद्वा एतद्यदेतदादित्यस्य परं कृष्णंरूपम् ॥ ३.४.३ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*tadvyakṣarattadādityamabhito'śrayattadvā etadyadetadādityasya paraṃ kṛṣṇaṃrūpam || 3.4.3 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All this, fame and the rest, flowed out to the sun and settled there. This is what produces the deep black spots in the sun.

### 3.5.1

अथ येऽस्योर्ध्वा रश्मयस्ता एवास्योर्ध्वा मधुनाड्यो गुह्या एवादेशा मधुकृतो ब्रह्मैव पुष्पं ता अमृता आपः ॥ ३.५.१ ॥

*atha ye'syordhvā raśmayastā evāsyordhvā madhunāḍyo guhyā evādeśā madhukṛto brahmaiva puṣpaṃ tā amṛtā āpaḥ || 3.5.1 ||*

Next, the sun's rays in the higher region are likewise its honey-cells of the higher region. The secret instructions are the bees, and Brahman (praṇava) is the flower. The water from the sacrifice is the nectar.

### 3.5.2

ते वा एते गुह्या आदेशा एतद्ब्रह्माभ्यतपं स्तस्याभितप्तस्य यशस्तेज इन्द्रियं वीर्यमन्नाद्यंरसोऽजायत ॥ ३.५.२ ॥

*te vā ete guhyā ādeśā etadbrahmābhyatapaṃ stasyābhitaptasya yaśasteja indriyaṃ vīryamannādyaṃraso'jāyata || 3.5.2 ||*

These same secret instructions stimulated Brahman in the form of praṇava. From this, thus stimulated, came fame, vitality, the power of the organs, energy, and the essence of food.

### 3.5.3

तद्व्यक्षरत्तदादित्यमभितोऽश्रयत्तद्वा एतद्यदेतदादित्यस्य मध्ये क्षोभत इव ॥ ३.५.३ ॥

*tadvyakṣarattadādityamabhito'śrayattadvā etadyadetadādityasya madhye kṣobhata iva || 3.5.3 ||*

All this, fame and the rest, flowed out to the sun and settled there. This is what appears to quiver within the sun.

### 3.5.4

ते वा एते रसानांरसा वेदा हि रसास्तेषामेते रसास्तानि वा एतान्यमृतानाममृतानि वेदा ह्यमृतास्तेषामेतान्यमृतानि ॥ ३.५.४ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*te vā ete rasānāṃrasā vedā hi rasāsteṣāmete rasāstāni vā etānyamṛtānāmamṛtāni vedā hyamṛtāsteṣāmetānyamṛtāni || 3.5.4 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

These colors, red and the others, are the essence of all essences. The Vedas are the essence, and the colors are the essence of the Vedas. These colors are the nectar of the nectar, the Vedas. The Vedas are the nectar, and so eternal, but the colors are the nectar of the Vedas.

### 3.6.1

तद्यत्प्रथमममृतं तद्वसव उपजीवन्त्यग्निना मुखेन न वै देवा अश्नन्ति न पिबन्त्येतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यन्ति ॥ ३.६.१ ॥

*tadyatprathamamamṛtaṃ tadvasava upajīvantyagninā mukhena na vai devā aśnanti na pibantyetadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyanti || 3.6.1 ||*

With fire at their head, the Vasus enjoy the first nectar, the red color. In fact, the gods and goddesses do not eat or drink. Simply seeing the nectar satisfies them.

### 3.6.2

त एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशन्त्येतस्माद्रूपादुद्यन्ति ॥ ३.६.२ ॥

*ta etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśantyetasmādrūpādudyanti || 3.6.2 ||*

They go into this red color of the sun, and they also emerge from it. That is, they gaze at this red color and are content, making no effort to obtain it; yet at times they are drawn to it and try to reach it.

### 3.6.3

स य एतदेवममृतं वेद वसूनामेवैको भूत्वाग्निनैव मुखेनैतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यति स य एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशत्येतस्माद्रूपादुदेति ॥ ३.६.३ ॥

*sa ya etadevamamṛtaṃ veda vasūnāmevaiko bhūtvāgninaiva mukhenaitadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyati sa ya etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśatyetasmādrūpādudeti || 3.6.3 ||*

Whoever knows this nectar in this way becomes one of the Vasus, since only the Vasus understand its meaning. With fire at his head, he then happily enjoys gazing at the nectar. He goes into this color and emerges from it again.

### 3.6.4

स यावदादित्यः पुरस्तादुदेता पश्चादस्तमेता वसूनामेव तावदाधिपत्यंस्वाराज्यं पर्येता ॥ ३.६.४ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*sa yāvadādityaḥ purastādudetā paścādastametā vasūnāmeva tāvadādhipatyaṃsvārājyaṃ paryetā || 3.6.4 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For as long as the sun rises in the east and sets in the west, that person will enjoy the sovereignty and freedom of the Vasus.

### 3.7.1

अथ यद्द्वितीयममृतं तद्रुद्रा उपजीवन्तीन्द्रेण मुखेन न वै देवा अश्नन्ति न पिबन्त्येतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यन्ति ॥ ३.७.१ ॥

*atha yaddvitīyamamṛtaṃ tadrudrā upajīvantīndreṇa mukhena na vai devā aśnanti na pibantyetadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyanti || 3.7.1 ||*

With Indra at their head, the Rudras enjoy the second nectar, the white color of the sun. In fact, the gods and goddesses do not eat or drink. Simply seeing the nectar satisfies them.

### 3.7.2

त एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशन्त्येतस्माद्रूपादुद्यन्ति ॥ ३.७.२ ॥

*ta etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśantyetasmādrūpādudyanti || 3.7.2 ||*

They go into this white color of the sun, and they also emerge from it.

### 3.7.3

स य एतदेवममृतं वेद रुद्राणामेवैको भूत्वेन्द्रेणैव मुखेनैतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यति स एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशत्येतस्माद्रूपादुदेति ॥ ३.७.३ ॥

*sa ya etadevamamṛtaṃ veda rudrāṇāmevaiko bhūtvendreṇaiva mukhenaitadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyati sa etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśatyetasmādrūpādudeti || 3.7.3 ||*

Whoever knows this nectar in this way becomes a Rudra himself. With Indra at his head, he gazes at the nectar and is happy. He goes into this color and emerges from it again.

### 3.7.4

स यावदादित्यः पुरस्तादुदेता पश्चादस्तमेता द्विस्तावद्दक्षिणत उदेतोत्तरतोऽस्तमेता रुद्राणामेव तावदाधिपत्यंस्वाराज्यं पर्येता ॥ ३.७.४ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yāvadādityaḥ purastādudetā paścādastametā dvistāvaddakṣiṇata udetottarato'stametā rudrāṇāmeva tāvadādhipatyaṃsvārājyaṃ paryetā || 3.7.4 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For twice as long as the sun rises in the east and sets in the west, the one who knows this will rise in the south and set in the north. That person will also gain sovereignty and freedom, as the Rudras have.

### 3.8.1

अथ यत्तृतीयममृतं तदादित्या उपजीवन्ति वरुणेन मुखेन न वै देवा अश्नन्ति न पिबन्त्येतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यन्ति ॥ ३.८.१ ॥

*atha yattṛtīyamamṛtaṃ tadādityā upajīvanti varuṇena mukhena na vai devā aśnanti na pibantyetadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyanti || 3.8.1 ||*

With Varuṇa at their head, the Ādityas enjoy the third nectar, the dark one. In fact, the gods and goddesses do not eat or drink. Simply seeing the nectar satisfies them.

### 3.8.2

त एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशन्त्येतस्माद्रूपादुद्यन्ति ॥ ३.८.२ ॥

*ta etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśantyetasmādrūpādudyanti || 3.8.2 ||*

They go into this dark color of the sun, and they also emerge from it.

### 3.8.3

स य एतदेवममृतं वेदादित्यानामेवैको भूत्वा वरुणेनैव मुखेनैतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यति स एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशत्येतस्माद्रूपादुदेति ॥ ३.८.३ ॥

*sa ya etadevamamṛtaṃ vedādityānāmevaiko bhūtvā varuṇenaiva mukhenaitadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyati sa etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśatyetasmādrūpādudeti || 3.8.3 ||*

Whoever knows this nectar in this way becomes one of the Ādityas. With Varuṇa at his head, he enjoys the nectar by gazing at it. He goes into this color and emerges from it again.

### 3.8.4

स यावदादित्यो दक्षिणत उदेतोत्तरतोऽस्तमेता द्विस्तावत्पश्चादुदेता पुरस्तादस्तमेतादित्यानामेव तावदाधिपत्यंस्वाराज्यं पर्येता ॥ ३.८.४ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yāvadādityo dakṣiṇata udetottarato'stametā dvistāvatpaścādudetā purastādastametādityānāmeva tāvadādhipatyaṃsvārājyaṃ paryetā || 3.8.4 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For twice as long as the sun rises in the south and sets in the north, the one who knows this will rise in the west and set in the east. That person will also gain sovereignty and freedom, as the Ādityas have.

### 3.9.1

अथ यच्चतुर्थममृतं तन्मरुत उपजीवन्ति सोमेन मुखेन न वै देवा अश्नन्ति न पिबन्त्येतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यन्ति ॥ ३.९.१ ॥

*atha yaccaturthamamṛtaṃ tanmaruta upajīvanti somena mukhena na vai devā aśnanti na pibantyetadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyanti || 3.9.1 ||*

With Soma at their head, the Maruts enjoy the fourth nectar, the deep black one. In fact, the gods and goddesses do not eat or drink. Simply seeing the nectar gives them enjoyment.

### 3.9.2

त एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशन्त्येतस्माद्रूपादुद्यन्ति ॥ ३.९.२ ॥

*ta etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśantyetasmādrūpādudyanti || 3.9.2 ||*

They go into this deep black color of the sun, and they also emerge from it.

### 3.9.3

स य एतदेवममृतं वेद मरुतामेवैको भूत्वा सोमेनैव मुखेनैतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यति स एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशत्येतस्माद्रूपादुदेति ॥ ३.९.३ ॥

*sa ya etadevamamṛtaṃ veda marutāmevaiko bhūtvā somenaiva mukhenaitadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyati sa etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśatyetasmādrūpādudeti || 3.9.3 ||*

Whoever knows this nectar in this way becomes one of the Maruts. With Soma at his head, he enjoys the nectar by gazing at it. He goes into this color and emerges from it again.

### 3.9.4

स यावदादित्यः पश्चादुदेता पुरस्तादस्तमेता द्विस्तावदुत्तरत उदेता दक्षिणतोऽस्तमेता मरुतामेव तावदाधिपत्य्ंस्वाराज्यं पर्येता ॥ ३.९.४ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yāvadādityaḥ paścādudetā purastādastametā dvistāvaduttarata udetā dakṣiṇato'stametā marutāmeva tāvadādhipatyṃsvārājyaṃ paryetā || 3.9.4 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For twice as long as the sun rises in the west and sets in the east, the one who knows this will rise in the north and set in the south. That person will also gain sovereignty and freedom, as the Maruts have.

### 3.10.1

अथ यत्पञ्चमममृतं तत्साध्या उपजीवन्ति ब्रह्मणा मुखेन न वै देवा अश्नन्ति न पिबन्त्येतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यन्ति ॥ ३.१०.१ ॥

*atha yatpañcamamamṛtaṃ tatsādhyā upajīvanti brahmaṇā mukhena na vai devā aśnanti na pibantyetadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyanti || 3.10.1 ||*

With Brahman, in the form of praṇava, Om, at their head, the Sādhyas enjoy the fifth nectar, the one that seems to tremble within the sun. In fact, the gods and goddesses do not eat or drink. Simply seeing the nectar gives them enjoyment.

### 3.10.2

त एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशन्त्येतस्माद्रूपादुद्यन्ति ॥ ३.१०.२ ॥

*ta etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśantyetasmādrūpādudyanti || 3.10.2 ||*

They go into this seemingly vibrating form of the sun, and they also emerge from it.

### 3.10.3

स य एतदेवममृतं वेद साध्यानामेवैको भूत्वा ब्रह्मणैव मुखेनैतदेवामृतं दृष्ट्वा तृप्यति स एतदेव रूपमभिसंविशत्येतस्माद्रूपादुदेति ॥ ३.१०.३ ॥

*sa ya etadevamamṛtaṃ veda sādhyānāmevaiko bhūtvā brahmaṇaiva mukhenaitadevāmṛtaṃ dṛṣṭvā tṛpyati sa etadeva rūpamabhisaṃviśatyetasmādrūpādudeti || 3.10.3 ||*

Whoever knows this nectar in this way becomes one of the Sādhyas. With Brahman, in the form of praṇava, at his head, he enjoys the nectar by gazing at it. He goes into this form of the sun and emerges from it again.

### 3.10.4

स यावदादित्य उत्तरत उदेता दक्षिणतोऽस्तमेता द्विस्तावदूर्ध्वं उदेतार्वागस्तमेता साध्यानामेव तावदाधिपत्यंस्वाराज्यं पर्येता ॥ ३.१०.४ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yāvadāditya uttarata udetā dakṣiṇato'stametā dvistāvadūrdhvaṃ udetārvāgastametā sādhyānāmeva tāvadādhipatyaṃsvārājyaṃ paryetā || 3.10.4 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

For twice as long as the sun rises in the north and sets in the south, the one who knows this will rise above and set below. That person will also gain sovereignty and freedom, as the Sādhyas have; that is, he can move up and down at will.

### 3.11.1

अथ तत ऊर्ध्व उदेत्य नैवोदेता नास्तमेतैकल एव मध्ये स्थाता तदेष श्लोकः ॥ ३.११.१ ॥

*atha tata ūrdhva udetya naivodetā nāstametaikala eva madhye sthātā tadeṣa ślokaḥ || 3.11.1 ||*

Next, once it has given all living beings the fruits of their work, the sun will be beyond such duties. It will neither rise nor set any more, but will remain alone in the middle. Here is a verse about this:

### 3.11.2

न वै तत्र न निम्लोच नोदियाय कदाचन । देवास्तेनाहंसत्येन मा विराधिषि ब्रह्मणेति ॥ ३.११.२ ॥

*na vai tatra na nimloca nodiyāya kadācana | devāstenāhaṃsatyena mā virādhiṣi brahmaṇeti || 3.11.2 ||*

In reply to a question: No, truly, in Brahmaloka the sun has never risen and never set. O gods, hear me and be my witnesses. What I say is true, and by this truth may nothing obstruct my realization of Brahman.

### 3.11.3

न ह वा अस्मा उदेति न निम्लोचति सकृद्दिवा हैवास्मै भवति य एतामेवं ब्रह्मोपनिषदं वेद ॥ ३.११.३ ॥

*na ha vā asmā udeti na nimlocati sakṛddivā haivāsmai bhavati ya etāmevaṃ brahmopaniṣadaṃ veda || 3.11.3 ||*

For the one who knows the secret teachings of Brahman, there is no sunrise or sunset, no day or night. For him it is always day, that is, light.

### 3.11.4

तद्धैतद्ब्रह्मा प्रजापतय उवाच प्रजापतिर्मनवे मनुः प्रजाभ्यस्तद्धैतदुद्दालकायारुणये ज्येष्ठाय पुत्राय पिता ब्रह्म प्रोवाच ॥ ३.११.४ ॥

*taddhaitadbrahmā prajāpataya uvāca prajāpatirmanave manuḥ prajābhyastaddhaitaduddālakāyāruṇaye jyeṣṭhāya putrāya pitā brahma provāca || 3.11.4 ||*

Brahmā first gave this teaching on honey to Prajāpati. Prajāpati then gave it to Manu, and Manu gave it to his children. Later the father Aruṇa taught this knowledge of Brahman to his eldest son, Uddālaka Āruṇi.

### 3.11.5

इदं वाव तज्ज्येष्ठाय पुत्राय पिता ब्रह्म प्रब्रूयात्प्रणाय्याय वान्तेवासिने ॥ ३.११.५ ॥

*idaṃ vāva tajjyeṣṭhāya putrāya pitā brahma prabrūyātpraṇāyyāya vāntevāsine || 3.11.5 ||*

A father will hand on this knowledge of Brahman to his eldest son or to a qualified resident student.

### 3.11.6

नान्यस्मै कस्मैचन यद्यप्यस्मा इमामद्भिः परिगृहीतां धनस्य पूर्णां दद्यादेतदेव ततो भूय इत्येतदेव ततो भूय इति ॥ ३.११.६ ॥ ॥ इति एकादशः खण्डः ॥

*nānyasmai kasmaicana yadyapyasmā imāmadbhiḥ parigṛhītāṃ dhanasya pūrṇāṃ dadyādetadeva tato bhūya ityetadeva tato bhūya iti || 3.11.6 || || iti ekādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

It must never be taught to anyone else, even if one is offered the entire world, full of riches and encircled by water. For this knowledge is worth more than that. This knowledge is surely worth more than that.

### 3.12.1

गायत्री वा ईदं सर्वं भूतं यदिदं किं च वाग्वै गायत्री वाग्वा इदं सर्वं भूतं गायति च त्रायते च ॥ ३.१२.१ ॥

*gāyatrī vā īdaṃ sarvaṃ bhūtaṃ yadidaṃ kiṃ ca vāgvai gāyatrī vāgvā idaṃ sarvaṃ bhūtaṃ gāyati ca trāyate ca || 3.12.1 ||*

Everything that exists in this world, whatever there is, is gāyatrī. Speech is gāyatrī, because speech names all things and also tells them not to be afraid.

### 3.12.2

या वै सा गायत्रीयं वाव सा येयं पृथिव्यस्यां हीदं सर्वं भूतं प्रतिष्ठितमेतामेव नातिशीयते ॥ ३.१२.२ ॥

*yā vai sā gāyatrīyaṃ vāva sā yeyaṃ pṛthivyasyāṃ hīdaṃ sarvaṃ bhūtaṃ pratiṣṭhitametāmeva nātiśīyate || 3.12.2 ||*

What is this gāyatrī is also this earth. For all things, moving or unmoving, are bound to this earth and cannot leave it.

### 3.12.3

या वै सा पृथिवीयं वाव सा यदिदमस्मिन्पुरुषे शरीरमस्मिन्हीमे प्राणाः प्रतिष्ठिता एतदेव नातिशीयन्ते ॥ ३.१२.३ ॥

*yā vai sā pṛthivīyaṃ vāva sā yadidamasminpuruṣe śarīramasminhīme prāṇāḥ pratiṣṭhitā etadeva nātiśīyante || 3.12.3 ||*

What is this earth is also this human body, since all the prāṇas are rooted in this body and cannot exist apart from it.

### 3.12.4

यद्वै तत्पुरुषे शरीरमिदं वाव तद्यदिदमस्मिन्नन्तः पुरुषे हृदयमस्मिन्हीमे प्राणाः प्रतिष्ठिता एतदेव नातिशीयन्ते ॥ ३.१२.४ ॥

*yadvai tatpuruṣe śarīramidaṃ vāva tadyadidamasminnantaḥ puruṣe hṛdayamasminhīme prāṇāḥ pratiṣṭhitā etadeva nātiśīyante || 3.12.4 ||*

What is in this human body is also in this human heart, since all these prāṇas are rooted in this heart and cannot exist apart from it.

### 3.12.5

सैषा चतुष्पदा षड्विधा गायत्री तदेतदृचाभ्यनूक्तम् ॥ ३.१२.५ ॥

*saiṣā catuṣpadā ṣaḍvidhā gāyatrī tadetadṛcābhyanūktam || 3.12.5 ||*

The gāyatrī has four quarters, and each is sixfold. A Ṛk mantra says this:

### 3.12.6

तावानस्य महिमा ततो ज्यायांश्च पूरुषः । पादोऽस्य सर्वा भूतानि त्रिपादस्यामृतं दिवीति ॥ ३.१२.६ ॥

*tāvānasya mahimā tato jyāyāṃśca pūruṣaḥ | pādo'sya sarvā bhūtāni tripādasyāmṛtaṃ divīti || 3.12.6 ||*

Such is its glory. Yet the glory of the puruṣa, that is, Brahman, who fills the whole world, is greater still. All creatures make up one quarter of him. The other three quarters are nectar in heaven.

### 3.12.7

यद्वै तद्ब्रह्मेतीदं वाव तद्योयं बहिर्धा पुरुषादाकाशो यो वै स बहिर्धा पुरुषादाकाशः ॥ ३.१२.७ ॥ अयं वाव स योऽयमन्तः पुरुष अकाशो यो वै सोऽन्तः पुरुष आकाशः ॥ ३.१२.८ ॥ अयं वाव स योऽयमन्तर्हृदय आकाशस्तदेतत्पूर्णमप्रवर्ति पूर्णमप्रवर्तिनींश्रियं लभते य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१२.९ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*yadvai tadbrahmetīdaṃ vāva tadyoyaṃ bahirdhā puruṣādākāśo yo vai sa bahirdhā puruṣādākāśaḥ || 3.12.7 || ayaṃ vāva sa yo'yamantaḥ puruṣa akāśo yo vai so'ntaḥ puruṣa ākāśaḥ || 3.12.8 || ayaṃ vāva sa yo'yamantarhṛdaya ākāśastadetatpūrṇamapravarti pūrṇamapravartinīṃśriyaṃ labhate ya evaṃ veda || 3.12.9 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

What is Brahman is also the space outside the body. What is the space outside the body is also the space inside the body. And what is the space inside the body is also the space within the heart. That treasure in the heart is complete and unchanging. Whoever knows this is always complete and does not undergo change.

### 3.13.1

तस्य ह वा एतस्य हृदयस्य पञ्च देवसुषयः स योऽस्य प्राङ्सुषिः स प्राणस्तच्चक्षुः स आदित्यस्तदेतत्तेजोऽन्नाद्यमित्युपासीत तेजस्व्यन्नादो भवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.१ ॥

*tasya ha vā etasya hṛdayasya pañca devasuṣayaḥ sa yo'sya prāṅsuṣiḥ sa prāṇastaccakṣuḥ sa ādityastadetattejo'nnādyamityupāsīta tejasvyannādo bhavati ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.1 ||*

The heart has five doors, guarded by the gods. The eastern door is prāṇa. It is also the eyes, and it is Āditya. Worship this as the source of brightness and food. Whoever knows this becomes bright and enjoys food.

### 3.13.2

अथ योऽस्य दक्षिणः सुषिः स व्यानस्तच्छ्रोत्रं स चन्द्रमास्तदेतच्छ्रीश्च यशश्चेत्युपासीत श्रीमान्यशस्वी भवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.२ ॥

*atha yo'sya dakṣiṇaḥ suṣiḥ sa vyānastacchrotraṃ sa candramāstadetacchrīśca yaśaścetyupāsīta śrīmānyaśasvī bhavati ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.2 ||*

Next, the heart's southern door is vyāna. It is also the ears, and it is the moon. Worship it as the source of prosperity and fame. Whoever knows this becomes prosperous and famous.

### 3.13.3

अथ योऽस्य प्रत्यङ्सुषिः सोऽपानः सा वाक्सोऽग्निस्तदेतद्ब्रह्मवर्चसमन्नाद्यमित्युपासीत ब्रह्मवर्चस्यन्नादो भवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.३ ॥

*atha yo'sya pratyaṅsuṣiḥ so'pānaḥ sā vākso'gnistadetadbrahmavarcasamannādyamityupāsīta brahmavarcasyannādo bhavati ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.3 ||*

Next, the heart's western door is apāna. It is also vāk, and it is fire. Worship this, Brahman in the form of apāna, as the radiance of Brahman and as food. Whoever knows this becomes radiant with the light of Brahman and a great eater of food.

### 3.13.4

अथ योऽस्योदङ्सुषिः स समानस्तन्मनः स पर्जन्यस्तदेतत्कीर्तिश्च व्युष्टिश्चेत्युपासीत कीर्तिमान्व्युष्टिमान्भवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.४ ॥

*atha yo'syodaṅsuṣiḥ sa samānastanmanaḥ sa parjanyastadetatkīrtiśca vyuṣṭiścetyupāsīta kīrtimānvyuṣṭimānbhavati ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.4 ||*

Next, the heart's northern door is samāna. It is also the mind, and it is the god of rain. Worship this, Brahman in the form of samāna, as fame and beauty. Whoever knows this becomes famous and beautiful.

### 3.13.5

अथ योऽस्योर्ध्वः सुषिः स उदानः स वायुः स आकाशस्तदेतदोजश्च महश्चेत्युपासीतौजस्वी महस्वान्भवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.५ ॥

*atha yo'syordhvaḥ suṣiḥ sa udānaḥ sa vāyuḥ sa ākāśastadetadojaśca mahaścetyupāsītaujasvī mahasvānbhavati ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.5 ||*

Next, the door at the top of the heart is udāna. It is also vāyu [air] and ākāśa [space]. Worship this [Brahman in the form of udāna] as strength and greatness. Whoever knows this becomes strong and great.

### 3.13.6

ते वा एते पञ्च ब्रह्मपुरुषाः स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य द्वारपाः स य एतानेवं पञ्च ब्रह्मपुरुषान्स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य द्वारपान्वेदास्य कुले वीरो जायते प्रतिपद्यते स्वर्गं लोकं य एतानेवं पञ्च ब्रह्मपुरुषान्स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य द्वारपान्वेद ॥ ३.१३.६ ॥

*te vā ete pañca brahmapuruṣāḥ svargasya lokasya dvārapāḥ sa ya etānevaṃ pañca brahmapuruṣānsvargasya lokasya dvārapānvedāsya kule vīro jāyate pratipadyate svargaṃ lokaṃ ya etānevaṃ pañca brahmapuruṣānsvargasya lokasya dvārapānveda || 3.13.6 ||*

These five prāṇas are themselves like Brahman, and they guard the gates of heaven. Anyone who regards them as Brahman and as the gatekeepers of heaven has a heroic child born into the family. And by knowing them in this way, as Brahman and as the gatekeepers of heaven, a person reaches heaven himself.

### 3.13.7

अथ यदतः परो दिवो ज्योतिर्दीप्यते विश्वतः पृष्ठेषु सर्वतः पृष्ठेष्वनुत्तमेषूत्तमेषु लोकेष्विदं वाव तद्यदिदमस्मिन्नन्तः पुरुषे ज्योतिः ॥ ३.१३.७ ॥

*atha yadataḥ paro divo jyotirdīpyate viśvataḥ pṛṣṭheṣu sarvataḥ pṛṣṭheṣvanuttameṣūttameṣu lokeṣvidaṃ vāva tadyadidamasminnantaḥ puruṣe jyotiḥ || 3.13.7 ||*

Then, beyond this heaven, above the world, beyond everything, in the highest world, with nothing higher than it, a light shines. That very light is the light within a human being.

### 3.13.8

तस्यैषा दृष्टिर्यत्रितदस्मिञ्छरीरे संस्पर्शेनोष्णिमानं विजानाति तस्यैषा श्रुतिर्यत्रैतत्कर्णावपिगृह्य निनदमिव नदथुरिवाग्नेरिव ज्वलत उपशृणोति तदेतद्दृष्टं च श्रुतं चेत्युपासीत चक्षुष्यः श्रुतो भवति य एवं वेद य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१३.८ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥

*tasyaiṣā dṛṣṭiryatritadasmiñcharīre saṃsparśenoṣṇimānaṃ vijānāti tasyaiṣā śrutiryatraitatkarṇāvapigṛhya ninadamiva nadathurivāgneriva jvalata upaśṛṇoti tadetaddṛṣṭaṃ ca śrutaṃ cetyupāsīta cakṣuṣyaḥ śruto bhavati ya evaṃ veda ya evaṃ veda || 3.13.8 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Here is the proof. Touch the body and you can feel its warmth. There is proof for the ears as well. Cover your ears and you will hear a sound like a moving chariot, a bellowing bullock, or a burning fire. One should meditate on that light in the body as something seen and heard. Whoever knows this becomes distinguished: people want to see him, and he is widely known.

### 3.14.1

सर्वं खल्विदं ब्रह्म तज्जलानिति शान्त उपासीत । अथ खलु क्रतुमयः पुरुषो यथाक्रतुरस्मिँल्लोके पुरुषो भवति तथेतः प्रेत्य भवति स क्रतुं कुर्वीत ॥ ३.१४.१ ॥

*sarvaṃ khalvidaṃ brahma tajjalāniti śānta upāsīta | atha khalu kratumayaḥ puruṣo yathākraturasmim̐lloke puruṣo bhavati tathetaḥ pretya bhavati sa kratuṃ kurvīta || 3.14.1 ||*

All this is Brahman. Everything arises from Brahman, returns to Brahman, and is held in being by Brahman. So one should meditate quietly on Brahman. Each person has a mind of his own. Whatever a person wills in this life, he becomes on leaving this world. Keep this in mind and meditate accordingly.

### 3.14.2

मनोमयः प्राणशरीरो भारूपः सत्यसंकल्प आकाशात्मा सर्वकर्मा सर्वकामः सर्वगन्धः सर्वरसः सर्वमिदमभ्यत्तोऽवाक्यनादरः ॥ ३.१४.२ ॥

*manomayaḥ prāṇaśarīro bhārūpaḥ satyasaṃkalpa ākāśātmā sarvakarmā sarvakāmaḥ sarvagandhaḥ sarvarasaḥ sarvamidamabhyatto'vākyanādaraḥ || 3.14.2 ||*

He is governed by the mind [that is, his mind decides what he should and should not do]. His body is subtle, and he is full of light. Whatever he wants, he always gets. His Self is as spotless as the sky. The whole world is his creation. [Desires are many, and] every one of those desires is his. All odors are his, and so are all tastes. He is present everywhere in the world. He has no sense organs, and he is free of desires.

### 3.14.3

एष म आत्मान्तर्हृदयेऽणीयान्व्रीहेर्वा यवाद्वा सर्षपाद्वा श्यामाकाद्वा श्यामाकतण्डुलाद्वैष म आत्मान्तर्हृदये ज्यायान्पृथिव्या ज्यायानन्तरिक्षाज्ज्यायान्दिवो ज्यायानेभ्यो लोकेभ्यः ॥ ३.१४.३ ॥

*eṣa ma ātmāntarhṛdaye'ṇīyānvrīhervā yavādvā sarṣapādvā śyāmākādvā śyāmākataṇḍulādvaiṣa ma ātmāntarhṛdaye jyāyānpṛthivyā jyāyānantarikṣājjyāyāndivo jyāyānebhyo lokebhyaḥ || 3.14.3 ||*

My Self within my heart is smaller than a grain of rice, a grain of barley, a mustard seed, or a grain of millet, smaller even than the kernel of a millet grain. That same Self in my heart is larger than the earth, larger than the mid-region, larger than heaven, larger even than all these worlds together.

### 3.14.4

सर्वकर्मा सर्वकामः सर्वगन्धः सर्वरसः सर्वमिदमभ्यात्तोऽवाक्यनादर एष म आत्मान्तर्हृदय एतद्ब्रह्मैतमितः प्रेत्याभिसंभवितास्मीति यस्य स्यादद्धा न विचिकित्सास्तीति ह स्माह शाण्डिल्यः शाण्डिल्यः ॥ ३.१४.४ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्दशः खण्डः ॥

*sarvakarmā sarvakāmaḥ sarvagandhaḥ sarvarasaḥ sarvamidamabhyātto'vākyanādara eṣa ma ātmāntarhṛdaya etadbrahmaitamitaḥ pretyābhisaṃbhavitāsmīti yasya syādaddhā na vicikitsāstīti ha smāha śāṇḍilyaḥ śāṇḍilyaḥ || 3.14.4 || || iti caturdaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

He is the sole creator. His desires are the desires of all, his odors the odors of all, his tastes the tastes of all. He is everywhere, has no sense organs, and is free of desires. He is my Self, and he is in my heart. He is none other than Brahman. On leaving this body I shall reach him. Whoever firmly believes this has no doubt in his mind. [He will surely attain Brahman.] This is what Śāṇḍilya said.

### 3.15.1

अन्तरिक्षोदरः कोशो भूमिबुध्नो न जीर्यति दिशो ह्यस्य स्रक्तयो द्यौरस्योत्तरं बिलं स एष कोशो वसुधानस्तस्मिन्विश्वमिदं श्रितम् ॥ ३.१५.१ ॥

*antarikṣodaraḥ kośo bhūmibudhno na jīryati diśo hyasya sraktayo dyaurasyottaraṃ bilaṃ sa eṣa kośo vasudhānastasminviśvamidaṃ śritam || 3.15.1 ||*

There is a chest whose interior is the mid-region and whose bottom is the earth. It never decays. The quarters are its sides, and heaven is the opening at its top. This chest holds treasures, for the whole universe rests inside it.

### 3.15.2

तस्य प्राची दिग्जुहूर्नाम सहमाना नाम दक्षिणा राज्ञी नाम प्रतीची सुभूता नामोदीची तासां वायुर्वत्सः स य एतमेवं वायुं दिशां वत्सं वेद न पुत्ररोदं रोदिति सोऽहमेतमेवं वायुं दिशां वत्सं वेद मा पुत्ररोदंरुदम् ॥ ३.१५.२ ॥

*tasya prācī digjuhūrnāma sahamānā nāma dakṣiṇā rājñī nāma pratīcī subhūtā nāmodīcī tāsāṃ vāyurvatsaḥ sa ya etamevaṃ vāyuṃ diśāṃ vatsaṃ veda na putrarodaṃ roditi so'hametamevaṃ vāyuṃ diśāṃ vatsaṃ veda mā putrarodaṃrudam || 3.15.2 ||*

The eastern quarter of the chest is named juhū, the southern sahamānā, the western rājñī, and the northern subhūtā. Vāyu [air] is the son of these quarters. Whoever knows that Vāyu is the child of the quarters does not have to mourn the loss of a child. 'I know that Vāyu is the child of the quarters. May I never have to mourn the loss of my child.'

### 3.15.3

अरिष्टं कोशं प्रपद्येऽमुनामुनामुना प्राणं प्रपद्येऽमुनामुनामुना भूः प्रपद्येऽमुनामुनामुना भुवः प्रपद्येऽमुनामुनामुना स्वः प्रपद्येऽमुनामुनामुना ॥ ३.१५.३ ॥

*ariṣṭaṃ kośaṃ prapadye'munāmunāmunā prāṇaṃ prapadye'munāmunāmunā bhūḥ prapadye'munāmunāmunā bhuvaḥ prapadye'munāmunāmunā svaḥ prapadye'munāmunāmunā || 3.15.3 ||*

For my child's life, I take refuge in that immortal kośa [the chest, representing the universe]. For my child's life, I take refuge in prāṇa [the vital breath]. For my child's life, I take refuge in bhūḥ [the earth]. For my child's life, I take refuge in bhuvaḥ [the mid-region]. For my child's life, I take refuge in svaḥ [heaven].

### 3.15.4

स यदवोचं प्राणं प्रपद्य इति प्राणो वा इदं सर्वं भूतं यदिदं किंच तमेव तत्प्रापत्सि ॥ ३.१५.४ ॥

*sa yadavocaṃ prāṇaṃ prapadya iti prāṇo vā idaṃ sarvaṃ bhūtaṃ yadidaṃ kiṃca tameva tatprāpatsi || 3.15.4 ||*

When I said, 'I take refuge in prāṇa,' I meant that prāṇa is all that is visible, whatever exists. So I have taken refuge in everything.

### 3.15.5

अथ यदवोचं भूः प्रपद्य इति पृथिवीं प्रपद्येऽन्तरिक्षं प्रपद्ये दिवं प्रपद्य इत्येव तदवोचम् ॥ ३.१५.५ ॥

*atha yadavocaṃ bhūḥ prapadya iti pṛthivīṃ prapadye'ntarikṣaṃ prapadye divaṃ prapadya ityeva tadavocam || 3.15.5 ||*

Then, by saying, 'I take refuge in bhūḥ,' I meant that I take refuge in the earth, in the mid-region, and in heaven.

### 3.15.6

अथ यदवोचं भुवः प्रपद्य इत्यग्निं प्रपद्ये वायुं प्रपद्य आदित्यं प्रपद्य इत्येव तदवोचम् ॥ ३.१५.६ ॥

*atha yadavocaṃ bhuvaḥ prapadya ityagniṃ prapadye vāyuṃ prapadya ādityaṃ prapadya ityeva tadavocam || 3.15.6 ||*

Then, by saying, 'I take refuge in bhuvaḥ,' I meant that I take refuge in fire, in air, and in the sun.

### 3.15.7

अथ यदवोचंस्वः प्रपद्य इत्यृग्वेदं प्रपद्ये यजुर्वेदं प्रपद्ये सामवेदं प्रपद्य इत्येव तदवोचं तदवोचम् ॥ ३.१५.७ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चदशः खण्डः ॥

*atha yadavocaṃsvaḥ prapadya ityṛgvedaṃ prapadye yajurvedaṃ prapadye sāmavedaṃ prapadya ityeva tadavocaṃ tadavocam || 3.15.7 || || iti pañcadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Then, by saying, 'I take refuge in svaḥ,' I meant that I take refuge in the Ṛg Veda, in the Yajur Veda, and in the Sāma Veda. This is what I meant.

### 3.16.1

पुरुषो वाव यज्ञस्तस्य यानि चतुर्विंशति वर्षाणि तत्प्रातःसवनं चतुर्विंशत्यक्षरा गायत्री गायत्रं प्रातःसवनं तदस्य वसवोऽन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वाव वसव एते हीदंसर्वं वासयन्ति ॥ ३.१६.१ ॥

*puruṣo vāva yajñastasya yāni caturviṃśati varṣāṇi tatprātaḥsavanaṃ caturviṃśatyakṣarā gāyatrī gāyatraṃ prātaḥsavanaṃ tadasya vasavo'nvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāva vasava ete hīdaṃsarvaṃ vāsayanti || 3.16.1 ||*

The human body is like a sacrifice, and its first twenty-four years are like the morning libation. The gāyatrī has twenty-four syllables, and the gāyatrī accompanies the morning libation. The Vasus dwell in this libation. The Vasus are the vital breaths and the sense organs, because the word vasu means those who live and who make others live.

### 3.16.2

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयात्प्राणा वसव इदं मे प्रातःसवनं माध्यंदिनंसवनमनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानां वसूनां मध्ये यज्ञो विलोप्सीयेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो ह भवति ॥ ३.१६.२ ॥

*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyātprāṇā vasava idaṃ me prātaḥsavanaṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃsavanamanusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāṃ vasūnāṃ madhye yajño vilopsīyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado ha bhavati || 3.16.2 ||*

If he falls ill during these first twenty-four years, he should say: 'O Prāṇas, O Vasus, please extend this first libation to the midday libation. As the sacrifice, may I not vanish among the Vasus, who are my prāṇas [who are like my life].' [If he prays in this way,] he is rid of his illness and recovers fully.

### 3.16.3

अथ यानि चतुश्चत्वारिंशद्वर्षाणि तन्माध्यंदिनं सवनं चतुश्चत्वारिंशदक्षरा त्रिष्टुप्त्रैष्टुभं माध्यंदिनंसवनं तदस्य रुद्रा अन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वाव रुद्रा एते हीदंसर्वंरोदयन्ति ॥ ३.१६.३ ॥

*atha yāni catuścatvāriṃśadvarṣāṇi tanmādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ catuścatvāriṃśadakṣarā triṣṭuptraiṣṭubhaṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃsavanaṃ tadasya rudrā anvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāva rudrā ete hīdaṃsarvaṃrodayanti || 3.16.3 ||*

The next forty-four years are like the midday libation. The triṣṭubh metre has forty-four syllables, and a hymn in the triṣṭubh metre accompanies the midday libation. The Rudras are linked with this libation. The prāṇas are called Rudras because they [are cruel and] make everyone in this world weep.

### 3.16.4

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयात्प्राणा रुद्रा इदं मे माध्यंदिनंसवनं तृतीयसवनमनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानांरुद्राणां मध्ये यज्ञो विलोप्सीयेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो ह भवति ॥ ३.१६.४ ॥

*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyātprāṇā rudrā idaṃ me mādhyaṃdinaṃsavanaṃ tṛtīyasavanamanusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāṃrudrāṇāṃ madhye yajño vilopsīyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado ha bhavati || 3.16.4 ||*

If he falls ill during these next forty-four years, he should say: 'O Prāṇas, O Rudras, please extend my midday libation and join it to the third libation. As the sacrifice, may I not vanish among the Rudras, who are my prāṇas [who are like my life].' [If he prays in this way,] he is rid of his illness and recovers fully.

### 3.16.5

अथ यान्यष्टाचत्वारिंशद्वर्षाणि तत्तृतीयसवनमष्टाचत्वारिंशदक्षरा जगती जागतं तृतीयसवनं तदस्यादित्या अन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वावादित्या एते हीदंसर्वमाददते ॥ ३.१६.५ ॥

*atha yānyaṣṭācatvāriṃśadvarṣāṇi tattṛtīyasavanamaṣṭācatvāriṃśadakṣarā jagatī jāgataṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ tadasyādityā anvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāvādityā ete hīdaṃsarvamādadate || 3.16.5 ||*

The next forty-eight years are the third libation. The jagatī metre has forty-eight syllables, and a hymn in the jagatī metre accompanies the third libation. The Ādityas are linked with this libation. The prāṇas are called Ādityas because they take in [ādā] all things.

### 3.16.6

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयात्प्राणा अदित्या इदं मे तृतीयसवनमायुरनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानामादित्यानां मध्ये यज्ञो विलोप्सीयेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो हैव भवति ॥ ३.१६.६ ॥

*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyātprāṇā adityā idaṃ me tṛtīyasavanamāyuranusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāmādityānāṃ madhye yajño vilopsīyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado haiva bhavati || 3.16.6 ||*

If he falls ill during the next forty-eight years, he should say: 'O Prāṇas, O Ādityas, please extend my evening libation to the end of my life. As the sacrifice, may I not vanish among the Ādityas, who are my prāṇas [who are like my life].' [If he prays in this way,] he is rid of his illness and recovers.

### 3.16.7

एतद्ध स्म वै तद्विद्वानाह महिदास ऐतरेयः स किं म एतदुपतपसि योऽहमनेन न प्रेष्यामीति स ह षोडशं वर्षशतमजीवत्प्र ह षोडशं वर्षशतं जीवति य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१६.७ ॥ ॥ इति षोडशः खण्डः ॥

*etaddha sma vai tadvidvānāha mahidāsa aitareyaḥ sa kiṃ ma etadupatapasi yo'hamanena na preṣyāmīti sa ha ṣoḍaśaṃ varṣaśatamajīvatpra ha ṣoḍaśaṃ varṣaśataṃ jīvati ya evaṃ veda || 3.16.7 || || iti ṣoḍaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Knowing this, Mahidāsa, the son of Itarā, said: 'O disease, why do you trouble me like this? Rest assured, I will not die [of this disease].' He lived for one hundred and sixteen years. Whoever knows this also lives that long.

### 3.17.1

स यदशिशिषति यत्पिपासति यन्न रमते ता अस्य दीक्षाः ॥ ३.१७.१ ॥

*sa yadaśiśiṣati yatpipāsati yanna ramate tā asya dīkṣāḥ || 3.17.1 ||*

That he wants to eat, wants to drink, and has no wish to indulge in sense pleasures: this is his initiation [this is how he must begin to practice self-restraint].

### 3.17.2

अथ यदश्नाति यत्पिबति यद्रमते तदुपसदैरेति ॥ ३.१७.२ ॥

*atha yadaśnāti yatpibati yadramate tadupasadaireti || 3.17.2 ||*

After this he will eat, drink, or enjoy pleasure as if observing upasad [when he may take only milk or water].

### 3.17.3

अथ यद्धसति यज्जक्षति यन्मैथुनं चरति स्तुतशस्त्रैरेव तदेति ॥ ३.१७.३ ॥

*atha yaddhasati yajjakṣati yanmaithunaṃ carati stutaśastraireva tadeti || 3.17.3 ||*

After this, when he laughs, eats, and even enjoys some sense pleasure, these stand for the sounds of the stutas and śastras.

### 3.17.4

अथ यत्तपो दानमार्जवमहिंसा सत्यवचनमिति ता अस्य दक्षिणाः ॥ ३.१७.४ ॥

*atha yattapo dānamārjavamahiṃsā satyavacanamiti tā asya dakṣiṇāḥ || 3.17.4 ||*

Next come austerity, charity, straightforwardness, nonviolence, and truthfulness. These are his dakṣiṇā.

### 3.17.5

तस्मादाहुः सोष्यत्यसोष्टेति पुनरुत्पादनमेवास्य तन्मरणमेवावभृथः ॥ ३.१७.५ ॥

*tasmādāhuḥ soṣyatyasoṣṭeti punarutpādanamevāsya tanmaraṇamevāvabhṛthaḥ || 3.17.5 ||*

That is why people say, 'He will give birth,' or 'He has given birth.' Either way it is a rebirth [when he begins to live his life as a sacrifice, that is his rebirth]. When death overtakes him, the sacrifice is concluded.

### 3.17.6

तद्धैतद्घोर् आङ्गिरसः कृष्णाय देवकीपुत्रायोक्त्वोवाचापिपास एव स बभूव सोऽन्तवेलायामेतत्त्रयं प्रतिपद्येताक्षितमस्यच्युतमसि प्राणसंशितमसीति तत्रैते द्वे ऋचौ भवतः ॥ ३.१७.६ ॥

*taddhaitadghor āṅgirasaḥ kṛṣṇāya devakīputrāyoktvovācāpipāsa eva sa babhūva so'ntavelāyāmetattrayaṃ pratipadyetākṣitamasyacyutamasi prāṇasaṃśitamasīti tatraite dve ṛcau bhavataḥ || 3.17.6 ||*

The sage Ghora, of the family of Aṅgirasa, taught this truth to Kṛṣṇa, the son of Devakī, and as a result Kṛṣṇa became free of all desires. Ghora then said: 'At the moment of death a person should repeat these three mantras: "You never decay, you never change, and you are the essence of life."' There are two Ṛk mantras on this:

### 3.17.7

आदित्प्रत्नस्य रेतसः ज्योतिः पश्यन्ति वासरम्; परो यदिध्यते दिवि ॥ ३.१७.७ ॥ उद्वयं तमसस्परि ज्योतिः पश्यन्त उत्तरंस्वः पश्यन्त उत्तरं देवं देवत्रा सूर्यमगन्म ज्योतिरुत्तममिति ज्योतिरुत्तममिति ॥ ३.१७.८ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तदशः खण्डः ॥

*āditpratnasya retasaḥ jyotiḥ paśyanti vāsaram; Paro yadidhyate divi || 3.17.7 || udvayaṃ tamasaspari jyotiḥ paśyanta uttaraṃsvaḥ paśyanta uttaraṃ devaṃ devatrā sūryamaganma jyotiruttamamiti jyotiruttamamiti || 3.17.8 || || iti saptadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

[The knowers of Brahman] see that the light shining in Para-Brahman is the seed of the world. Like daylight, this light pervades everything. It is eternal. It is that great light which causes the world.

### 3.18.1

मनो ब्रह्मेत्युपासीतेत्यध्यात्ममथाधिदैवतमाकाशो ब्रह्मेत्युभयमादिष्टं भवत्यध्यात्मं चाधिदैवतं च ॥ ३.१८.१ ॥

*mano brahmetyupāsītetyadhyātmamathādhidaivatamākāśo brahmetyubhayamādiṣṭaṃ bhavatyadhyātmaṃ cādhidaivataṃ ca || 3.18.1 ||*

The mind is Brahman: this form of worship is called adhyātma. Next is the one called adhidaivata: Space is Brahman. [That is, meditate on space as Brahman.] These two ways of meditating are advised, adhyātma and adhidaivata.

### 3.18.2

तदेतच्चतुष्पाद्ब्रह्म वाक्पादः प्राणः पादश्चक्षुः पादः श्रोत्रं पाद इत्यध्यात्ममथाधिदैवतमग्निः पादो वायुः पादा अदित्यः पादो दिशः पाद इत्युभयमेवादिष्टं भवत्यध्यात्मं चैवाधिदैवतं च ॥ ३.१८.२ ॥

*tadetaccatuṣpādbrahma vākpādaḥ prāṇaḥ pādaścakṣuḥ pādaḥ śrotraṃ pāda ityadhyātmamathādhidaivatamagniḥ pādo vāyuḥ pādā adityaḥ pādo diśaḥ pāda ityubhayamevādiṣṭaṃ bhavatyadhyātmaṃ caivādhidaivataṃ ca || 3.18.2 ||*

Brahman as the mind has four feet [or quarters]. The organ of speech is one foot, prāṇa another, the eyes the third, and the ears the fourth. This is the adhyātma [physical and mental] aspect of Brahman. Next is the adhidaivata aspect. Fire is one foot, air another, the sun the next, and the quarters the fourth. These two ways of meditating are advised, adhyātma and adhidaivata.

### 3.18.3

वागेव ब्रह्मणश्चतुर्थः पादः सोऽग्निना ज्योतिषा भाति च तपति च भाति च तपति च कीर्त्या यशसा ब्रह्मवर्चसेन य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१८.३ ॥

*vāgeva brahmaṇaścaturthaḥ pādaḥ so'gninā jyotiṣā bhāti ca tapati ca bhāti ca tapati ca kīrtyā yaśasā brahmavarcasena ya evaṃ veda || 3.18.3 ||*

Vāk is one of the four feet of Brahman [as the mind]. It shines with the light of fire and gives off heat as well. Whoever knows this shines and gives off warmth through good work, through fame, and through the radiance gained by a scholarly and disciplined life.

### 3.18.4

प्राण एव ब्रह्मणश्चतुर्थः पादः स वायुना ज्योतिषा भाति च तपति च् भाति च तपति च कीर्त्या यशसा ब्रह्मवर्चसेन य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१८.४ ॥

*prāṇa eva brahmaṇaścaturthaḥ pādaḥ sa vāyunā jyotiṣā bhāti ca tapati c bhāti ca tapati ca kīrtyā yaśasā brahmavarcasena ya evaṃ veda || 3.18.4 ||*

Prāṇa [the organ of smell] is one of the four feet of Brahman [as the mind]. It shines with the light of vāyu [air] and gives off heat as well. Whoever knows this shines and gives off warmth through good work, through fame, and through the radiance gained by a scholarly and disciplined life.

### 3.18.5

चक्षुरेव ब्रह्मणश्चतुर्थः पादः स आदित्येन ज्योतिषा भाति च तपति च भाति च तपति च कीर्त्या यशसा ब्रह्मवर्चसेन य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१८.५ ॥

*cakṣureva brahmaṇaścaturthaḥ pādaḥ sa ādityena jyotiṣā bhāti ca tapati ca bhāti ca tapati ca kīrtyā yaśasā brahmavarcasena ya evaṃ veda || 3.18.5 ||*

The organ of sight is one of the four feet of Brahman [as the mind]. It shines with the light of the sun and gives off heat as well. Whoever knows this shines and gives off warmth through good work, through fame, and through the radiance gained by a scholarly and disciplined life.

### 3.18.6

श्रोत्रमेव ब्रह्मणश्चतुर्थः पादः स दिग्भिर्ज्योतिषा भाति च तपति च भाति च तपति च कीर्त्या यशसा ब्रह्मवर्चसेन य एवं वेद य एवं वेद ॥ ३.१८.६ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टादशः खण्डः ॥

*śrotrameva brahmaṇaścaturthaḥ pādaḥ sa digbhirjyotiṣā bhāti ca tapati ca bhāti ca tapati ca kīrtyā yaśasā brahmavarcasena ya evaṃ veda ya evaṃ veda || 3.18.6 || || iti aṣṭādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

The organ of hearing is one of the four feet of Brahman [as the mind]. It shines with the light of the quarters and gives off heat as well. Whoever knows this shines and gives off warmth through good work, through fame, and through the radiance gained by a scholarly and disciplined life.

### 3.19.1

आदित्यो ब्रह्मेत्यादेशस्तस्योपव्याख्यानमसदेवेदमग्र आसीत् । तत्सदासीत्तत्समभवत्तदाण्डं निरवर्तत तत्संवत्सरस्य मात्रामशयत तन्निरभिद्यत ते आण्डकपाले रजतं च सुवर्णं चाभवताम् ॥ ३.१९.१ ॥

*ādityo brahmetyādeśastasyopavyākhyānamasadevedamagra āsīt | tatsadāsīttatsamabhavattadāṇḍaṃ niravartata tatsaṃvatsarasya mātrāmaśayata tannirabhidyata te āṇḍakapāle rajataṃ ca suvarṇaṃ cābhavatām || 3.19.1 ||*

It has been said, 'Āditya is Brahman.' This is now explained. At first this universe was non-existent, having no names or forms. [It could not be seen, but it existed in a subtle form.] Gradually it took shape, as a shoot emerges from a seed. Then it grew into an egg and stayed that way for a full year. After that it split in two, one half turning to silver and the other to gold.

### 3.19.2

तद्यद्रजतं सेयं पृथिवी यत्सुवर्णं सा द्यौर्यज्जरायु ते पर्वता यदुल्बं समेघो नीहारो या धमनयस्ता नद्यो यद्वास्तेयमुदकं स समुद्रः ॥ ३.१९.२ ॥

*tadyadrajataṃ seyaṃ pṛthivī yatsuvarṇaṃ sā dyauryajjarāyu te parvatā yadulbaṃ samegho nīhāro yā dhamanayastā nadyo yadvāsteyamudakaṃ sa samudraḥ || 3.19.2 ||*

Of the egg's two parts, the silver one is this earth and the golden one is heaven. The thick membranes are the mountains, and the thin membranes are the clouds and mist. The veins are the rivers, and the fluid in the bladder is the ocean.

### 3.19.3

अथ यत्तदजायत सोऽसावादित्यस्तं जायमानं घोषा उलूलवोऽनूदतिष्ठन्त्सर्वाणि च भूतानि सर्वे च कामास्तस्मात्तस्योदयं प्रति प्रत्यायनं प्रति घोषा उलूलवोऽनूत्तिष्ठन्ति सर्वाणि च भूतानि सर्वे च कामाः ॥ ३.१९.३ ॥

*atha yattadajāyata so'sāvādityastaṃ jāyamānaṃ ghoṣā ulūlavo'nūdatiṣṭhantsarvāṇi ca bhūtāni sarve ca kāmāstasmāttasyodayaṃ prati pratyāyanaṃ prati ghoṣā ulūlavo'nūttiṣṭhanti sarvāṇi ca bhūtāni sarve ca kāmāḥ || 3.19.3 ||*

What was then born was the sun. All beings greeted its appearance with joyful sounds, and many desirable things came into being. Ever since, both sunrise and sunset have been marked by joyful sounds from all beings, and many enjoyable things appear at those times too.

### 3.19.4

स य एतमेवं विद्वानादित्यं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽभ्याशो ह यदेनं साधवो घोषा आ च गच्छेयुरुप च निम्रेडेरन्निम्रेडेरन् ॥ ३.१९.४ ॥ ॥ इति एकोनविंशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयोऽध्यायः ॥

*sa ya etamevaṃ vidvānādityaṃ brahmetyupāste'bhyāśo ha yadenaṃ sādhavo ghoṣā ā ca gaccheyurupa ca nimreḍerannimreḍeran || 3.19.4 || || iti ekonaviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti tṛtīyo'dhyāyaḥ ||*

Whoever knows the sun as Brahman and worships it in this way soon hears sounds pleasing to the ear and has many good things to enjoy.

### 4.1.1

॥ चतुर्थोऽध्यायः ॥ जानश्रुतिर्ह पौत्रायणः श्रद्धादेयो बहुदायी बहुपाक्य आस स ह सर्वत आवसथान्मापयांचक्रे सर्वत एव मेऽन्नमत्स्यन्तीति ॥ ४.१.१ ॥

*|| caturtho'dhyāyaḥ || jānaśrutirha pautrāyaṇaḥ śraddhādeyo bahudāyī bahupākya āsa sa ha sarvata āvasathānmāpayāṃcakre sarvata eva me'nnamatsyantīti || 4.1.1 ||*

Long ago there lived a king who was the great-grandson of Janaśruta. He was very charitable, giving many gifts and always with proper respect. He also had great quantities of food cooked for people. Thinking, 'People everywhere will eat my food,' he had many rest-houses built in different places.

### 4.1.2

अथ हंसा निशायामतिपेतुस्तद्धैवं हं सोहं समभ्युवाद हो होऽयि भल्लाक्ष भल्लाक्ष जानश्रुतेः पौत्रायणस्य समं दिवा ज्योतिराततं तन्मा प्रसाङ्क्षी स्तत्त्वा मा प्रधाक्षीरिति ॥ ४.१.२ ॥

*atha haṃsā niśāyāmatipetustaddhaivaṃ haṃ sohaṃ samabhyuvāda ho ho'yi bhallākṣa bhallākṣa jānaśruteḥ pautrāyaṇasya samaṃ divā jyotirātataṃ tanmā prasāṅkṣī stattvā mā pradhākṣīriti || 4.1.2 ||*

One night he saw some swans flying overhead. The swan at the back called to the one in front: 'Hey, you short-sighted one! Can't you see that the brightness of Jānaśruti has spread across the sky like daylight? Be careful not to touch it. Make sure it doesn't burn you.'

### 4.1.3

तमु ह परः प्रत्युवाच कम्वर एनमेतत्सन्तं सयुग्वानमिव रैक्वमात्थेति यो नु कथं सयुग्वा रैक्व इति ॥ ४.१.३ ॥

*tamu ha paraḥ pratyuvāca kamvara enametatsantaṃ sayugvānamiva raikvamāttheti yo nu kathaṃ sayugvā raikva iti || 4.1.3 ||*

The swan in front answered: 'Say, who is this person? To hear you talk, one would think he was Raikva with the cart.' The other swan then asked, 'And who is this Raikva with the cart that you mention?'

### 4.1.4

यथा कृतायविजितायाधरेयाः संयन्त्येवमेनं सर्वं तदभिसमैति यत्किंच प्रजाः साधु कुर्वन्ति यस्तद्वेद यत्स वेद स मयैतदुक्त इति ॥ ४.१.४ ॥

*yathā kṛtāyavijitāyādhareyāḥ saṃyantyevamenaṃ sarvaṃ tadabhisamaiti yatkiṃca prajāḥ sādhu kurvanti yastadveda yatsa veda sa mayaitadukta iti || 4.1.4 ||*

'In a game of dice, whoever wins the toss called kṛta automatically wins the lower tosses too. In the same way, all the good work that people do goes to Raikva's credit. Whoever knows what Raikva knows becomes like Raikva. That is how I would describe Raikva.'

### 4.1.5

तदु ह जानश्रुतिः पौत्रायण उपशुश्राव स ह संजिहान एव क्षत्तारमुवाचाङ्गारे ह सयुग्वानमिव रैक्वमात्थेति यो नु कथं सयुग्वा रैक्व इति ॥ ४.१.५ ॥ यथा कृतायविजितायाधरेयाः संयन्त्येवमेनं सर्वं तदभिसमैति यत्किंच प्रजाः साधु कुर्वन्ति यस्तद्वेद यत्स वेद स मयैतदुक्त इति ॥ ४.१.६ ॥

*tadu ha jānaśrutiḥ pautrāyaṇa upaśuśrāva sa ha saṃjihāna eva kṣattāramuvācāṅgāre ha sayugvānamiva raikvamāttheti yo nu kathaṃ sayugvā raikva iti || 4.1.5 || yathā kṛtāyavijitāyādhareyāḥ saṃyantyevamenaṃ sarvaṃ tadabhisamaiti yatkiṃca prajāḥ sādhu kurvanti yastadveda yatsa veda sa mayaitadukta iti || 4.1.6 ||*

Jānaśruti Pautrāyaṇa overheard what the swan said. On getting out of bed, the first thing he did was ask his attendant [who stood nearby]: 'My child, can I be compared with Raikva with the cart? Who is this Raikva with the cart? What kind of person is he?' [He then repeated the swan's words:] 'In a game of dice, whoever wins the toss called kṛta automatically wins the lower tosses too. In the same way, all the good work that people do goes to Raikva's credit. Whoever knows what Raikva knows becomes like Raikva. That is how I would describe Raikva.'

### 4.1.7

स ह क्षत्तान्विष्य नाविदमिति प्रत्येयाय तं होवाच यत्रारे ब्राह्मणस्यान्वेषणा तदेनमर्च्छेति ॥ ४.१.७ ॥

*sa ha kṣattānviṣya nāvidamiti pratyeyāya taṃ hovāca yatrāre brāhmaṇasyānveṣaṇā tadenamarccheti || 4.1.7 ||*

[Jānaśruti told his attendant to go and search for Raikva.] After searching, the attendant thought, 'I can't find him,' and came back to his master. Jānaśruti then told him: 'Well, why not go where brāhmins are found, in forests and solitary places? Search for him there.'

### 4.1.8

सोऽधस्ताच्छकटस्य पामानं कषमाणमुपोपविवेश तं हाभ्युवाद त्वं नु भगवः सयुग्वा रैक्व इत्यहं ह्यरा३ इति ह प्रतिजज्ञे स ह क्षत्ताविदमिति प्रत्येयाय ॥ ४.१.८ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*so'dhastācchakaṭasya pāmānaṃ kaṣamāṇamupopaviveśa taṃ hābhyuvāda tvaṃ nu bhagavaḥ sayugvā raikva ityahaṃ hyarā3 iti ha pratijajñe sa ha kṣattāvidamiti pratyeyāya || 4.1.8 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

The attendant noticed a man sitting under a cart, scratching a rash on his body. He sat down close by and asked, 'Sir, are you Raikva with the cart?' The man replied, 'Well, yes, I am.' Thinking, 'I have found him,' the attendant went back.

### 4.2.1

तदु ह जानश्रुतिः पौत्रायणः षट्शतानि गवां निष्कमश्वतरीरथं तदादाय प्रतिचक्रमे तं हाभ्युवाद ॥ ४.२.१ ॥

*tadu ha jānaśrutiḥ pautrāyaṇaḥ ṣaṭśatāni gavāṃ niṣkamaśvatarīrathaṃ tadādāya praticakrame taṃ hābhyuvāda || 4.2.1 ||*

Jānaśruti Pautrāyaṇa then set out for that place with six hundred cows, a gold necklace, and a mule-drawn chariot, and spoke to him [Raikva]:

### 4.2.2

रैक्वेमानि षट्शतानि गवामयं निष्कोऽयमश्वतरीरथोऽनु म एतां भगवो देवतां शाधि यां देवतामुपास्स इति ॥ ४.२.२ ॥

*raikvemāni ṣaṭśatāni gavāmayaṃ niṣko'yamaśvatarīratho'nu ma etāṃ bhagavo devatāṃ śādhi yāṃ devatāmupāssa iti || 4.2.2 ||*

'O Raikva, all of these are for you: six hundred cows, a gold necklace, and a chariot drawn by mules. Please tell me about the god you worship.'

### 4.2.3

तमु ह परः प्रत्युवाचाह हारेत्वा शूद्र तवैव सह गोभिरस्त्विति तदु ह पुनरेव जानश्रुतिः पौत्रायणः सहस्रं गवां निष्कमश्वतरीरथं दुहितरं तदादाय प्रतिचक्रमे ॥ ४.२.३ ॥

*tamu ha paraḥ pratyuvācāha hāretvā śūdra tavaiva saha gobhirastviti tadu ha punareva jānaśrutiḥ pautrāyaṇaḥ sahasraṃ gavāṃ niṣkamaśvatarīrathaṃ duhitaraṃ tadādāya praticakrame || 4.2.3 ||*

Raikva said to him, 'You śūdra, keep the necklace and chariot, along with the cows.' Jānaśruti went away and came back again, this time with one thousand cows, a gold necklace, a chariot drawn by mules, and his own daughter.

### 4.2.4

तं हाभ्युवाद रैक्वेदं सहस्रं गवामयं निष्कोऽयमश्वतरीरथ इयं जायायं ग्रामो यस्मिन्नास्सेऽन्वेव मा भगवः शाधीति ॥ ४.२.४ ॥

*taṃ hābhyuvāda raikvedaṃ sahasraṃ gavāmayaṃ niṣko'yamaśvatarīratha iyaṃ jāyāyaṃ grāmo yasminnāsse'nveva mā bhagavaḥ śādhīti || 4.2.4 ||*

He said to Raikva: 'I give you these thousand cows, this gold necklace, this chariot drawn by mules, this daughter of mine as your wife, and also this village where you live. Now, sir, please teach me.'

### 4.2.5

तस्या ह मुखमुपोद्गृह्णन्नुवाचाजहारेमाः शूद्रानेनैव मुखेनालापयिष्यथा इति ते हैते रैक्वपर्णा नाम महावृषेषु यत्रास्मा उवास स तस्मै होवाच ॥ ४.२.५ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*tasyā ha mukhamupodgṛhṇannuvācājahāremāḥ śūdrānenaiva mukhenālāpayiṣyathā iti te haite raikvaparṇā nāma mahāvṛṣeṣu yatrāsmā uvāsa sa tasmai hovāca || 4.2.5 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Lifting the princess's face, Raikva said: 'You have brought me many things [but they do not make me speak]. It is this face that makes me speak.' From then on, the villages in the Mahāvṛṣa province where Raikva lived were known as Raikvaparṇā. It was there that Raikva taught Jānaśruti. Raikva said to him:

### 4.3.1

वायुर्वाव संवर्गो यदा वा अग्निरुद्वायति वायुमेवाप्येति यदा सूर्योऽस्तमेति वायुमेवाप्येति यदा चन्द्रोऽस्तमेति वायुमेवाप्येति ॥ ४.३.१ ॥

*vāyurvāva saṃvargo yadā vā agnirudvāyati vāyumevāpyeti yadā sūryo'stameti vāyumevāpyeti yadā candro'stameti vāyumevāpyeti || 4.3.1 ||*

Air swallows everything. When fire goes out, it vanishes into the air. When the sun sets, it vanishes into the air. And when the moon sets, it vanishes into the air.

### 4.3.2

यदाप उच्छुष्यन्ति वायुमेवापियन्ति वायुर्ह्येवैतान्सर्वान्संवृङ्क्त इत्यधिदैवतम् ॥ ४.३.२ ॥

*yadāpa ucchuṣyanti vāyumevāpiyanti vāyurhyevaitānsarvānsaṃvṛṅkta ityadhidaivatam || 4.3.2 ||*

When water dries up, it vanishes into the air. Air swallows all of these. This is the worship of the forces of nature (adhidaivata).

### 4.3.3

अथाध्यात्मं प्राणो वाव संवर्गः स यदा स्वपिति प्राणमेव वागप्येति प्राणं चक्षुः प्राणं श्रोत्रं प्राणं मनः प्राणो ह्येवैतान्सर्वान्संवृङ्क्त इति ॥ ४.३.३ ॥

*athādhyātmaṃ prāṇo vāva saṃvargaḥ sa yadā svapiti prāṇameva vāgapyeti prāṇaṃ cakṣuḥ prāṇaṃ śrotraṃ prāṇaṃ manaḥ prāṇo hyevaitānsarvānsaṃvṛṅkta iti || 4.3.3 ||*

Next is the worship relating to the body. Prāṇa swallows everything. When a person sleeps, speech, the eyes, the ears, and the mind all go into prana. Prāṇa swallows all of these.

### 4.3.4

तौ वा एतौ द्वौ संवर्गौ वायुरेव देवेषु प्राणः प्राणेषु ॥ ४.३.४ ॥

*tau vā etau dvau saṃvargau vāyureva deveṣu prāṇaḥ prāṇeṣu || 4.3.4 ||*

Everything is swallowed by these two: among the gods by air, and among the organs by prāṇa.

### 4.3.5

अथ ह शौनकं च कापेयमभिप्रतारिणं च काक्षसेनिं परिविष्यमाणौ ब्रह्मचारी बिभिक्षे तस्मा उ ह न ददतुः ॥ ४.३.५ ॥

*atha ha śaunakaṃ ca kāpeyamabhipratāriṇaṃ ca kākṣaseniṃ pariviṣyamāṇau brahmacārī bibhikṣe tasmā u ha na dadatuḥ || 4.3.5 ||*

Once, as Śaunaka, the son of Kapi, and Abhipratārin, the son of Kakṣasena, were being served their meal, a brahmacārin came and begged for food. But they refused to give him any.

### 4.3.6

स होवाच महात्मनश्चतुरो देव एकः कः स जगार भुवनस्य गोपास्तं कापेय नाभिपश्यन्ति मर्त्या अभिप्रतारिन्बहुधा वसन्तं यस्मै वा एतदन्नं तस्मा एतन्न दत्तमिति ॥ ४.३.६ ॥

*sa hovāca mahātmanaścaturo deva ekaḥ kaḥ sa jagāra bhuvanasya gopāstaṃ kāpeya nābhipaśyanti martyā abhipratārinbahudhā vasantaṃ yasmai vā etadannaṃ tasmā etanna dattamiti || 4.3.6 ||*

The brahmacārin said: 'One god has swallowed four sages. Who is it that protects this world? O Kāpeya, O Abhipratārin, that god exists in many forms, but human beings cannot see him. This food is meant for him, yet you are not giving it to him.'

### 4.3.7

तदु ह शौनकः कापेयः प्रतिमन्वानः प्रत्येयायात्मा देवानां जनिता प्रजानां हिरण्यदंष्ट्रो बभसोऽनसूरिर्महान्तमस्य महिमानमाहुरनद्यमानो यदनन्नमत्तीति वै वयं ब्रह्मचारिन्नेदमुपास्महे दत्तास्मै भिक्षामिति ॥ ४.३.७ ॥

*tadu ha śaunakaḥ kāpeyaḥ pratimanvānaḥ pratyeyāyātmā devānāṃ janitā prajānāṃ hiraṇyadaṃṣṭro babhaso'nasūrirmahāntamasya mahimānamāhuranadyamāno yadanannamattīti vai vayaṃ brahmacārinnedamupāsmahe dattāsmai bhikṣāmiti || 4.3.7 ||*

After reflecting on this, Śaunaka Kāpeya went to the brahmacārin and said, 'He is the self of all the gods and goddesses and the creator of all things moving and unmoving. He eats with golden [firm] teeth. He is intelligent. Others cannot eat him, and he eats what is not food. Wise people think highly of his greatness. O brahmacārin, we worship him.' He then said, 'Give this man alms.'

### 4.3.8

तस्म उ ह ददुस्ते वा एते पञ्चान्ये पञ्चान्ये दश सन्तस्तत्कृतं तस्मात्सर्वासु दिक्ष्वन्नमेव दश कृतं सैषा विराडन्नादी तयेदं सर्वं दृष्टं सर्वमस्येदं दृष्टं भवत्यन्नादो भवति य एवं वेद य एवं वेद ॥ ४.३.८ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*tasma u ha daduste vā ete pañcānye pañcānye daśa santastatkṛtaṃ tasmātsarvāsu dikṣvannameva daśa kṛtaṃ saiṣā virāḍannādī tayedaṃ sarvaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ sarvamasyedaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ bhavatyannādo bhavati ya evaṃ veda ya evaṃ veda || 4.3.8 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

So they gave him alms. The first five [Vāyu and the rest] and the second five [prāṇa and the rest] add up to ten. That is kṛta [the highest throw of dice]. These ten are the ten directions, and they are the food. This kṛta is Virāṭ. Since Virāṭ pervades all, everything is its food. Through that Virāṭ all this is seen. Whoever knows this Virāṭ becomes Virāṭ himself, pervading all, with everything as his food.

### 4.4.1

सत्यकामो ह जाबालो जबालां मातरमामन्त्रयांचक्रे ब्रह्मचर्यं भवति विवत्स्यामि किंगोत्रो न्वहमस्मीति ॥ ४.४.१ ॥

*satyakāmo ha jābālo jabālāṃ mātaramāmantrayāṃcakre brahmacaryaṃ bhavati vivatsyāmi kiṃgotro nvahamasmīti || 4.4.1 ||*

Satyakāma Jābāla once said to his mother Jabālā: 'Revered mother, I want to live with a teacher as a celibate student. What is my lineage?'

### 4.4.2

सा हैनमुवाच नाहमेतद्वेद तात यद्गोत्रस्त्वमसि बह्वहं चरन्ती परिचारिणी यौवने त्वामलभे साहमेतन्न वेद यद्गोत्रस्त्वमसि जबाला तु नामाहमस्मि सत्यकामो नाम त्वमसि स सत्यकाम एव जाबालो ब्रवीथा इति ॥ ४.४.२ ॥

*sā hainamuvāca nāhametadveda tāta yadgotrastvamasi bahvahaṃ carantī paricāriṇī yauvane tvāmalabhe sāhametanna veda yadgotrastvamasi jabālā tu nāmāhamasmi satyakāmo nāma tvamasi sa satyakāma eva jābālo bravīthā iti || 4.4.2 ||*

Jabālā answered him: 'My son, your lineage is unknown to me. In my youth I had you while I was kept busy serving many people, and so I can tell you nothing about it. I am called Jabālā and you are called Satyakāma. If anyone asks about your lineage, say, "I am Satyakāma Jābāla."'

### 4.4.3

स ह हारिद्रुमतं गौतममेत्योवाच ब्रह्मचर्यं भगवति वत्स्याम्युपेयां भगवन्तमिति ॥ ४.४.३ ॥

*sa ha hāridrumataṃ gautamametyovāca brahmacaryaṃ bhagavati vatsyāmyupeyāṃ bhagavantamiti || 4.4.3 ||*

Satyakāma approached Gautama, son of Haridrumata, and said: 'Revered sir, I want to live with you as a celibate. I have come to you, revered sir, to become your disciple.'

### 4.4.4

तं होवाच किंगोत्रो नु सोम्यासीति स होवाच नाहमेतद्वेद भो यद्गोत्रोऽहमस्म्यपृच्छं मातरं सा मा प्रत्यब्रवीद्बह्वहं चरन्ती परिचरिणी यौवने त्वामलभे साहमेतन्न वेद यद्गोत्रस्त्वमसि जबाला तु नामाहमस्मि सत्यकामो नाम त्वमसीति सोऽहं सत्यकामो जाबालोऽस्मि भो इति ॥ ४.४.४ ॥

*taṃ hovāca kiṃgotro nu somyāsīti sa hovāca nāhametadveda bho yadgotro'hamasmyapṛcchaṃ mātaraṃ sā mā pratyabravīdbahvahaṃ carantī paricariṇī yauvane tvāmalabhe sāhametanna veda yadgotrastvamasi jabālā tu nāmāhamasmi satyakāmo nāma tvamasīti so'haṃ satyakāmo jābālo'smi bho iti || 4.4.4 ||*

Gautama asked him, 'O Somya, what is your lineage?' Satyakāma answered: 'Sir, I do not know it. When I asked my mother, she told me: "In my youth I had you while I was kept busy serving many people, and so I can tell you nothing about your lineage. I am called Jabālā and you are called Satyakāma." So, sir, I am Satyakāma Jābāla.'

### 4.4.5

तं होवाच नैतदब्राह्मणो विवक्तुमर्हति समिधं सोम्याहरोप त्वा नेष्ये न सत्यादगा इति तमुपनीय कृशानामबलानां चतुःशता गा निराकृत्योवाचेमाः सोम्यानुसंव्रजेति ता अभिप्रस्थापयन्नुवाच नासहस्रेणावर्तेयेति स ह वर्षगणं प्रोवास ता यदा सहस्रं सम्पेदुः ॥ ४.४.५ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*taṃ hovāca naitadabrāhmaṇo vivaktumarhati samidhaṃ somyāharopa tvā neṣye na satyādagā iti tamupanīya kṛśānāmabalānāṃ catuḥśatā gā nirākṛtyovācemāḥ somyānusaṃvrajeti tā abhiprasthāpayannuvāca nāsahasreṇāvarteyeti sa ha varṣagaṇaṃ provāsa tā yadā sahasraṃ sampeduḥ || 4.4.5 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Gautama said to him: 'Only a brāhmin could speak in this way, so you must be a brāhmin. O Somya, bring me some fuel for the sacrificial fire. You have not strayed from the truth, and I will initiate you as a brāhmin by giving you the sacred thread.' After the initiation he picked out four hundred weak and starving cows and told Satyakāma, 'O Somya, take these cows away and tend them.' As he drove them off, Satyakāma said, 'I will not return until they number a thousand.' He stayed away for many years, until their number had reached a thousand.

### 4.5.1

अथ हैनमृषभोऽभ्युवाद सत्यकाम३ इति भगव इति ह प्रतिशुश्राव प्राप्ताः सोम्य सहस्रं स्मः प्रापय न आचार्यकुलम् ॥ ४.५.१ ॥

*atha hainamṛṣabho'bhyuvāda satyakāma3 iti bhagava iti ha pratiśuśrāva prāptāḥ somya sahasraṃ smaḥ prāpaya na ācāryakulam || 4.5.1 ||*

Then a bull called out to him, 'O Satyakāma!' He answered, 'Yes, lord.' The bull went on: 'We number a thousand now. Lead us to the teacher's house.'

### 4.5.2
