And once that is pleased, the eater finds joy in his children, his animals, plentiful food, bodily strength, and his good life and scholarship.

### 5.24.1

स य इदमविद्वाग्निहोत्रं जुहोति यथाङ्गारानपोह्य भस्मनि जुहुयात्तादृक्तत्स्यात् ॥ ५.२४.१ ॥

*sa ya idamavidvāgnihotraṃ juhoti yathāṅgārānapohya bhasmani juhuyāttādṛktatsyāt || 5.24.1 ||*

Performing the Agnihotra sacrifice with no knowledge of the Vaiśvānara Self is like pouring oblations into ashes rather than into the fire.

### 5.24.2

अथ य एतदेवं विद्वानग्निहोत्रं जुहोति तस्य सर्वेषु लोकेषु सर्वेषु भूतेषु सर्वेष्वात्मसु हुतं भवति ॥ ५.२४.२ ॥

*atha ya etadevaṃ vidvānagnihotraṃ juhoti tasya sarveṣu lokeṣu sarveṣu bhūteṣu sarveṣvātmasu hutaṃ bhavati || 5.24.2 ||*

But the person who performs the Agnihotra sacrifice fully knowing the Vaiśvānara Self is considered to have made oblations to all the worlds, all beings, and all selves.

### 5.24.3

तद्यथेषीकातूलमग्नौ प्रोतं प्रदूयेतैवंहास्य सर्वे पाप्मानः प्रदूयन्ते य एतदेवं विद्वानग्निहोत्रं जुहोति ॥ ५.२४.३ ॥

*tadyatheṣīkātūlamagnau protaṃ pradūyetaivaṃhāsya sarve pāpmānaḥ pradūyante ya etadevaṃ vidvānagnihotraṃ juhoti || 5.24.3 ||*

The cotton fibres of the iṣīkā grass burn away completely when thrown into the fire. In the same way, all the sins of the one who performs the Agnihotra sacrifice with knowledge of the Vaiśvānara Self are burnt up.

### 5.24.4

तस्मादु हैवंविद्यद्यपि चण्डालायोच्छिष्टं प्रयच्छेदात्मनि हैवास्य तद्वैश्वानरे हुतं स्यादिति तदेष श्लोकः ॥ ५.२४.४ ॥

*tasmādu haivaṃvidyadyapi caṇḍālāyocchiṣṭaṃ prayacchedātmani haivāsya tadvaiśvānare hutaṃ syāditi tadeṣa ślokaḥ || 5.24.4 ||*

So even if someone who knows the Vaiśvānara Ātman gives what is left of his meal to a person without caste, it will be like an oblation made to his own Vaiśvānara Self. There is a verse on this subject:

### 5.24.5

यथेह क्षुधिता बाला मातरं पर्युपासत एवं सर्वाणि भूतान्यग्निहोत्रमुपासत इत्यग्निहोत्रमुपासत इति ॥ ५.२४.५ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्विंशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमोऽध्यायः ॥

*yatheha kṣudhitā bālā mātaraṃ paryupāsata evaṃ sarvāṇi bhūtānyagnihotramupāsata ityagnihotramupāsata iti || 5.24.5 || || iti caturviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti pañcamo'dhyāyaḥ ||*

In this world, hungry children go to their mother and beg for food. In the same way, all living beings beg that the Agnihotra sacrifice be performed without delay.

### 6.1.1

॥ षष्ठोऽध्यायः ॥ श्वेतकेतुर्हारुणेय आस तं ह पितोवाच श्वेतकेतो वस ब्रह्मचर्यं न वै सोम्यास्मत्कुलीनोऽननूच्य ब्रह्मबन्धुरिव भवतीति ॥ ६.१.१ ॥

*|| ṣaṣṭho'dhyāyaḥ || śvetaketurhāruṇeya āsa taṃ ha pitovāca śvetaketo vasa brahmacaryaṃ na vai somyāsmatkulīno'nanūcya brahmabandhuriva bhavatīti || 6.1.1 ||*

Āruṇi had a son called Śvetaketu. One day Āruṇi told him, "Śvetaketu, it is time for you to live as a brahmacārin. Everyone in our family has studied the scriptures and been a good brāhmin."

### 6.1.2

स ह द्वादशवर्ष उपेत्य चतुर्विंशतिवर्षः सर्वान्वेदानधीत्य महामना अनूचानमानी स्तब्ध एयाय तंह पितोवाच ॥ ६.१.२ ॥

*sa ha dvādaśavarṣa upetya caturviṃśativarṣaḥ sarvānvedānadhītya mahāmanā anūcānamānī stabdha eyāya taṃha pitovāca || 6.1.2 ||*

At twelve, Śvetaketu went to his teacher's house. Having studied all the Vedas, he came back at twenty-four, very solemn and conceited, regarding himself as a great scholar. [Seeing this,] his father said, "O Śvetaketu, you have grown very solemn and conceited, and you consider yourself a great scholar. But did you ask your teacher for that teaching [about Brahman]—

### 6.1.3

श्वेतकेतो यन्नु सोम्येदं महामना अनूचानमानी स्तब्धोऽस्युत तमादेशमप्राक्ष्यः येनाश्रुतं श्रुतं भवत्यमतं मतमविज्ञातं विज्ञातमिति कथं नु भगवः स आदेशो भवतीति ॥ ६.१.३ ॥

*śvetaketo yannu somyedaṃ mahāmanā anūcānamānī stabdho'syuta tamādeśamaprākṣyaḥ yenāśrutaṃ śrutaṃ bhavatyamataṃ matamavijñātaṃ vijñātamiti kathaṃ nu bhagavaḥ sa ādeśo bhavatīti || 6.1.3 ||*

"—the teaching through which the unheard becomes heard, the unthought becomes thought, and the unknown becomes known?" [Śvetaketu asked,] "Sir, what teaching is that?"

### 6.1.4

यथा सोम्यैकेन मृत्पिण्डेन सर्वं मृन्मयं विज्ञातं स्याद्वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं मृत्तिकेत्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.१.४ ॥

*yathā somyaikena mṛtpiṇḍena sarvaṃ mṛnmayaṃ vijñātaṃ syādvācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ mṛttiketyeva satyam || 6.1.4 ||*

O Somya, it is like this. Know one lump of earth and you know everything made of earth. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The earth is what is real.

### 6.1.5

यथा सोम्यैकेन लोहमणिना सर्वं लोहमयं विज्ञातं स्याद्वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं लोहमित्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.१.५ ॥

*yathā somyaikena lohamaṇinā sarvaṃ lohamayaṃ vijñātaṃ syādvācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ lohamityeva satyam || 6.1.5 ||*

O Somya, it is like this. Know one lump of gold and you know everything made of gold. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The gold is what is real.

### 6.1.6

यथा सोम्यिकेन नखनिकृन्तनेन सर्वं कार्ष्णायसं विज्ञातं स्याद्वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं कृष्णायसमित्येव सत्यमेवंसोम्य स आदेशो भवतीति ॥ ६.१.६ ॥

*yathā somyikena nakhanikṛntanena sarvaṃ kārṣṇāyasaṃ vijñātaṃ syādvācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ kṛṣṇāyasamityeva satyamevaṃsomya sa ādeśo bhavatīti || 6.1.6 ||*

O Somya, it is like this. Know one nail-cutter and you know everything made of iron. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The iron is what is real. O Somya, this is the teaching I was speaking of.

### 6.1.7

न वै नूनं भगवन्तस्त एतदवेदिषुर्यद्ध्येतदवेदिष्यन्कथं मे नावक्ष्यन्निति भगवांस्त्वेव मे तद्ब्रवीत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१.७ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*na vai nūnaṃ bhagavantasta etadavediṣuryaddhyetadavediṣyankathaṃ me nāvakṣyanniti bhagavāṃstveva me tadbravītviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.1.7 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

[Śvetaketu said:] "My revered teachers surely did not know this truth. Had they known it, why would they not have told me? So please explain it to me, sir." "Very well, my son," his father said.

### 6.2.1

सदेव सोम्येदमग्र आसीदेकमेवाद्वितीयम् । तद्धैक आहुरसदेवेदमग्र आसीदेकमेवाद्वितीयं तस्मादसतः सज्जायत ॥ ६.२.१ ॥

*sadeva somyedamagra āsīdekamevādvitīyam | taddhaika āhurasadevedamagra āsīdekamevādvitīyaṃ tasmādasataḥ sajjāyata || 6.2.1 ||*

Somya, before this world became manifest, only existence was there, one without a second. Some hold, on this subject, that before this world became manifest only non-existence was there, one without a second, and that existence arose out of that non-existence.

### 6.2.2

कुतस्तु खलु सोम्यैवंस्यादिति होवाच कथमसतः सज्जायेतेति। सत्त्वेव सोम्येदमग्र आसीदेकमेवाद्वितीयम् ॥ ६.२.२ ॥

*kutastu khalu somyaivaṃsyāditi hovāca kathamasataḥ sajjāyeteti| sattveva somyedamagra āsīdekamevādvitīyam || 6.2.2 ||*

The father said: "O Somya, what evidence is there for this, that something has arisen from nothing? No, O Somya, before this world came into being, only existence was there, one without a second."

### 6.2.3

तदैक्षत बहु स्यां प्रजायेयेति तत्तेजोऽसृजत तत्तेज ऐक्षत बहु स्यां प्रजायेयेति तदपोऽसृजत । तस्माद्यत्र क्वच शोचति स्वेदते वा पुरुषस्तेजस एव तदध्यापो जायन्ते ॥ ६.२.३ ॥

*tadaikṣata bahu syāṃ prajāyeyeti tattejo'sṛjata tatteja aikṣata bahu syāṃ prajāyeyeti tadapo'sṛjata | tasmādyatra kvaca śocati svedate vā puruṣastejasa eva tadadhyāpo jāyante || 6.2.3 ||*

That Existence resolved, "I shall be many. I shall be born." He then created fire. That fire in turn resolved, "I shall be many. I shall be born." Fire then produced water. This is why, whenever or wherever a person grieves or sweats, he produces water.

### 6.2.4

ता आप ऐक्षन्त बह्व्यः स्याम प्रजायेमहीति ता अन्नमसृजन्त तस्माद्यत्र क्व च वर्षति तदेव भूयिष्ठमन्नं भवत्यद्भ्य एव तदध्यन्नाद्यं जायते ॥ ६.२.४ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*tā āpa aikṣanta bahvyaḥ syāma prajāyemahīti tā annamasṛjanta tasmādyatra kva ca varṣati tadeva bhūyiṣṭhamannaṃ bhavatyadbhya eva tadadhyannādyaṃ jāyate || 6.2.4 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

That water resolved, "I shall be many; I shall be born." That water then created food. This is why, whenever and wherever it rains, food immediately grows in great abundance. Food is produced from water.

### 6.3.1

तेषां खल्वेषां भूतानां त्रीण्येव बीजानि भवन्त्याण्डजं जीवजमुद्भिज्जमिति ॥ ६.३.१ ॥

*teṣāṃ khalveṣāṃ bhūtānāṃ trīṇyeva bījāni bhavantyāṇḍajaṃ jīvajamudbhijjamiti || 6.3.1 ||*

Living beings arise in three ways: from eggs, from parents, and from plants.

### 6.3.2

सेयं देवतैक्षत हन्ताहमिमास्तिस्रो देवता अनेन जीवेनात्मनानुप्रविश्य नामरूपे व्याकरवाणीति ॥ ६.३.२ ॥

*seyaṃ devataikṣata hantāhamimāstisro devatā anena jīvenātmanānupraviśya nāmarūpe vyākaravāṇīti || 6.3.2 ||*

That god [Existence] resolved: "I shall enter these three deities [fire, water, and earth] as the individual self and show myself in many names and forms."

### 6.3.3

तासां त्रिवृतं त्रिवृतमेकैकां करवाणीति सेयं देवतेमास्तिस्रो देवता अनेनैव जीवेनात्मनानुप्रविश्य नामरूपे व्याकरोत् ॥ ६.३.३ ॥

*tāsāṃ trivṛtaṃ trivṛtamekaikāṃ karavāṇīti seyaṃ devatemāstisro devatā anenaiva jīvenātmanānupraviśya nāmarūpe vyākarot || 6.3.3 ||*

Sat [Existence] thought, "I shall divide each of these three deities into three." Then, having entered these three deities as the individual self, he showed himself as names and forms.

### 6.3.4

तासां त्रिवृतं त्रिवृतमेकैकामकरोद्यथा तु खलु सोम्येमास्तिस्रो देवतास्त्रिवृत्त्रिवृदेकैका भवति तन्मे विजानीहीति ॥ ६.३.४ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*tāsāṃ trivṛtaṃ trivṛtamekaikāmakarodyathā tu khalu somyemāstisro devatāstrivṛttrivṛdekaikā bhavati tanme vijānīhīti || 6.3.4 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

[Having resolved this,] he made each of these three elements threefold. Now learn from me, O Somya, how each of these three deities becomes threefold.

### 6.4.1

यदग्ने रोहितंरूपं तेजसस्तद्रूपं यच्छुक्लं तदपां यत्कृष्णं तदन्नस्यापागादग्नेरग्नित्वं वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं त्रीणि रूपाणीत्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.४.१ ॥

*yadagne rohitaṃrūpaṃ tejasastadrūpaṃ yacchuklaṃ tadapāṃ yatkṛṣṇaṃ tadannasyāpāgādagneragnitvaṃ vācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ trīṇi rūpāṇītyeva satyam || 6.4.1 ||*

The red colour of gross fire comes from subtle fire, the white colour from subtle water, and the dark colour from subtle earth. So what makes fire "fire" has disappeared. All changes are only words, names and nothing more [that is, fire is just a name for a certain condition]. The three colours are what is real.

### 6.4.2

यदादित्यस्य रोहितंरूपं तेजसस्तद्रूपं यच्छुक्लं तदपां यत्कृष्णं तदन्नस्यापागादादित्यादादित्यत्वं वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं त्रीणि रूपाणीत्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.४.२ ॥

*yadādityasya rohitaṃrūpaṃ tejasastadrūpaṃ yacchuklaṃ tadapāṃ yatkṛṣṇaṃ tadannasyāpāgādādityādādityatvaṃ vācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ trīṇi rūpāṇītyeva satyam || 6.4.2 ||*

The sun's red colour comes from fire, its white colour from water, and its dark colour from earth. So what makes the sun the "sun" has disappeared. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The three colours are what is real.

### 6.4.3

यच्छन्द्रमसो रोहितंरूपं तेजसस्तद्रूपं यच्छुक्लं तदपां यत्कृष्णं तदन्नस्यापागाच्चन्द्राच्चन्द्रत्वं वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं त्रीणि रूपाणीत्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.४.३ ॥

*yacchandramaso rohitaṃrūpaṃ tejasastadrūpaṃ yacchuklaṃ tadapāṃ yatkṛṣṇaṃ tadannasyāpāgāccandrāccandratvaṃ vācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ trīṇi rūpāṇītyeva satyam || 6.4.3 ||*

The moon's red colour comes from fire, its white colour from water, and its dark colour from earth. So what makes the moon the "moon" has disappeared. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The three colours are what is real.

### 6.4.4

यद्विद्युतो रोहितंरूपं तेजसस्तद्रूपं यच्छुक्लं तदपां यत्कृष्णं तदन्नस्यापागाद्विद्युतो विद्युत्त्वं वाचारम्भणं विकारो नामधेयं त्रीणि रूपाणीत्येव सत्यम् ॥ ६.४.४ ॥

*yadvidyuto rohitaṃrūpaṃ tejasastadrūpaṃ yacchuklaṃ tadapāṃ yatkṛṣṇaṃ tadannasyāpāgādvidyuto vidyuttvaṃ vācārambhaṇaṃ vikāro nāmadheyaṃ trīṇi rūpāṇītyeva satyam || 6.4.4 ||*

Lightning's red colour comes from fire, its white colour from water, and its dark colour from earth. So what makes lightning "lightning" has disappeared. All changes are only words, names and nothing more. The three colours are what is real.

### 6.4.5

एतद्ध स्म वै तद्विद्वांस आहुः पूर्वे महाशाला महाश्रोत्रिया न नोऽद्य कश्चनाश्रुतममतमविज्ञातमुदाहरिष्यतीति ह्येभ्यो विदांचक्रुः ॥ ६.४.५ ॥

*etaddha sma vai tadvidvāṃsa āhuḥ pūrve mahāśālā mahāśrotriyā na no'dya kaścanāśrutamamatamavijñātamudāhariṣyatīti hyebhyo vidāṃcakruḥ || 6.4.5 ||*

The great householders of earlier times, well versed in the Vedas, knew this. They said, "No one can name anything that we have not heard of, thought of, or already known." This is because they had come to know about the three colours.

### 6.4.6

यदु रोहितमिवाभूदिति तेजसस्तद्रूपमिति तद्विदांचक्रुर्यदु शुक्लमिवाभूदित्यपांरूपमिति तद्विदांचक्रुर्यदु कृष्णमिवाभूदित्यन्नस्य रूपमिति तद्विदांचक्रुः ॥ ६.४.६ ॥

*yadu rohitamivābhūditi tejasastadrūpamiti tadvidāṃcakruryadu śuklamivābhūdityapāṃrūpamiti tadvidāṃcakruryadu kṛṣṇamivābhūdityannasya rūpamiti tadvidāṃcakruḥ || 6.4.6 ||*

They knew that anything else seen as red was the colour of fire, anything else seen as white was the colour of water, and anything else seen as dark was the colour of earth.

### 6.4.7

यद्वविज्ञातमिवाभूदित्येतासामेव देवतानांसमास इति तद्विदांचक्रुर्यथा तु खलु सोम्येमास्तिस्रो देवताः पुरुषं प्राप्य त्रिवृत्त्रिवृदेकैका भवति तन्मे विजानीहीति ॥ ६.४.७ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*yadvavijñātamivābhūdityetāsāmeva devatānāṃsamāsa iti tadvidāṃcakruryathā tu khalu somyemāstisro devatāḥ puruṣaṃ prāpya trivṛttrivṛdekaikā bhavati tanme vijānīhīti || 6.4.7 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

And anything else not clearly known, they understood to be a combination of those three deities [fire, water, and earth]. O Somya, now learn from me how these three deities enter a person and become threefold.

### 6.5.1

अन्नमशितं त्रेधा विधीयते तस्य यः स्थविष्ठो धातुस्तत्पुरीषं भवति यो मध्यमस्तन्मांसं योऽणिष्ठस्तन्मनः ॥ ६.५.१ ॥

*annamaśitaṃ tredhā vidhīyate tasya yaḥ sthaviṣṭho dhātustatpurīṣaṃ bhavati yo madhyamastanmāṃsaṃ yo'ṇiṣṭhastanmanaḥ || 6.5.1 ||*

Food, when eaten, splits into three parts. Its grossest part turns into excreta, the less gross part into our flesh, and the finest part into our mind.

### 6.5.2

आपः पीतास्त्रेधा विधीयन्ते तासां यः स्थविष्ठो धातुस्तन्मूत्रं भवति यो मध्यमस्तल्लोहितं योऽणिष्ठः स प्राणः ॥ ६.५.२ ॥

*āpaḥ pītāstredhā vidhīyante tāsāṃ yaḥ sthaviṣṭho dhātustanmūtraṃ bhavati yo madhyamastallohitaṃ yo'ṇiṣṭhaḥ sa prāṇaḥ || 6.5.2 ||*

Water, when drunk, splits into three parts. Its grossest part turns into urine, the less gross part into blood, and the finest part into prāṇa, the vital force.

### 6.5.3

तेजोऽशितं त्रेधा विधीयते तस्य यः स्थविष्ठो धातुस्तदस्थि भवति यो मध्यमः स मज्जा योऽणिष्ठः सा वाक् ॥ ६.५.३ ॥

*tejo'śitaṃ tredhā vidhīyate tasya yaḥ sthaviṣṭho dhātustadasthi bhavati yo madhyamaḥ sa majjā yo'ṇiṣṭhaḥ sā vāk || 6.5.3 ||*

Fire [butter, oil, and the like], when eaten, splits into three parts. Its grossest part turns into bone, the less gross part into marrow, and the subtlest part into speech.

### 6.5.4

अन्नमयंहि सोम्य मनः आपोमयः प्राणस्तेजोमयी वागिति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.५.४ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*annamayaṃhi somya manaḥ āpomayaḥ prāṇastejomayī vāgiti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.5.4 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

"O Somya, food nourishes the mind, water nourishes prāṇa, and fire nourishes speech." [Śvetaketu then said,] "Sir, would you please explain this to me again?" "Yes, Somya, I will explain it again," his father answered.

### 6.6.1

दध्नः सोम्य मथ्यमानस्य योऽणिमा स उर्ध्वः समुदीषति तत्सर्पिर्भवति ॥ ६.६.१ ॥

*dadhnaḥ somya mathyamānasya yo'ṇimā sa urdhvaḥ samudīṣati tatsarpirbhavati || 6.6.1 ||*

When curd is churned, its finest part comes up to the surface and turns into butter.

### 6.6.2

एवमेव खलु सोम्यान्नस्याश्यमानस्य योऽणिमा स उर्ध्वः समुदीषति तन्मनो भवति ॥ ६.६.२ ॥

*evameva khalu somyānnasyāśyamānasya yo'ṇimā sa urdhvaḥ samudīṣati tanmano bhavati || 6.6.2 ||*

In the same way, O Somya, the finest part of the food we eat comes up to the surface and turns into the mind.

### 6.6.3

अपांसोम्य पीयमानानां योऽणिमा स उर्ध्वः समुदीषति सा प्राणो भवति ॥ ६.६.३ ॥

*apāṃsomya pīyamānānāṃ yo'ṇimā sa urdhvaḥ samudīṣati sā prāṇo bhavati || 6.6.3 ||*

O Somya, the finest part of the water we drink comes up to the surface and turns into our prāṇa.

### 6.6.4

तेजसः सोम्याश्यमानस्य योऽणिमा स उर्ध्वः समुदीषति सा वाग्भवति ॥ ६.६.४ ॥

*tejasaḥ somyāśyamānasya yo'ṇimā sa urdhvaḥ samudīṣati sā vāgbhavati || 6.6.4 ||*

O Somya, the finest part of the fire [butter and the like] we eat comes up to the surface and turns into our speech.

### 6.6.6

अन्नमयं हि सोम्य मन आपोमयः प्राणस्तेजोमयी वागिति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.६.६ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*annamayaṃ hi somya mana āpomayaḥ prāṇastejomayī vāgiti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.6.6 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

"O Somya, food certainly nourishes the mind, water nourishes prāṇa, and fire nourishes speech." [Śvetaketu then said,] "Sir, would you please explain this to me again?" "Yes, I will explain it again, O Somya," his father answered.

### 6.7.1

षोडशकलः सोम्य पुरुषः पञ्चदशाहानि माशीः काममपः पिबापोमयः प्राणो नपिबतो विच्छेत्स्यत इति ॥ ६.७.१ ॥

*ṣoḍaśakalaḥ somya puruṣaḥ pañcadaśāhāni māśīḥ kāmamapaḥ pibāpomayaḥ prāṇo napibato vicchetsyata iti || 6.7.1 ||*

O Somya, a person has sixteen parts, [and all your sixteen parts are intact]. For fifteen days eat nothing, but drink all the water you like. Life depends on water. Without water you will lose your life.

### 6.7.2

स ह पञ्चदशाहानि नशाथ हैनमुपससाद किं ब्रवीमि भो इत्यृचः सोम्य यजूंषि सामानीति स होवाच न वै मा प्रतिभान्ति भो इति ॥ ६.७.२ ॥

*sa ha pañcadaśāhāni naśātha hainamupasasāda kiṃ bravīmi bho ityṛcaḥ somya yajūṃṣi sāmānīti sa hovāca na vai mā pratibhānti bho iti || 6.7.2 ||*

For fifteen days Śvetaketu ate nothing. Then he went to his father and said, "O Father, what should I recite?" His father said, "Recite the Ṛk, Yajuḥ, and Sāma mantras." Śvetaketu answered, "I cannot remember any of them, sir."

### 6.7.3

तं होवाच यथा सोम्य महतोऽभ्या हितस्यैकोऽङ्गारः खद्योतमात्रः परिशिष्टः स्यात्तेन ततोऽपि न बहु दहेदेवंसोम्य ते षोडशानां कलानामेका कलातिशिष्टा स्यात्तयैतर्हि वेदान्नानुभवस्यशानाथ मे विज्ञास्यसीति ॥ ६.७.३ ॥

*taṃ hovāca yathā somya mahato'bhyā hitasyaiko'ṅgāraḥ khadyotamātraḥ pariśiṣṭaḥ syāttena tato'pi na bahu dahedevaṃsomya te ṣoḍaśānāṃ kalānāmekā kalātiśiṣṭā syāttayaitarhi vedānnānubhavasyaśānātha me vijñāsyasīti || 6.7.3 ||*

The father said to Śvetaketu: "O Somya, if only a small ember the size of a firefly is left of a blazing fire, it cannot burn anything larger than itself. Likewise, O Somya, because only one small part of your sixteen parts is left, you cannot remember the Vedas. Eat something, and then you will understand what I am saying."

### 6.7.4

स हशाथ हैनमुपससाद तं ह यत्किंच पप्रच्छ सर्वंह प्रतिपेदे ॥ ६.७.४ ॥

*sa haśātha hainamupasasāda taṃ ha yatkiṃca papraccha sarvaṃha pratipede || 6.7.4 ||*

Śvetaketu ate and then returned to his father. He could now follow whatever his father asked him.

### 6.7.5

तं होवाच यथा सोम्य महतोऽभ्याहितस्यैकमङ्गारं खद्योतमात्रं परिशिष्टं तं तृणैरुपसमाधाय प्राज्वलयेत्तेन ततोऽपि बहु दहेत् ॥ ६.७.५ ॥

*taṃ hovāca yathā somya mahato'bhyāhitasyaikamaṅgāraṃ khadyotamātraṃ pariśiṣṭaṃ taṃ tṛṇairupasamādhāya prājvalayettena tato'pi bahu dahet || 6.7.5 ||*

The father said to him: "O Somya, if only a small ember the size of a firefly is left of a blazing fire, it can flare up again when some grass is added. In fact, it can then blaze even more strongly than before."

### 6.7.6

एवं सोम्य ते षोडशानां कलानामेका कलातिशिष्टाभूत्सान्नेनोपसमाहिता प्राज्वाली तयैतर्हि वेदाननुभवस्यन्नमयंहि सोम्य मन आपोमयः प्राणस्तेजोमयी वागिति तद्धास्य विजज्ञाविति विजज्ञाविति ॥ ६.७.६ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*evaṃ somya te ṣoḍaśānāṃ kalānāmekā kalātiśiṣṭābhūtsānnenopasamāhitā prājvālī tayaitarhi vedānanubhavasyannamayaṃhi somya mana āpomayaḥ prāṇastejomayī vāgiti taddhāsya vijajñāviti vijajñāviti || 6.7.6 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

"In the same way, O Somya, only one of your sixteen parts was left. But once nourished by food it has revived, and through it you can now follow the Vedas. O Somya, this is why I said that the mind is nourished by food, prāṇa by water, and speech by fire." Śvetaketu now understood what his father was saying.

### 6.8.1

उद्दालको हारुणिः श्वेतकेतुं पुत्रमुवाच स्वप्नान्तं मे सोम्य विजानीहीति यत्रैतत्पुरुषः स्वपिति नाम सता सोम्य तदा सम्पन्नो भवति स्वमपीतो भवति तस्मादेनं स्वपितीत्याचक्षते स्वंह्यपीतो भवति ॥ ६.८.१ ॥

*uddālako hāruṇiḥ śvetaketuṃ putramuvāca svapnāntaṃ me somya vijānīhīti yatraitatpuruṣaḥ svapiti nāma satā somya tadā sampanno bhavati svamapīto bhavati tasmādenaṃ svapitītyācakṣate svaṃhyapīto bhavati || 6.8.1 ||*

Uddālaka Āruṇi said to his son Śvetaketu: "O Somya, let me explain deep sleep to you. When a person is said to be asleep, O Somya, he becomes one with Sat [Existence] and reaches his real Self. That is why people say of him, 'He is sleeping.' He is then in his Self."

### 6.8.2

स यथा शकुनिः सूत्रेण प्रबद्धो दिशं दिशं पतित्वान्यत्रायतनमलब्ध्वा बन्धनमेवोपश्रयत एवमेव खलु सोम्य तन्मनो दिशं दिशं पतित्वान्यत्रायतनमलब्ध्वा प्राणमेवोपश्रयते प्राणबन्धनं हि सोम्य मन इति ॥ ६.८.२ ॥

*sa yathā śakuniḥ sūtreṇa prabaddho diśaṃ diśaṃ patitvānyatrāyatanamalabdhvā bandhanamevopaśrayata evameva khalu somya tanmano diśaṃ diśaṃ patitvānyatrāyatanamalabdhvā prāṇamevopaśrayate prāṇabandhanaṃ hi somya mana iti || 6.8.2 ||*

A bird tied to a string flutters this way and that, and, finding no shelter anywhere, gives in to its bondage. In the same way, O Somya, the mind runs off in every direction, and, finding no place to rest, gives itself up to prāṇa, the vital force. The mind, O Somya, is tied to prāṇa.

### 6.8.3

अशनापिपासे मे सोम्य विजानीहीति यत्रैतत्पुरुषोऽशिशिषति नामाप एव तदशितं नयन्ते तद्यथा गोनायोऽश्वनायः पुरुषनाय इत्येवं तदप आचक्षतेऽशनायेति तत्रितच्छुङ्गमुत्पतितं सोम्य विजानीहि नेदममूलं भविष्यतीति ॥ ६.८.३ ॥

*aśanāpipāse me somya vijānīhīti yatraitatpuruṣo'śiśiṣati nāmāpa eva tadaśitaṃ nayante tadyathā gonāyo'śvanāyaḥ puruṣanāya ityevaṃ tadapa ācakṣate'śanāyeti tatritacchuṅgamutpatitaṃ somya vijānīhi nedamamūlaṃ bhaviṣyatīti || 6.8.3 ||*

O Somya, now let me teach you about hunger and thirst. When someone is called hungry, understand that water has carried off the food he ate. People speak of a leader of cows, a leader of horses, a leader of people; in the same way they call water the leader of food. So too, O Somya, understand that this sprout [the body] has come from something [from food and drink]. It cannot exist without a root.

### 6.8.4

तस्य क्व मूलं स्यादन्यत्रान्नादेवमेव खलु सोम्यान्नेन शुङ्गेनापो मूलमन्विच्छाद्भिः सोम्य शुङ्गेन तेजो मूलमन्विच्छ तेजसा सोम्य शुङ्गेन सन्मूलमन्विच्छ सन्मूलाः सोम्येमाः सर्वाः प्रजाः सदायतनाः सत्प्रतिष्ठाः ॥ ६.८.४ ॥

*tasya kva mūlaṃ syādanyatrānnādevameva khalu somyānnena śuṅgenāpo mūlamanvicchādbhiḥ somya śuṅgena tejo mūlamanviccha tejasā somya śuṅgena sanmūlamanviccha sanmūlāḥ somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sadāyatanāḥ satpratiṣṭhāḥ || 6.8.4 ||*

Where could the body have its root if not in food? In the same way, O Somya, when food is the sprout, look for water as its root. When water is the sprout, O Somya, look for fire as its root. When fire is the sprout, O Somya, look for Sat [Existence] as its root. O Somya, Sat is the root of all these beings, their abode and their support.

### 6.8.5

अथ यत्रैतत्पुरुषः पिपासति नाम तेज एव तत्पीतं नयते तद्यथा गोनायोऽश्वनायः पुरुषनाय इत्येवं तत्तेज आचष्ट उदन्येति तत्रैतदेव शुङ्गमुत्पतितं सोम्य विजानीहि नेदममूलं भविष्यतीति ॥ ६.८.५ ॥

*atha yatraitatpuruṣaḥ pipāsati nāma teja eva tatpītaṃ nayate tadyathā gonāyo'śvanāyaḥ puruṣanāya ityevaṃ tatteja ācaṣṭa udanyeti tatraitadeva śuṅgamutpatitaṃ somya vijānīhi nedamamūlaṃ bhaviṣyatīti || 6.8.5 ||*

Further, when someone is called thirsty, understand that fire has carried off the water he drank. People speak of a leader of cows, a leader of horses, a leader of people; in the same way they call fire the leader of water. So too, O Somya, understand that this sprout [the body] has come from something [from food and drink]. It cannot exist without a root.

### 6.8.6

तस्य क्व मूलं स्यादन्यत्राद्भ्य्ऽद्भिः सोम्य शुङ्गेन तेजो मूलमन्विच्छ तेजसा सोम्य शुङ्गेन सन्मूलमन्विच्छ सन्मूलाः सोम्येमाः सर्वाः प्रजाः सदायतनाः सत्प्रतिष्ठा यथा तु खलु सोम्येमास्तिस्रो देवताः पुरुषं प्राप्य त्रिवृत्त्रिवृदेकैका भवति तदुक्तं पुरस्तादेव भवत्यस्य सोम्य पुरुषस्य प्रयतो वाङ्मनसि सम्पद्यते मनः प्राणे प्राणस्तेजसि तेजः परस्यां देवतायाम् ॥ ६.८.६ ॥

*tasya kva mūlaṃ syādanyatrādbhy'dbhiḥ somya śuṅgena tejo mūlamanviccha tejasā somya śuṅgena sanmūlamanviccha sanmūlāḥ somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sadāyatanāḥ satpratiṣṭhā yathā tu khalu somyemāstisro devatāḥ puruṣaṃ prāpya trivṛttrivṛdekaikā bhavati taduktaṃ purastādeva bhavatyasya somya puruṣasya prayato vāṅmanasi sampadyate manaḥ prāṇe prāṇastejasi tejaḥ parasyāṃ devatāyām || 6.8.6 ||*

Where could the body have its root if not in water? O Somya, when water is the sprout, look for fire as its root. When fire is the sprout, O Somya, look for Sat [Existence] as its root. O Somya, Sat is the root of all these beings, their abode and their support. How these three deities [fire, water and earth] enter a body and each become threefold, O Somya, has already been explained. When this person is dying, O Somya, his speech merges in the mind, his mind in prāṇa, his prāṇa in fire, and that fire in Brahman, the Supreme Deity.

### 6.8.7

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.८.७ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.8.7 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.9.1

यथा सोम्य मधु मधुकृतो निस्तिष्ठन्ति नानात्ययानां वृक्षाणांरसान्समवहारमेकतांरसं गमयन्ति ॥ ६.९.१ ॥

*yathā somya madhu madhukṛto nistiṣṭhanti nānātyayānāṃ vṛkṣāṇāṃrasānsamavahāramekatāṃrasaṃ gamayanti || 6.9.1 ||*

O Somya, bees make honey by gathering the juices of many different trees and blending them into a single juice—

### 6.9.2

ते यथा तत्र न विवेकं लभन्तेऽमुष्याहं वृक्षस्य रसोऽस्म्यमुष्याहं वृक्षस्य रसोऽस्मीत्येवमेव खलु सोम्येमाः सर्वाः प्रजाः सति सम्पद्य न विदुः सति सम्पद्यामह इति ॥ ६.९.२ ॥

*te yathā tatra na vivekaṃ labhante'muṣyāhaṃ vṛkṣasya raso'smyamuṣyāhaṃ vṛkṣasya raso'smītyevameva khalu somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sati sampadya na viduḥ sati sampadyāmaha iti || 6.9.2 ||*

—and, O Somya, those juices no longer have any awareness of separate identity, no thought of ‘I am the juice of this tree’ or ‘I am the juice of that tree.’ In the same way, when all these beings reach unity in the Self, they are not aware of it. They do not think, ‘We [were once separate, but] now we are all one with the Self.’

### 6.9.3

त इह व्यघ्रो वा सिंहो वा वृको वा वराहो वा कीटो वा पतङ्गो वा दंशो वा मशको वा यद्यद्भवन्ति तदाभवन्ति ॥ ६.९.३ ॥

*ta iha vyaghro vā siṃho vā vṛko vā varāho vā kīṭo vā pataṅgo vā daṃśo vā maśako vā yadyadbhavanti tadābhavanti || 6.9.3 ||*

Whatever they had been earlier in this world, whether tiger, lion, leopard, boar, bug, insect, flea or mosquito, they are born as that again. [They never know that they came from Sat.]

### 6.9.4

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.९.४ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.9.4 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.10.1

इमाः सोम्य नद्यः पुरस्तात्प्राच्यः स्यन्दन्ते पश्चात्प्रतीच्यस्ताः समुद्रात्समुद्रमेवापियन्ति स समुद्र एव भवति ता यथा तत्र न विदुरियमहमस्मीयमहमस्मीति ॥ ६.१०.१ ॥

*imāḥ somya nadyaḥ purastātprācyaḥ syandante paścātpratīcyastāḥ samudrātsamudramevāpiyanti sa samudra eva bhavati tā yathā tatra na viduriyamahamasmīyamahamasmīti || 6.10.1 ||*

O Somya, the eastern rivers flow east and the western rivers flow west. They rise from the sea, return to it and become one with it. And once they arrive at the sea, they have no sense of separate identity, no thought of ‘I am this river’ or ‘I am that river’—

### 6.10.2

एवमेव खलु सोम्येमाः सर्वाः प्रजाः सत आगम्य न विदुः सत आगच्छामह इति त इह व्याघ्रो वा सिंहो वा वृको वा वराहो वा कीटो वा पतङ्गो वा दंशो वा मशको वा यद्यद्भवन्ति तदाभवन्ति ॥ ६.१०.२ ॥

*evameva khalu somyemāḥ sarvāḥ prajāḥ sata āgamya na viduḥ sata āgacchāmaha iti ta iha vyāghro vā siṃho vā vṛko vā varāho vā kīṭo vā pataṅgo vā daṃśo vā maśako vā yadyadbhavanti tadābhavanti || 6.10.2 ||*

—In just this way, O Somya, all these beings have come from Sat [Brahman] and never realise it. The thought ‘We have come from Sat’ never occurs to them. Whatever they had been earlier in this world, whether tiger, lion, leopard, boar, bug, insect, flea or mosquito, they are born as that again [according to their karma. They never know that they came from Sat].

### 6.10.3

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१०.३ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.10.3 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.11.1

अस्य सोम्य महतो वृक्षस्य यो मूलेऽभ्याहन्याज्जीवन्स्रवेद्यो मध्येऽभ्याहन्याज्जीवन्स्रवेद्योऽग्रेऽभ्याहन्याज्जीवन्स्रवेत्स एष जीवेनात्मनानुप्रभूतः पेपीयमानो मोदमानस्तिष्ठति ॥ ६.११.१ ॥ अस्य यदेकां शाखां जीवो जहात्यथ सा शुष्यति द्वितीयां जहात्यथ सा शुष्यति तृतीयां जहात्यथ सा शुष्यति सर्वं जहाति सर्वः शुष्यति ॥ ६.११.२ ॥

*asya somya mahato vṛkṣasya yo mūle'bhyāhanyājjīvansravedyo madhye'bhyāhanyājjīvansravedyo'gre'bhyāhanyājjīvansravetsa eṣa jīvenātmanānuprabhūtaḥ pepīyamāno modamānastiṣṭhati || 6.11.1 || asya yadekāṃ śākhāṃ jīvo jahātyatha sā śuṣyati dvitīyāṃ jahātyatha sā śuṣyati tṛtīyāṃ jahātyatha sā śuṣyati sarvaṃ jahāti sarvaḥ śuṣyati || 6.11.2 ||*

O Somya, if someone strikes the root of a large tree, the tree goes on living, though it may ooze some sap. If he strikes it in the middle, it still lives, though it may ooze some sap. If he strikes it at the top, it survives, though it may ooze some sap. Because the self pervades it, the tree keeps drinking in moisture and lives happily. But if the self leaves one branch, that branch withers and dies. If it leaves a second branch, that one dies too. If it leaves a third, that one also dies. And if the self withdraws from the entire tree, the entire tree dies.

### 6.11.3

एवमेव खलु सोम्य विद्धीति होवाच जीवापेतं वाव किलेदं म्रियते न जीवो म्रियते इति स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.११.३ ॥ ॥ इति एकादशः खण्डः ॥

*evameva khalu somya viddhīti hovāca jīvāpetaṃ vāva kiledaṃ mriyate na jīvo mriyate iti sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.11.3 || || iti ekādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

The father said, ‘O Somya, understand this: when the self leaves the body, the body certainly dies, but the self never dies. The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.12.1

न्यग्रोधफलमत आहरेतीदं भगव इति भिन्द्धीति भिन्नं भगव इति किमत्र पश्यसीत्यण्व्य इवेमा धाना भगव इत्यासामङ्गैकां भिन्द्धीति भिन्ना भगव इति किमत्र पश्यसीति न किंचन भगव इति ॥ ६.१२.१ ॥

*nyagrodhaphalamata āharetīdaṃ bhagava iti bhinddhīti bhinnaṃ bhagava iti kimatra paśyasītyaṇvya ivemā dhānā bhagava ityāsāmaṅgaikāṃ bhinddhīti bhinnā bhagava iti kimatra paśyasīti na kiṃcana bhagava iti || 6.12.1 ||*

Uddālaka said, ‘Fetch me a fruit from this banyan tree.’ Śvetaketu answered, ‘Here it is, sir.’ Uddālaka: ‘Split it open.’ Śvetaketu: ‘It is split, sir.’ Uddālaka: ‘What can you see inside?’ Śvetaketu: ‘Tiny seeds, sir.’ Uddālaka: ‘Split one of those, my son.’ Śvetaketu: ‘It is split, sir.’ Uddālaka: ‘What can you see in it?’ Śvetaketu: ‘Nothing, sir.’

### 6.12.2

तं होवाच यं वै सोम्यैतमणिमानं न निभालयस एतस्य वै सोम्यैषोऽणिम्न एवं महान्यग्रोधस्तिष्ठति श्रद्धत्स्व सोम्येति ॥ ६.१२.२ ॥

*taṃ hovāca yaṃ vai somyaitamaṇimānaṃ na nibhālayasa etasya vai somyaiṣo'ṇimna evaṃ mahānyagrodhastiṣṭhati śraddhatsva somyeti || 6.12.2 ||*

Uddālaka said: ‘O Somya, you cannot see the finest part of that seed. Yet hidden in that finest part is the enormous banyan tree. Trust what I am telling you, O Somya.’

### 6.12.3

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदद्ं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१२.३ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidadṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.12.3 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.13.1

लवणमेतदुदकेऽवधायाथ मा प्रातरुपसीदथा इति स ह तथा चकार तं होवाच यद्दोषा लवणमुदकेऽवाधा अङ्ग तदाहरेति तद्धावमृश्य न विवेद ॥ ६.१३.१ ॥ यथा विलीनमेवाङ्गास्यान्तादाचामेति कथमिति लवणमिति मध्यादाचामेति कथमिति लवणमित्यन्तादाचामेति कथमिति लवणमित्यभिप्रास्यैतदथ मोपसीदथा इति तद्ध तथा चकार तच्छश्वत्संवर्तते तं होवाचात्र वाव किल तत्सोम्य न निभालयसेऽत्रैव किलेति ॥ ६.१३.२ ॥

*lavaṇametadudake'vadhāyātha mā prātarupasīdathā iti sa ha tathā cakāra taṃ hovāca yaddoṣā lavaṇamudake'vādhā aṅga tadāhareti taddhāvamṛśya na viveda || 6.13.1 || yathā vilīnamevāṅgāsyāntādācāmeti kathamiti lavaṇamiti madhyādācāmeti kathamiti lavaṇamityantādācāmeti kathamiti lavaṇamityabhiprāsyaitadatha mopasīdathā iti taddha tathā cakāra tacchaśvatsaṃvartate taṃ hovācātra vāva kila tatsomya na nibhālayase'traiva kileti || 6.13.2 ||*

[Uddālaka said,] ‘Drop this lump of salt in water and come back to me in the morning.’ Śvetaketu did what he was told. Uddālaka said to him, ‘My son, bring me the salt you put in the water.’ Śvetaketu searched but could not find it, because the salt had dissolved. [Uddālaka said,] ‘My son, sip the water from the surface.’ [Śvetaketu did so, and Uddālaka asked,] ‘What is the taste?’ [Śvetaketu answered,] ‘Salty.’ [Uddālaka then said:] ‘Sip it from the middle. What is the taste?’ ‘Salty.’ ‘Sip it from the bottom. What is the taste?’ ‘Salty.’ ‘Throw the water out and then come to me.’ Śvetaketu did this. His father said to him:

### 6.13.3

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१३.३ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.13.3 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.14.1

यथा सोम्य पुरुषं गन्धारेभ्योऽभिनद्धाक्षमानीय तं ततोऽतिजने विसृजेत्स यथा तत्र प्राङ्वोदङ्वाधराङ्वा प्रत्यङ्वा प्रध्मायीताभिनद्धाक्ष आनीतोऽभिनद्धाक्षो विसृष्टः ॥ ६.१४.१ ॥

*yathā somya puruṣaṃ gandhārebhyo'bhinaddhākṣamānīya taṃ tato'tijane visṛjetsa yathā tatra prāṅvodaṅvādharāṅvā pratyaṅvā pradhmāyītābhinaddhākṣa ānīto'bhinaddhākṣo visṛṣṭaḥ || 6.14.1 ||*

O Somya, imagine a person brought blindfolded from the Gandhāra country and abandoned in a deserted place. He turns now east, now north, now south, now west, crying out: ‘I was brought here blindfolded! I was left here blindfolded!’—

### 6.14.2

तस्य यथाभिनहनं प्रमुच्य प्रब्रूयादेतां दिशं गन्धारा एतां दिशं व्रजेति स ग्रामाद्ग्रामं पृच्छन्पण्डितो मेधावी गन्धारानेवोपसम्पद्येतैवमेवेहाचार्यवान्पुरुषो वेद तस्य तावदेव चिरं यावन्न विमोक्ष्येऽथ सम्पत्स्य इति ॥ ६.१४.२ ॥

*tasya yathābhinahanaṃ pramucya prabrūyādetāṃ diśaṃ gandhārā etāṃ diśaṃ vrajeti sa grāmādgrāmaṃ pṛcchanpaṇḍito medhāvī gandhārānevopasampadyetaivamevehācāryavānpuruṣo veda tasya tāvadeva ciraṃ yāvanna vimokṣye'tha sampatsya iti || 6.14.2 ||*

—And imagine someone takes off his blindfold and tells him, ‘Gandhāra lies this way; go in this direction.’ The sensible man travels from village to village, asking the way and trusting what people tell him, until he arrives in Gandhāra. In the same way, a person who finds a teacher gains knowledge. The only delay he faces lasts until he is free of his body. After that he merges in the Self.

### 6.14.3

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१४.३ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्दशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.14.3 || || iti caturdaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.15.1

पुरुषं सोम्योतोपतापिनं ज्ञातयः पर्युपासते जानासि मां जानासि मामिति तस्य यावन्न वाङ्मनसि सम्पद्यते मनः प्राणे प्राणस्तेजसि तेजः परस्यां देवतायां तावज्जानाति ॥ ६.१५.१ ॥

*puruṣaṃ somyotopatāpinaṃ jñātayaḥ paryupāsate jānāsi māṃ jānāsi māmiti tasya yāvanna vāṅmanasi sampadyate manaḥ prāṇe prāṇastejasi tejaḥ parasyāṃ devatāyāṃ tāvajjānāti || 6.15.1 ||*

O Somya, when someone is gravely ill, his relatives gather around him and ask, ‘Do you know me? Do you know me?’ He can recognise them for as long as his speech has not merged in his mind, his mind in his prāṇa, his prāṇa in the heat of his body, and that heat in the Supreme Self.

### 6.15.2

अथ यदास्य वाङ्मनसि सम्पद्यते मनः प्राणे प्राणस्तेजसि तेजः परस्यां देवतायामथ न जानाति ॥ ६.१५.२ ॥

*atha yadāsya vāṅmanasi sampadyate manaḥ prāṇe prāṇastejasi tejaḥ parasyāṃ devatāyāmatha na jānāti || 6.15.2 ||*

Then, once his speech merges in his mind, his mind in prāṇa, his prāṇa in the heat of his body, and that heat in the Supreme Self, he can no longer recognise them.

### 6.15.3

स य एषोऽणिमैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत् सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति भूय एव मा भगवान्विज्ञापयत्विति तथा सोम्येति होवाच ॥ ६.१५.३ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya eṣo'ṇimaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tat satyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti bhūya eva mā bhagavānvijñāpayatviti tathā somyeti hovāca || 6.15.3 || || iti pañcadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘The subtlest thing of all is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ [Śvetaketu then said,] ‘Sir, please teach me this once more.’ ‘Very well, Somya, I will teach it again,’ his father answered.

### 6.16.1

पुरुषं सोम्योत हस्तगृहीतमानयन्त्यपहार्षीत्स्तेयमकार्षीत्परशुमस्मै तपतेति स यदि तस्य कर्ता भवति तत एवानृतमात्मानं कुरुते सोऽनृताभिसंधोऽनृतेनात्मानमन्तर्धाय परशुं तप्तं प्रतिगृह्णाति स दह्यतेऽथ हन्यते ॥ ६.१६.१ ॥

*puruṣaṃ somyota hastagṛhītamānayantyapahārṣītsteyamakārṣītparaśumasmai tapateti sa yadi tasya kartā bhavati tata evānṛtamātmānaṃ kurute so'nṛtābhisaṃdho'nṛtenātmānamantardhāya paraśuṃ taptaṃ pratigṛhṇāti sa dahyate'tha hanyate || 6.16.1 ||*

O Somya, suppose a man is led in with his hands bound, and people say, ‘This man has stolen. He has committed robbery. Heat an axe for him.’ If he did commit the crime, he will surely show himself a liar. Being dishonest and trying to shelter behind falsehood, he will be burned when he takes hold of the hot axe, and he will then be put to death.

### 6.16.2

अथ यदि तस्याकर्ता भवति ततेव सत्यमात्मानं कुरुते स सत्याभिसन्धः सत्येनात्मानमन्तर्धाय परशुं तप्तं प्रतिगृह्णाति सन दह्यतेऽथ मुच्यते ॥ ६.१६.२ ॥

*atha yadi tasyākartā bhavati tateva satyamātmānaṃ kurute sa satyābhisandhaḥ satyenātmānamantardhāya paraśuṃ taptaṃ pratigṛhṇāti sana dahyate'tha mucyate || 6.16.2 ||*

But if he did not commit the crime, he will surely show himself truthful. Being honest, he is shielded by the truth, and the hot axe does not burn him when he takes hold of it. He is then released.

### 6.16.3

स यथा तत्र नादाह्येतैतदात्म्यमिदं सर्वं तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि श्वेतकेतो इति तद्धास्य विजज्ञाविति विजज्ञाविति ॥ ६.१६.३ ॥ ॥ इति षोडशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठोऽध्यायः ॥

*sa yathā tatra nādāhyetaitadātmyamidaṃ sarvaṃ tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā tattvamasi śvetaketo iti taddhāsya vijajñāviti vijajñāviti || 6.16.3 || || iti ṣoḍaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti ṣaṣṭho'dhyāyaḥ ||*

‘Because that man is honest, the hot axe does not harm him. That [Self] is the Self of all this. It is the Truth. It is the Self. You are That, O Śvetaketu.’ Śvetaketu understood this thoroughly from his father.

### 7.1.1

॥ सप्तमोऽध्यायः ॥ अधीहि भगव इति होपससाद सनत्कुमारं नारदस्तं होवाच यद्वेत्थ तेन मोपसीद ततस्त ऊर्ध्वं वक्ष्यामीति स होवाच ॥ ७.१.१ ॥

*|| saptamo'dhyāyaḥ || adhīhi bhagava iti hopasasāda sanatkumāraṃ nāradastaṃ hovāca yadvettha tena mopasīda tatasta ūrdhvaṃ vakṣyāmīti sa hovāca || 7.1.1 ||*

Nārada approached Sanatkumāra [for spiritual instruction] and said, ‘Sir, please teach me.’ Sanatkumāra answered, ‘First tell me what you already know, and I will teach you from there.’ Nārada said—

### 7.1.2

ऋग्वेदं भगवोऽध्येमि यजुर्वेदं सामवेदमाथर्वणं चतुर्थमितिहासपुराणं पञ्चमं वेदानां वेदं पित्र्यं राशिं दैवं निधिं वाकोवाक्यमेकायनं देवविद्यां ब्रह्मविद्यां भूतविद्यां क्षत्रविद्यां नक्षत्रविद्यां सर्पदेवजनविद्यामेतद्भगवोऽध्येमि ॥ ७.१.२ ॥

*ṛgvedaṃ bhagavo'dhyemi yajurvedaṃ sāmavedamātharvaṇaṃ caturthamitihāsapurāṇaṃ pañcamaṃ vedānāṃ vedaṃ pitryaṃ rāśiṃ daivaṃ nidhiṃ vākovākyamekāyanaṃ devavidyāṃ brahmavidyāṃ bhūtavidyāṃ kṣatravidyāṃ nakṣatravidyāṃ sarpadevajanavidyāmetadbhagavo'dhyemi || 7.1.2 ||*

Sir, I have studied the Ṛg Veda, the Yajur Veda, the Sāma Veda and, as the fourth, the Atharva Veda; as the fifth, history and the Purāṇas. I have also studied grammar, funeral rites, mathematics, the science of omens, the science of underground resources, logic, moral science, astrology, Vedic knowledge, the science of the elements, archery, astronomy, the science of snakes, and music, dance and the other fine arts. Sir, that is what I know.

### 7.1.3

सोऽहं भगवो मन्त्रविदेवास्मि नात्मविच्छ्रुतं ह्येव मे भगवद्दृशेभ्यस्तरति शोकमात्मविदिति सोऽहं भगवः शोचामि तं मा भगवाञ्छोकस्य पारं तारयत्विति तं होवाच यद्वै किंचैतदध्यगीष्ठा नामैवैतत् ॥ ७.१.३ ॥

*so'haṃ bhagavo mantravidevāsmi nātmavicchrutaṃ hyeva me bhagavaddṛśebhyastarati śokamātmaviditi so'haṃ bhagavaḥ śocāmi taṃ mā bhagavāñchokasya pāraṃ tārayatviti taṃ hovāca yadvai kiṃcaitadadhyagīṣṭhā nāmaivaitat || 7.1.3 ||*

‘It is true that I have learnt a great deal, but all I know is the meaning of words. I do not know the Self. Sir, I have heard from great persons like you that only those who know the Self get beyond sorrow. I am suffering from sorrow. Please carry me across the ocean of sorrow.’ Sanatkumāra then told Nārada, ‘All that you have studied so far is merely words.’

### 7.1.4

नाम वा ऋग्वेदो यजुर्वेदः सामवेद आथर्वणश्चतुर्थ इतिहासपुराणः पञ्चमो वेदानां वेदः पित्र्यो राशिर्दैवो निधिर्वाकोवाक्यमेकायनं देवविद्या ब्रह्मविद्या भूतविद्या क्षत्रविद्या नक्षत्रविद्या सर्पदेवजनविद्या नामैवैतन्नामोपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.१.४ ॥

*nāma vā ṛgvedo yajurvedaḥ sāmaveda ātharvaṇaścaturtha itihāsapurāṇaḥ pañcamo vedānāṃ vedaḥ pitryo rāśirdaivo nidhirvākovākyamekāyanaṃ devavidyā brahmavidyā bhūtavidyā kṣatravidyā nakṣatravidyā sarpadevajanavidyā nāmaivaitannāmopāssveti || 7.1.4 ||*

The Ṛg Veda, the Yajur Veda, the Sāma Veda and, as the fourth, the Atharva Veda; as the fifth, history and the Purāṇas; grammar, funeral rites, mathematics, the science of omens, the science of underground resources, logic, moral science, astrology, Vedic knowledge, the science of the elements, archery, astronomy, the science of snakes, and music, dance and the other fine arts: all of this is name. These are only names. Worship name.

### 7.1.5

स यो नाम ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावन्नाम्नो गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो नाम ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो नाम्नो भूय इति नाम्नो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.१.५ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo nāma brahmetyupāste yāvannāmno gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo nāma brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo nāmno bhūya iti nāmno vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.1.5 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships name as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as name extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than name?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than name,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.2.1

वाग्वाव नाम्नो भूयसी वाग्वा ऋग्वेदं विज्ञापयति यजुर्वेदं सामवेदमाथर्वणं चतुर्थमितिहासपुराणं पञ्चमं वेदानां वेदं पित्र्यंराशिं दैवं निधिं वाकोवाक्यमेकायनं देवविद्यां ब्रह्मविद्यां भूतविद्यां क्षत्रविद्यां सर्पदेवजनविद्यां दिवं च पृथिवीं च वायुं चाकाशं चापश्च तेजश्च देवांश्च मनुष्यांश्च पशूंश्च वयांसि च तृणवनस्पतीञ्श्वापदान्याकीटपतङ्गपिपीलकं धर्मं चाधर्मं च सत्यं चानृतं च साधु चासाधु च हृदयज्ञं चाहृदयज्ञं च यद्वै वाङ्नाभविष्यन्न धर्मो नाधर्मो व्यज्ञापयिष्यन्न सत्यं नानृतं न साधु नासाधु न हृदयज्ञो नाहृदयज्ञो वागेवैतत्सर्वं विज्ञापयति वाचमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.२.१ ॥

*vāgvāva nāmno bhūyasī vāgvā ṛgvedaṃ vijñāpayati yajurvedaṃ sāmavedamātharvaṇaṃ caturthamitihāsapurāṇaṃ pañcamaṃ vedānāṃ vedaṃ pitryaṃrāśiṃ daivaṃ nidhiṃ vākovākyamekāyanaṃ devavidyāṃ brahmavidyāṃ bhūtavidyāṃ kṣatravidyāṃ sarpadevajanavidyāṃ divaṃ ca pṛthivīṃ ca vāyuṃ cākāśaṃ cāpaśca tejaśca devāṃśca manuṣyāṃśca paśūṃśca vayāṃsi ca tṛṇavanaspatīñśvāpadānyākīṭapataṅgapipīlakaṃ dharmaṃ cādharmaṃ ca satyaṃ cānṛtaṃ ca sādhu cāsādhu ca hṛdayajñaṃ cāhṛdayajñaṃ ca yadvai vāṅnābhaviṣyanna dharmo nādharmo vyajñāpayiṣyanna satyaṃ nānṛtaṃ na sādhu nāsādhu na hṛdayajño nāhṛdayajño vāgevaitatsarvaṃ vijñāpayati vācamupāssveti || 7.2.1 ||*

Speech is certainly greater than name. It is speech that makes known the Ṛg Veda, the Yajur Veda, the Sāma Veda and, as the fourth, the Atharva Veda; as the fifth, history and the Purāṇas; grammar, funeral rites, mathematics, the science of omens, the science of underground resources, logic, moral science, astrology, Vedic knowledge, the science of the elements, archery, astronomy, the science of snakes, and music, dance and the other fine arts; heaven and earth; air, space, water and fire; gods and human beings; cattle and birds; creepers and great trees; beasts of prey, worms, fleas and ants; merit and demerit; truth and untruth; good and evil; the pleasant and the unpleasant. Without speech there would be no awareness of merit and demerit, of truth and untruth, of good and evil, of the pleasant and the unpleasant. Speech alone lets all this be understood. Worship speech.

### 7.2.2

स यो वाचं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावद्वाचो गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो वाचं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो वाचो भूय इति वाचो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.२.२ ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo vācaṃ brahmetyupāste yāvadvāco gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo vācaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo vāco bhūya iti vāco vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.2.2 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships speech as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as speech extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than speech?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than speech,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.3.1

मनो वाव वाचो भूयो यथा वै द्वे वामलके द्वे वा कोले द्वौ वाक्षौ मुष्टिरनुभवत्येवं वाचं च नाम च मनोऽनुभवति स यदा मनसा मनस्यति मन्त्रानधीयीयेत्यथाधीते कर्माणि कुर्वीयेत्यथ कुरुते पुत्रांश्च पशूंश्चेच्छेयेत्यथेच्छत इमं च लोकममुं चेच्छेयेत्यथेच्छते मनो ह्यात्मा मनो हि लोको मनो हि ब्रह्म मन उपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.३.१ ॥

*mano vāva vāco bhūyo yathā vai dve vāmalake dve vā kole dvau vākṣau muṣṭiranubhavatyevaṃ vācaṃ ca nāma ca mano'nubhavati sa yadā manasā manasyati mantrānadhīyīyetyathādhīte karmāṇi kurvīyetyatha kurute putrāṃśca paśūṃśceccheyetyathecchata imaṃ ca lokamamuṃ ceccheyetyathecchate mano hyātmā mano hi loko mano hi brahma mana upāssveti || 7.3.1 ||*

The mind is greater than speech. As a closed fist can hold two āmalaka fruits, two kola fruits [plums] or two akṣa fruits, so the mind can hold within it both speech and name. When a person thinks, ‘I will read the mantras,’ he reads them. When he thinks, ‘I will do this,’ he does it. When he decides, ‘I will have children and animals,’ he can try to have them. When he decides, ‘I will conquer this world and the next,’ he can try to do so. [This is the mark of the mind: if it says it will do something, it can do it.] The mind is the self. The mind is the world. The mind is Brahman. Worship the mind.

### 7.3.2

स यो मनो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावन्मनसो गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो मनो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो मनसो भूय इति मनसो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.३.२ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo mano brahmetyupāste yāvanmanaso gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo mano brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo manaso bhūya iti manaso vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.3.2 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships the mind as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as the mind extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than the mind?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than the mind,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.4.1

संकल्पो वाव मनसो भूयान्यदा वै संकल्पयतेऽथ मनस्यत्यथ वाचमीरयति तामु नाम्नीरयति नाम्नि मन्त्रा एकं भवन्ति मन्त्रेषु कर्माणि ॥ ७.४.१ ॥

*saṃkalpo vāva manaso bhūyānyadā vai saṃkalpayate'tha manasyatyatha vācamīrayati tāmu nāmnīrayati nāmni mantrā ekaṃ bhavanti mantreṣu karmāṇi || 7.4.1 ||*

The will is certainly greater than the mind. When a person wills, thinking begins. Next he directs the organ of speech, and at last he makes it utter the name. All the mantras merge in the names, and all actions merge in the mantras.

### 7.4.2

तानि ह वा एतानि संकल्पैकायनानि संकल्पात्मकानि संकल्पे प्रतिष्ठितानि समकॢपतां द्यावापृथिवी समकल्पेतां वायुश्चाकाशं च समकल्पन्तापश्च तेजश्च तेषां सं कॢप्त्यै वर्षं संकल्पते वर्षस्य संकॢप्त्या अन्नं संकल्पतेऽन्नस्य सं कॢप्त्यै प्राणाः संकल्पन्ते प्राणानां सं कॢप्त्यै मन्त्राः संकल्पन्ते मन्त्राणां सं कॢप्त्यै कर्माणि संकल्पन्ते कर्मणां संकॢप्त्यै लोकः संकल्पते लोकस्य सं कॢप्त्यै सर्वं संकल्पते स एष संकल्पः संकल्पमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.४.२ ॥

*tāni ha vā etāni saṃkalpaikāyanāni saṃkalpātmakāni saṃkalpe pratiṣṭhitāni samakḷpatāṃ dyāvāpṛthivī samakalpetāṃ vāyuścākāśaṃ ca samakalpantāpaśca tejaśca teṣāṃ saṃ kḷptyai varṣaṃ saṃkalpate varṣasya saṃkḷptyā annaṃ saṃkalpate'nnasya saṃ kḷptyai prāṇāḥ saṃkalpante prāṇānāṃ saṃ kḷptyai mantrāḥ saṃkalpante mantrāṇāṃ saṃ kḷptyai karmāṇi saṃkalpante karmaṇāṃ saṃkḷptyai lokaḥ saṃkalpate lokasya saṃ kḷptyai sarvaṃ saṃkalpate sa eṣa saṃkalpaḥ saṃkalpamupāssveti || 7.4.2 ||*

All these things [mind, speech, name and so on] merge in saṅkalpa, arise from saṅkalpa and rest on saṅkalpa. [The will sets the direction of everything you do; it is the soul of everything.] Heaven and earth will, and so do air, space, water and fire. [It is their will that determines their work.] Through their will the rain wills, and through the will of the rain, food wills. The will of food is the will of life. The will of life is the will of the mantras, and the will of the mantras is the will of all activities. The will of the activities is the will of the worlds, and the will of the worlds determines the will of everything. Such is the will. Worship this will.

### 7.4.3

स यः संकल्पं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते संकॢप्तान्वै स लोकान्ध्रुवान्ध्रुवः प्रतिष्ठितान् प्रतिष्ठितोऽव्यथमानानव्यथमानोऽभिसिध्यति यावत्संकल्पस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यः संकल्पं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवः संकल्पाद्भूय इति संकल्पाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.४.३ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*sa yaḥ saṃkalpaṃ brahmetyupāste saṃkḷptānvai sa lokāndhruvāndhruvaḥ pratiṣṭhitān pratiṣṭhito'vyathamānānavyathamāno'bhisidhyati yāvatsaṃkalpasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yaḥ saṃkalpaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavaḥ saṃkalpādbhūya iti saṃkalpādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.4.3 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships saṅkalpa as Brahman can reach any world he wills. He becomes true and reaches the world of truth. He is firmly established and reaches a world that is firmly established. He is free of pain and reaches a world free of pain. Whoever worships saṅkalpa as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as saṅkalpa extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than saṅkalpa?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than saṅkalpa,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.5.1

चित्तं वाव सं कल्पाद्भूयो यदा वै चेतयतेऽथ संकल्पयतेऽथ मनस्यत्यथ वाचमीरयति तामु नाम्नीरयति नाम्नि मन्त्रा एकं भवन्ति मन्त्रेषु कर्माणि ॥ ७.५.१ ॥

*cittaṃ vāva saṃ kalpādbhūyo yadā vai cetayate'tha saṃkalpayate'tha manasyatyatha vācamīrayati tāmu nāmnīrayati nāmni mantrā ekaṃ bhavanti mantreṣu karmāṇi || 7.5.1 ||*

Intelligence is certainly greater than will-power. A person first understands, and then he wills. After that he thinks the matter over repeatedly, then directs the organ of speech, and at last makes it utter the name. All the mantras then merge in the names, and all actions merge in the mantras.

### 7.5.2

तानि ह वा एतानि चित्तैकायनानि चित्तात्मानि चित्ते प्रतिष्ठितानि तस्माद्यद्यपि बहुविदचित्तो भवति नायमस्तीत्येवैनमाहुर्यदयं वेद यद्वा अयं विद्वान्नेत्थमचित्तः स्यादित्यथ यद्यल्पविच्चित्तवान्भवति तस्मा एवोत शुश्रूषन्ते चित्तंह्येवैषामेकायनं चित्तमात्मा चित्तं प्रतिष्ठा चित्तमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.५.२ ॥

*tāni ha vā etāni cittaikāyanāni cittātmāni citte pratiṣṭhitāni tasmādyadyapi bahuvidacitto bhavati nāyamastītyevainamāhuryadayaṃ veda yadvā ayaṃ vidvānnetthamacittaḥ syādityatha yadyalpaviccittavānbhavati tasmā evota śuśrūṣante cittaṃhyevaiṣāmekāyanaṃ cittamātmā cittaṃ pratiṣṭhā cittamupāssveti || 7.5.2 ||*

All these [will-power, mind and so on] merge in intelligence, are guided by intelligence and rest on intelligence. That is why, when a person is learned but dull, people [ignore him and] say: ‘He does not exist, however much he seems to know. If he were really learned, he would not be so foolish.’ But when a person lacks learning and has intelligence, people listen to him [with respect]. Intelligence governs all these; it is their soul and their support. So worship intelligence.

### 7.5.3

स यश्चित्तं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते चित्तान्वै स लोकान्ध्रुवान्ध्रुवः प्रतिष्ठितान्प्रतिष्ठितोऽव्यथमानानव्यथमानोऽभिसिध्यति यावच्चित्तस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यश्चित्तं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवश्चित्ताद्भूय इति चित्ताद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.५.३ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yaścittaṃ brahmetyupāste cittānvai sa lokāndhruvāndhruvaḥ pratiṣṭhitānpratiṣṭhito'vyathamānānavyathamāno'bhisidhyati yāvaccittasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yaścittaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavaścittādbhūya iti cittādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.5.3 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships intelligence as Brahman reaches worlds of intelligence [the things he considers important]. He becomes true and reaches the world of truth. He is firmly established and reaches a world that is firmly established. He is free of pain and reaches a world free of pain. Whoever worships intelligence as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as intelligence extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than intelligence?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than intelligence,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.6.1

ध्यानं वाव चित्ताद्भूयो ध्यायतीव पृथिवी ध्यायतीवान्तरिक्षं ध्यायतीव द्यौर्ध्यायन्तीवापो ध्यायन्तीव पर्वता देवमनुष्यास्तस्माद्य इह मनुष्याणां महत्तां प्राप्नुवन्ति ध्यानापादांशा इवैव ते भवन्त्यथ येऽल्पाः कलहिनः पिशुना उपवादिनस्तेऽथ ये प्रभवो ध्यानापादांशा इवैव ते भवन्ति ध्यानमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.६.१ ॥

*dhyānaṃ vāva cittādbhūyo dhyāyatīva pṛthivī dhyāyatīvāntarikṣaṃ dhyāyatīva dyaurdhyāyantīvāpo dhyāyantīva parvatā devamanuṣyāstasmādya iha manuṣyāṇāṃ mahattāṃ prāpnuvanti dhyānāpādāṃśā ivaiva te bhavantyatha ye'lpāḥ kalahinaḥ piśunā upavādinaste'tha ye prabhavo dhyānāpādāṃśā ivaiva te bhavanti dhyānamupāssveti || 7.6.1 ||*

Meditation is certainly greater than intelligence. The earth seems to be in meditation. So does the space between earth and heaven, and so does heaven. Water seems to be in meditation, and the mountains too. Gods and human beings also seem to be in meditation.

### 7.6.2

स यो ध्यानं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावद्ध्यानस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो ध्यानं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो ध्यानाद्भूय इति ध्यानाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.६.२ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo dhyānaṃ brahmetyupāste yāvaddhyānasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo dhyānaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo dhyānādbhūya iti dhyānādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.6.2 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships meditation as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as meditation extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than meditation?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than meditation,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.7.1

विज्ञानं वाव ध्यानाद्भूयः विज्ञानेन वा ऋग्वेदं विजानाति यजुर्वेदं सामवेदमाथर्वणं चतुर्थमितिहासपुराणं पञ्चमं वेदानां वेदं पित्र्यंराशिं दैवं निधिं वाकोवाक्यमेकायनं देवविद्यां ब्रह्मविद्यां भूतविद्यां क्षत्रविद्यां नक्षत्रविद्यांसर्पदेवजनविद्यां दिवं च पृथिवीं च वायुं चाकाशं चापश्च तेजश्च देवांश्च मनुष्यांश्च पशूंश्च वयांसि च तृणवनस्पतीञ्छ्वापदान्याकीटपतङ्गपिपीलकं धर्मं चाधर्मं च सत्यं चानृतं च साधु चासाधु च हृदयज्ञं चाहृदयज्ञं चान्नं च रसं चेमं च लोकममुं च विज्ञानेनैव विजानाति विज्ञानमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.७.१ ॥

*vijñānaṃ vāva dhyānādbhūyaḥ vijñānena vā ṛgvedaṃ vijānāti yajurvedaṃ sāmavedamātharvaṇaṃ caturthamitihāsapurāṇaṃ pañcamaṃ vedānāṃ vedaṃ pitryaṃrāśiṃ daivaṃ nidhiṃ vākovākyamekāyanaṃ devavidyāṃ brahmavidyāṃ bhūtavidyāṃ kṣatravidyāṃ nakṣatravidyāṃsarpadevajanavidyāṃ divaṃ ca pṛthivīṃ ca vāyuṃ cākāśaṃ cāpaśca tejaśca devāṃśca manuṣyāṃśca paśūṃśca vayāṃsi ca tṛṇavanaspatīñchvāpadānyākīṭapataṅgapipīlakaṃ dharmaṃ cādharmaṃ ca satyaṃ cānṛtaṃ ca sādhu cāsādhu ca hṛdayajñaṃ cāhṛdayajñaṃ cānnaṃ ca rasaṃ cemaṃ ca lokamamuṃ ca vijñānenaiva vijānāti vijñānamupāssveti || 7.7.1 ||*

Vijñāna [knowledge put to practical use] is certainly greater than meditation. Through vijñāna one knows the Ṛg Veda, the Yajur Veda, the Sāma Veda and, as the fourth, the Atharva Veda; as the fifth, history and the Purāṇas; grammar, funeral rites, mathematics, the science of omens, the science of underground resources, logic, moral science, astrology, Vedic knowledge, the science of the elements, archery, astronomy, the science of snakes, and music, dance and the other fine arts; heaven and earth; air, space, water and fire; gods and human beings; cattle and birds; creepers and great trees; beasts of prey, worms, fleas and ants; merit and demerit; truth and untruth; good and evil; the pleasant and the unpleasant; food and water; this world and the other world. All of this is known through vijñāna. Worship vijñāna.

### 7.7.2

स यो विज्ञानं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते विज्ञानवतो वै स लोकाञ्ज्ञानवतोऽभिसिध्यति यावद्विज्ञानस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो विज्ञानं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो विज्ञानाद्भूय इति विज्ञानाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.७.२ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo vijñānaṃ brahmetyupāste vijñānavato vai sa lokāñjñānavato'bhisidhyati yāvadvijñānasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo vijñānaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo vijñānādbhūya iti vijñānādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.7.2 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships vijñāna as Brahman reaches the vijñānamaya and jñānamaya worlds. Whoever worships vijñāna as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as vijñāna extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than vijñāna?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than vijñāna,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.8.1

बलं वाव विज्ञानाद्भूयोऽपि ह शतं विज्ञानवतामेको बलवानाकम्पयते स यदा बली भवत्यथोत्थाता भवत्युत्तिष्ठन्परिचरिता भवति परिचरन्नुपसत्ता भवत्युपसीदन्द्रष्टा भवति श्रोता भवति मन्ता भवति बोद्धा भवति कर्ता भवति विज्ञाता भवति बलेन वै पृथिवी तिष्ठति बलेनान्तरिक्षं बलेन द्यौर्बलेन पर्वता बलेन देवमनुष्या बलेन पशवश्च वयांसि च तृणवनस्पतयः श्वापदान्याकीटपतङ्गपिपीलकं बलेन लोकस्तिष्ठति बलमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.८.१ ॥

*balaṃ vāva vijñānādbhūyo'pi ha śataṃ vijñānavatāmeko balavānākampayate sa yadā balī bhavatyathotthātā bhavatyuttiṣṭhanparicaritā bhavati paricarannupasattā bhavatyupasīdandraṣṭā bhavati śrotā bhavati mantā bhavati boddhā bhavati kartā bhavati vijñātā bhavati balena vai pṛthivī tiṣṭhati balenāntarikṣaṃ balena dyaurbalena parvatā balena devamanuṣyā balena paśavaśca vayāṃsi ca tṛṇavanaspatayaḥ śvāpadānyākīṭapataṅgapipīlakaṃ balena lokastiṣṭhati balamupāssveti || 7.8.1 ||*

Strength is certainly greater than understanding. A single strong person can make even a hundred people of understanding tremble with fear. A person who is strong will be keen and active. He will then begin to serve his teacher, and serving him he will stay close to him. Sitting near the teacher, he will observe him and listen to his words. Then he will reflect on them and try to understand. Next he will act on them, and at last he will grasp their inner meaning. Strength holds up the earth. It holds up the interspace, heaven, the mountains, gods and human beings, cattle, birds, creepers and trees. It holds up beasts of prey, worms, fleas and ants. It holds up the entire world. Worship strength.

### 7.8.2

स यो बलं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावद्बलस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यो बलं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवो बलाद्भूय इति बलाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.८.२ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo balaṃ brahmetyupāste yāvadbalasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo balaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo balādbhūya iti balādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.8.2 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships strength as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as strength extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than strength?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than strength.’

### 7.9.1

अन्नं वाव बलाद्भूयस्तस्माद्यद्यपि दश रात्रीर्नाश्नीयाद्यद्यु ह जीवेदथवाद्रष्टाश्रोतामन्ताबोद्धाकर्ताविज्ञाता भवत्यथान्नस्यायै द्रष्टा भवति श्रोता भवति मन्ता भवति बोद्धा भवति कर्ता भवति विज्ञाता भवत्यन्नमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.९.१ ॥

*annaṃ vāva balādbhūyastasmādyadyapi daśa rātrīrnāśnīyādyadyu ha jīvedathavādraṣṭāśrotāmantāboddhākartāvijñātā bhavatyathānnasyāyai draṣṭā bhavati śrotā bhavati mantā bhavati boddhā bhavati kartā bhavati vijñātā bhavatyannamupāssveti || 7.9.1 ||*

Food is certainly greater than strength. So if a person goes without food for ten days and nights, he may stay alive, but he will be unable to see, hear, think, understand, work, or fully grasp the meaning of what he is taught. Once he eats, he can see, hear, think, understand, work and fully grasp the meaning of the teachings. So worship food.

### 7.9.2

स योऽन्नं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽन्नवतो वै स लोकान्पानवतोऽभिसिध्यति यावदन्नस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति योऽन्नं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवोऽन्नाद्भूय इत्यन्नाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.९.२ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo'nnaṃ brahmetyupāste'nnavato vai sa lokānpānavato'bhisidhyati yāvadannasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo'nnaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo'nnādbhūya ityannādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.9.2 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships food as Brahman reaches worlds rich in food and drink. Whoever worships food as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as food extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than food?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than food,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.10.1

आपो वावान्नाद्भूयस्तस्माद्यदा सुवृष्टिर्न भवति व्याधीयन्ते प्राणा अन्नं कनीयो भविष्यतीत्यथ यदा सुवृष्टिर्भवत्यानन्दिनः प्राणा भवन्त्यन्नं बहु भविष्यतीत्याप एवेमा मूर्ता येयं पृथिवी यदन्तरिक्षं यद्द्यौर्यत्पर्वता यद्देवमनुष्यायत्पशवश्च वयांसि च तृणवनस्पतयः श्वापदान्याकीटपतङ्गपिपीलकमाप एवेमा मूर्ता अप उपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.१०.१ ॥

*āpo vāvānnādbhūyastasmādyadā suvṛṣṭirna bhavati vyādhīyante prāṇā annaṃ kanīyo bhaviṣyatītyatha yadā suvṛṣṭirbhavatyānandinaḥ prāṇā bhavantyannaṃ bahu bhaviṣyatītyāpa evemā mūrtā yeyaṃ pṛthivī yadantarikṣaṃ yaddyauryatparvatā yaddevamanuṣyāyatpaśavaśca vayāṃsi ca tṛṇavanaspatayaḥ śvāpadānyākīṭapataṅgapipīlakamāpa evemā mūrtā apa upāssveti || 7.10.1 ||*

Water is certainly greater than food. So when no rain comes, people grow anxious, thinking, ‘There will not be enough food.’ When rain falls well, they rejoice, thinking, ‘There will be plenty of food.’ The earth, the interspace, heaven, the mountains, gods and human beings, cattle and birds, creepers and trees, beasts of prey, worms, insects and ants: all of these are water in different forms. So worship water.

### 7.10.2

स योऽपो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्त आप्नोति सर्वान्कामांस्तृप्तिमान्भवति यावदपां गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति योऽपो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवोऽद्भ्यो भूय इत्यद्भ्यो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.१०.२ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*sa yo'po brahmetyupāsta āpnoti sarvānkāmāṃstṛptimānbhavati yāvadapāṃ gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yo'po brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavo'dbhyo bhūya ityadbhyo vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.10.2 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships water as Brahman gains all he desires and is happy. Whoever worships water as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as water extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than water?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than water,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.11.1

तेजो वावाद्भ्यो भूयस्तद्वा एतद्वायुमागृह्याकाशमभितपति तदाहुर्निशोचति नितपति वर्षिष्यति वा इति तेज एव तत्पूर्वं दर्शयित्वाथापः सृजते तदेतदूर्ध्वाभिश्च तिरश्चीभिश्च विद्युद्भिराह्रादाश्चरन्ति तस्मादाहुर्विद्योतते स्तनयति वर्षिष्यति वा इति तेज एव तत्पूर्वं दर्शयित्वाथापः सृजते तेज उपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.११.१ ॥

*tejo vāvādbhyo bhūyastadvā etadvāyumāgṛhyākāśamabhitapati tadāhurniśocati nitapati varṣiṣyati vā iti teja eva tatpūrvaṃ darśayitvāthāpaḥ sṛjate tadetadūrdhvābhiśca tiraścībhiśca vidyudbhirāhrādāścaranti tasmādāhurvidyotate stanayati varṣiṣyati vā iti teja eva tatpūrvaṃ darśayitvāthāpaḥ sṛjate teja upāssveti || 7.11.1 ||*

Fire [or heat] is certainly better than water. Resting on air for support, that fire heats the sky. Then people say, ‘It is very hot. The body is burning. Rain will come soon.’ Fire first produces these signs and then brings the rain. So lightning flashes straight up or zigzags sideways, with thunder accompanying it, and people say, ‘There is lightning and thunder. Rain will come soon.’

### 7.11.2

स यस्तेजो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते तेजस्वी वै स तेजस्वतो लोकान्भास्वतोऽपहततमस्कानभिसिध्यति यावत्तेजसो गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यस्तेजो ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवस्तेजसो भूय इति तेजसो वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.११.२ ॥ ॥ इति एकादशः खण्डः ॥

*sa yastejo brahmetyupāste tejasvī vai sa tejasvato lokānbhāsvato'pahatatamaskānabhisidhyati yāvattejaso gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yastejo brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavastejaso bhūya iti tejaso vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.11.2 || || iti ekādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships fire as Brahman becomes bright himself and reaches worlds that are bright, shining and without a trace of darkness. Whoever worships fire as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as fire extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than tejas?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than tejas,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.12.1

आकाशो वाव तेजसो भूयानाकाशे वै सूर्याचन्द्रमसावुभौ विद्युन्नक्षत्राण्यग्निराकाशेनाह्वयत्याकाशेन शृणोत्याकाशेन प्रतिशृणोत्याकाशे रमत आकाशे न रमत आकाशे जायत आकाशमभिजायत आकाशमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.१२.१ ॥

*ākāśo vāva tejaso bhūyānākāśe vai sūryācandramasāvubhau vidyunnakṣatrāṇyagnirākāśenāhvayatyākāśena śṛṇotyākāśena pratiśṛṇotyākāśe ramata ākāśe na ramata ākāśe jāyata ākāśamabhijāyata ākāśamupāssveti || 7.12.1 ||*

Ākāśa [space] is certainly greater than fire. Within ākāśa are the sun and the moon, and lightning, the stars and fire as well. Through ākāśa one person can speak to another. Through ākāśa one can hear, and through ākāśa one can hear what others are saying. In ākāśa one enjoys, and in ākāśa one suffers. In ākāśa a person is born, and toward ākāśa plants and trees grow. Worship ākāśa.

### 7.12.2

स य आकाशं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्त आकाशवतो वै स लोकान्प्रकाशवतोऽसंबाधानुरुगायवतोऽभिसिध्यति यावदाकाशस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति य आकाशं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगव आकाशाद्भूय इति आकाशाद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.१२.२ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya ākāśaṃ brahmetyupāsta ākāśavato vai sa lokānprakāśavato'saṃbādhānurugāyavato'bhisidhyati yāvadākāśasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati ya ākāśaṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagava ākāśādbhūya iti ākāśādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.12.2 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships ākāśa [space] as Brahman reaches worlds that are spacious, shining, free of all defects, and wide. Whoever worships ākāśa as Brahman is free to do as he wishes as far as ākāśa extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than ākāśa?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than ākāśa,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.13.1

स्मरो वावाकाशाद्भूयस्तस्माद्यद्यपि बहव आसीरन्न स्मरन्तो नैव ते कंचन शृणुयुर्न मन्वीरन्न विजानीरन्यदा वाव ते स्मरेयुरथ शृणुयुरथ मन्वीरन्नथ विजानीरन्स्मरेण वै पुत्रान्विजानाति स्मरेण पशून्स्मरमुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.१३.१ ॥

*smaro vāvākāśādbhūyastasmādyadyapi bahava āsīranna smaranto naiva te kaṃcana śṛṇuyurna manvīranna vijānīranyadā vāva te smareyuratha śṛṇuyuratha manvīrannatha vijānīransmareṇa vai putrānvijānāti smareṇa paśūnsmaramupāssveti || 7.13.1 ||*

Memory is certainly greater than ākāśa [space]. So if many people gather together and their memory fails, they can neither hear nor think nor know anything. If they remember, they can hear, think and know. It is through memory that one recognises one's children and animals. So worship memory.

### 7.13.2

स यः स्मरं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्ते यावत्स्मरस्य गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति यः स्मरं ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगवः स्मराद्भूय इति स्मराद्वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.१३.२ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥

*sa yaḥ smaraṃ brahmetyupāste yāvatsmarasya gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati yaḥ smaraṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagavaḥ smarādbhūya iti smarādvāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.13.2 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

‘Whoever worships memory as Brahman moves freely as far as memory extends.’ Nārada asked, ‘Sir, is anything higher than memory?’ ‘Certainly there is something higher than memory,’ Sanatkumāra answered. Nārada then said, ‘Sir, please explain it to me.’

### 7.14.1

आशा वाव स्मराद्भूयस्याशेद्धो वै स्मरो मन्त्रानधीते कर्माणि कुरुते पुत्रांश्च पशूंश्चेच्छत इमं च लोकममुं चेच्छत आशामुपास्स्वेति ॥ ७.१४.१ ॥

*āśā vāva smarādbhūyasyāśeddho vai smaro mantrānadhīte karmāṇi kurute putrāṃśca paśūṃścecchata imaṃ ca lokamamuṃ cecchata āśāmupāssveti || 7.14.1 ||*

Hope is certainly better than memory. Hope stirs a person's memory, and with memory one learns the mantras and performs rituals. One then longs for children and animals, and longs also to attain this world and the next. So worship hope.

### 7.14.2

स य आशां ब्रह्मेत्युपास्त आशयास्य सर्वे कामाः समृध्यन्त्यमोघा हास्याशिषो भवन्ति यावदाशाया गतं तत्रास्य यथाकामचारो भवति य आशां ब्रह्मेत्युपास्तेऽस्ति भगव आशाया भूय इत्याशाया वाव भूयोऽस्तीति तन्मे भगवान्ब्रवीत्विति ॥ ७.१४.२ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्दशः खण्डः ॥

*sa ya āśāṃ brahmetyupāsta āśayāsya sarve kāmāḥ samṛdhyantyamoghā hāsyāśiṣo bhavanti yāvadāśāyā gataṃ tatrāsya yathākāmacāro bhavati ya āśāṃ brahmetyupāste'sti bhagava āśāyā bhūya ityāśāyā vāva bhūyo'stīti tanme bhagavānbravītviti || 7.14.2 || || iti caturdaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

'Whoever worships hope as Brahman has every desire fulfilled and unfailingly receives whatever he wants. As far as hope reaches, he can move freely.' Nārada asked, 'Sir, is there anything higher than hope?' Sanatkumāra answered, 'Certainly there is something higher than hope.' Nārada said, 'Sir, please explain it to me.'

### 7.15.1

प्राणो वा आशाया भूयान्यथा वा अरा नाभौ समर्पिता एवमस्मिन्प्राणे सर्वंसमर्पितं प्राणः प्राणेन याति प्राणः प्राणं ददाति प्राणाय ददाति प्राणो ह पिता प्राणो माता प्राणो भ्राता प्राणः स्वसा प्राण आचार्यः प्राणो ब्राह्मणः ॥ ७.१५.१ ॥

*prāṇo vā āśāyā bhūyānyathā vā arā nābhau samarpitā evamasminprāṇe sarvaṃsamarpitaṃ prāṇaḥ prāṇena yāti prāṇaḥ prāṇaṃ dadāti prāṇāya dadāti prāṇo ha pitā prāṇo mātā prāṇo bhrātā prāṇaḥ svasā prāṇa ācāryaḥ prāṇo brāhmaṇaḥ || 7.15.1 ||*

Prāṇa [the vital force] is certainly greater than hope. Everything rests on prāṇa, the way spokes are fixed to the hub of a wheel. Prāṇa operates by its own power [that is, it is both the means and the end]. Prāṇa gives prāṇa to prāṇa, and prāṇa directs prāṇa to prāṇa. Prāṇa is father, mother, brother, sister, teacher, and brāhmin.

### 7.15.2

स यदि पितरं वा मातरं वा भ्रातरं वा स्वसारं वाचार्यं वा ब्राह्मणं वा किंचिद्भृशमिव प्रत्याह धिक्त्वास्त्वित्येवैनमाहुः पितृहा वै त्वमसि मातृहा वै त्वमसि भ्रातृहा वै त्वमसि स्वसृहा वै त्वमस्याचार्यहा वै त्वमसि ब्राह्मणहा वै त्वमसीति ॥ ७.१५.२ ॥

*sa yadi pitaraṃ vā mātaraṃ vā bhrātaraṃ vā svasāraṃ vācāryaṃ vā brāhmaṇaṃ vā kiṃcidbhṛśamiva pratyāha dhiktvāstvityevainamāhuḥ pitṛhā vai tvamasi mātṛhā vai tvamasi bhrātṛhā vai tvamasi svasṛhā vai tvamasyācāryahā vai tvamasi brāhmaṇahā vai tvamasīti || 7.15.2 ||*

If someone speaks rudely to his father, mother, brother, sister, teacher, or a brāhmin, people tell him: 'Shame on you! You have murdered your father, your mother, your brother, your sister, your teacher, a brāhmin.'

### 7.15.3

अथ यद्यप्येनानुत्क्रान्तप्राणाञ्छूलेन समासं व्यतिषंदहेन्नैवैनं ब्रूयुः पितृहासीति न मातृहासीति न भ्रातृहासीति न स्वसृहासीति नाचार्यहासीति न ब्राह्मणहासीति ॥ ७.१५.३ ॥

*atha yadyapyenānutkrāntaprāṇāñchūlena samāsaṃ vyatiṣaṃdahennaivainaṃ brūyuḥ pitṛhāsīti na mātṛhāsīti na bhrātṛhāsīti na svasṛhāsīti nācāryahāsīti na brāhmaṇahāsīti || 7.15.3 ||*

Yet once they have died, a person may heap their bodies on a funeral pyre and burn them, piercing them with a spear [to make the body burn faster], and no one will tell him he has killed his father, mother, brother, sister, teacher, or a brāhmin.

### 7.15.4

प्राणो ह्येवैतानि सर्वाणि भवति स वा एष एवं पश्यन्नेवं मन्वान एवं विजानन्नतिवादी भवति तं चेद्ब्रूयुरतिवाद्यसीत्यतिवाद्यस्मीति ब्रूयान्नापह्नुवीत ॥ ७.१५.४ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चदशः खण्डः ॥

*prāṇo hyevaitāni sarvāṇi bhavati sa vā eṣa evaṃ paśyannevaṃ manvāna evaṃ vijānannativādī bhavati taṃ cedbrūyurativādyasītyativādyasmīti brūyānnāpahnuvīta || 7.15.4 || || iti pañcadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

All this is indeed prāṇa. Whoever sees this, thinks this, and knows this becomes a superior speaker. If someone tells him, 'You are a superior speaker,' he can say, 'Yes, I am.' He has no need to deny it.

### 7.16.1

एष तु वा अतिवदति यः सत्येनातिवदति सोऽहं भगवः सत्येनातिवदानीति सत्यं त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्यमिति सत्यं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.१६.१ ॥ ॥ इति षोडशः खण्डः ॥

*eṣa tu vā ativadati yaḥ satyenātivadati so'haṃ bhagavaḥ satyenātivadānīti satyaṃ tveva vijijñāsitavyamiti satyaṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.16.1 || || iti ṣoḍaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

'But a person must first know the Truth; only then is he truly an ativādī.' Nārada said, 'Sir, I want to become an ativādī by knowing the Truth.' Sanatkumāra answered, 'But one must earnestly desire to know the Truth.' Nārada said, 'Sir, I do earnestly desire to know the Truth.'

### 7.17.1

यदा वै विजानात्यथ सत्यं वदति नाविजानन्सत्यं वदति विजानन्नेव सत्यं वदति विज्ञानं त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्यमिति विज्ञानं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.१७.१ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तदशः खण्डः ॥

*yadā vai vijānātyatha satyaṃ vadati nāvijānansatyaṃ vadati vijānanneva satyaṃ vadati vijñānaṃ tveva vijijñāsitavyamiti vijñānaṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.17.1 || || iti saptadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'Only when a person knows with certainty can he truly speak of the Truth. Without knowing well, he cannot. The one who speaks of Truth is the one who knows with certainty. But one must seek knowledge in depth.' Nārada said, 'Sir, I seek knowledge in depth.'

### 7.18.1

यदा वै मनुतेऽथ विजानाति नामत्वा विजानाति मत्वैव विजानाति मतिस्त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्येति मतिं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.१८.१ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टादशः खण्डः ॥

*yadā vai manute'tha vijānāti nāmatvā vijānāti matvaiva vijānāti matistveva vijijñāsitavyeti matiṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.18.1 || || iti aṣṭādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'Deep knowing comes when a person has learned to think well. Without good thinking, no one can know deeply. Certain knowledge comes through deep thought. But one must want to learn how to think well.' Nārada answered, 'Sir, I want to learn how to think well.'

### 7.19.1

यदा वै श्रद्दधात्यथ मनुते नाश्रद्दधन्मनुते श्रद्दधदेव मनुते श्रद्धा त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्येति श्रद्धां भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.१९.१ ॥ ॥ इति एकोनविंशतितमः खण्डः ॥

*yadā vai śraddadhātyatha manute nāśraddadhanmanute śraddadhadeva manute śraddhā tveva vijijñāsitavyeti śraddhāṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.19.1 || || iti ekonaviṃśatitamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'When a person has respect for what he hears, he gives it proper thought. Without this respect he places no value on what he hears. One thinks deeply about what one respects. But one must try to attain this respect.' Nārada answered, 'Sir, I want to have this respect.'

### 7.20.1

यदा वै निस्तिष्ठत्यथ श्रद्दधाति नानिस्तिष्ठञ्छ्रद्दधाति निस्तिष्ठन्नेव श्रद्दधाति निष्ठा त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्येति निष्ठां भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.२०.१ ॥ ॥ इति विंशतितमः खण्डः ॥

*yadā vai nistiṣṭhatyatha śraddadhāti nānistiṣṭhañchraddadhāti nistiṣṭhanneva śraddadhāti niṣṭhā tveva vijijñāsitavyeti niṣṭhāṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.20.1 || || iti viṃśatitamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'A person has respect when he is steady and devoted to his teacher. Without steadiness, respect is impossible. Steadiness is present when respect and devotion are genuine. But one must seek this steadiness very earnestly.' Nārada answered, 'I seek this steadiness.'

### 7.21.1

यदा वै करोत्यथ निस्तिष्ठति नाकृत्वा निस्तिष्ठति कृत्वैव निस्तिष्ठति कृतिस्त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्येति कृतिं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.२१.१ ॥ ॥ इति एकविंशः खण्डः ॥

*yadā vai karotyatha nistiṣṭhati nākṛtvā nistiṣṭhati kṛtvaiva nistiṣṭhati kṛtistveva vijijñāsitavyeti kṛtiṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.21.1 || || iti ekaviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'A person grows steady by continuing to do his duty. Whoever does not do his duty cannot have steadiness. Steadiness is gained by doing one's duty. But one should try to learn what duty means.' Nārada answered, 'Sir, I want to know about duty.'

### 7.22.1

यदा वै सुखं लभतेऽथ करोति नासुखं लब्ध्वा करोति सुखमेव लब्ध्वा करोति सुखं त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्यमिति सुखं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.२२.१ ॥ ॥ इति द्वाविंशः खण्डः ॥
