*yadā vai sukhaṃ labhate'tha karoti nāsukhaṃ labdhvā karoti sukhameva labdhvā karoti sukhaṃ tveva vijijñāsitavyamiti sukhaṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.22.1 || || iti dvāviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'A person works when it brings him happiness. Without happiness he has no interest in working. It is by finding happiness that one does one's duty. But one must try to understand the true nature of this happiness.' Nārada answered, 'Sir, I want to know the true nature of happiness well.'

### 7.23.1

यो वै भूमा तत्सुखं नाल्पे सुखमस्ति भूमैव सुखं भूमा त्वेव विजिज्ञासितव्य इति भूमानं भगवो विजिज्ञास इति ॥ ७.२३.१ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोविंशः खण्डः ॥

*yo vai bhūmā tatsukhaṃ nālpe sukhamasti bhūmaiva sukhaṃ bhūmā tveva vijijñāsitavya iti bhūmānaṃ bhagavo vijijñāsa iti || 7.23.1 || || iti trayoviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'The infinite is the source of happiness. In the finite there is no happiness; happiness exists only in the infinite. But one must try to understand what the infinite is.' Nārada answered, 'Sir, I want to understand the infinite clearly.'

### 7.24.1

यत्र नान्यत्पश्यति नान्यच्छृणोति नान्यद्विजानाति स भूमाथ यत्रान्यत्पश्यत्यन्यच्छृणोत्यन्यद्विजानाति तदल्पं यो वै भूमा तदमृतमथ यदल्पं तन्मर्त्य्ं स भगवः कस्मिन्प्रतिष्ठित इति स्वे महिम्नि यदि वा न महिम्नीति ॥ ७.२४.१ ॥

*yatra nānyatpaśyati nānyacchṛṇoti nānyadvijānāti sa bhūmātha yatrānyatpaśyatyanyacchṛṇotyanyadvijānāti tadalpaṃ yo vai bhūmā tadamṛtamatha yadalpaṃ tanmartyṃ sa bhagavaḥ kasminpratiṣṭhita iti sve mahimni yadi vā na mahimnīti || 7.24.1 ||*

Sanatkumāra said: 'Bhūmā [the infinite] is that in which one sees nothing else, hears nothing else, and knows [that is, finds] nothing else. Alpa [the finite] is that in which one sees, hears, and knows something else. The infinite is immortal; the finite is mortal.' Nārada asked, 'Sir, on what does bhūmā rest?' Sanatkumāra answered, 'On its own power, or not even on that [that is, it depends on nothing else].'

### 7.24.2

गोअश्वमिह महिमेत्याचक्षते हस्तिहिरण्यं दासभार्यं क्षेत्राण्यायतनानीति नाहमेवं ब्रवीमि ब्रवीमीति होवाचान्योह्यन्यस्मिन्प्रतिष्ठित इति ॥ ७.२४.२ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्विंशः खण्डः ॥

*goaśvamiha mahimetyācakṣate hastihiraṇyaṃ dāsabhāryaṃ kṣetrāṇyāyatanānīti nāhamevaṃ bravīmi bravīmīti hovācānyohyanyasminpratiṣṭhita iti || 7.24.2 || || iti caturviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

People in this world call cattle, horses, elephants, gold, servants, wives, farmland, and houses a person's glory. That is not the glory I mean, because these things are not independent of one another. This is what I am speaking of:

### 7.25.1

स एवाधस्तात्स उपरिष्टात्स पश्चात्स पुरस्तात्स दक्षिणतः स उत्तरतः स एवेदं सर्वमित्यथातोऽहंकारादेश एवाहमेवाधस्तादहमुपरिष्टादहं पश्चादहं पुरस्तादहं दक्षिणतोऽहमुत्तरतोऽहमेवेदं सर्वमिति ॥ ७.२५.१ ॥

*sa evādhastātsa upariṣṭātsa paścātsa purastātsa dakṣiṇataḥ sa uttarataḥ sa evedaṃ sarvamityathāto'haṃkārādeśa evāhamevādhastādahamupariṣṭādahaṃ paścādahaṃ purastādahaṃ dakṣiṇato'hamuttarato'hamevedaṃ sarvamiti || 7.25.1 ||*

That bhūmā is below and above, behind and in front, to the right and to the left. All this is bhūmā. Now, regarding one's own identity: I am below and above, behind and in front, to the right and to the left. I am all this.

### 7.25.2

अथात आत्मादेश एवात्मैवाधस्तादात्मोपरिष्टादात्मा पश्चादात्मा पुरस्तादात्मा दक्षिणत आत्मोत्तरत आत्मैवेदं सर्वमिति स वा एष एवं पश्यन्नेवं मन्वान एवं विजानन्नात्मरतिरात्मक्रीड आत्ममिथुन आत्मानन्दः स स्वराड्भवति तस्य सर्वेषु लोकेषु कामचारो भवति अथ येऽन्यथातो विदुरन्यराजानस्ते क्षय्यलोका भवन्ति तेषां सर्वेषु लोकेष्वकामचारो भवति ॥ ७.२५.२ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चविंशः खण्डः ॥

*athāta ātmādeśa evātmaivādhastādātmopariṣṭādātmā paścādātmā purastādātmā dakṣiṇata ātmottarata ātmaivedaṃ sarvamiti sa vā eṣa evaṃ paśyannevaṃ manvāna evaṃ vijānannātmaratirātmakrīḍa ātmamithuna ātmānandaḥ sa svarāḍbhavati tasya sarveṣu lokeṣu kāmacāro bhavati atha ye'nyathāto viduranyarājānaste kṣayyalokā bhavanti teṣāṃ sarveṣu lokeṣvakāmacāro bhavati || 7.25.2 || || iti pañcaviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Next comes the teaching on the Self. The Self is below and above, behind and in front, to the right and to the left. The Self is all this. Whoever sees, thinks, and knows in this way, who loves the Self, plays with the Self, enjoys the Self's company, and finds joy in the Self, is supreme and can go as he wishes through all the worlds. Those who think otherwise are ruled by others. They cannot stay in the worlds they inhabit, nor move through the worlds as they wish [that is, they live under many limitations].

### 7.26.1

तस्य ह वा एतस्यैवं पश्यत एवं मन्वानस्यैवं विजानत आत्मतः प्राण आत्मत आशात्मतः स्मर आत्मत आकाश आत्मतस्तेज आत्मत आप आत्मत आविर्भावतिरोभावावात्मतोऽन्नमात्मतो बलमात्मतो विज्ञानमात्मतो ध्यानमात्मतश्चित्तमात्मतः संकल्प आत्मतो मन आत्मतो वागात्मतो नामात्मतो मन्त्रा आत्मतः कर्माण्यात्मत एवेदंसर्वमिति ॥ ७.२६.१ ॥

*tasya ha vā etasyaivaṃ paśyata evaṃ manvānasyaivaṃ vijānata ātmataḥ prāṇa ātmata āśātmataḥ smara ātmata ākāśa ātmatasteja ātmata āpa ātmata āvirbhāvatirobhāvāvātmato'nnamātmato balamātmato vijñānamātmato dhyānamātmataścittamātmataḥ saṃkalpa ātmato mana ātmato vāgātmato nāmātmato mantrā ātmataḥ karmāṇyātmata evedaṃsarvamiti || 7.26.1 ||*

For the person who sees and thinks this way and has this knowledge, everything arises from the Self: life, hope, memory, space, fire, water, birth and death, food, strength, knowledge in depth, meditation, the heart, resolution, the mind, speech, name, mantras, and all work. All this comes from the Self.

### 7.26.2

तदेष श्लोको न पश्यो मृत्युं पश्यति न रोगं नोत दुःखतां सर्वं ह पश्यः पश्यति सर्वमाप्नोति सर्वश इति स एकधा भवति त्रिधा भवति पञ्चधा सप्तधा नवधा चैव पुनश्चैकादशः स्मृतः शतं च दश चैकश्च सहस्राणि च विंशतिराहारशुद्धौ सत्त्वशुद्धौ ध्रुवा स्मृतिः स्मृतिलम्भे सर्वग्रन्थीनां विप्रमोक्षस्तस्मै मृदितकषायाय तमसस्पारं दर्शयति भगवान्सनत्कुमारस्तं स्कन्द इत्याचक्षते तं स्कन्द इत्याचक्षते ॥ ७.२६.२ ॥ ॥ इति षड्विंशः खण्डः ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमोऽध्यायः ॥

*tadeṣa śloko na paśyo mṛtyuṃ paśyati na rogaṃ nota duḥkhatāṃ sarvaṃ ha paśyaḥ paśyati sarvamāpnoti sarvaśa iti sa ekadhā bhavati tridhā bhavati pañcadhā saptadhā navadhā caiva punaścaikādaśaḥ smṛtaḥ śataṃ ca daśa caikaśca sahasrāṇi ca viṃśatirāhāraśuddhau sattvaśuddhau dhruvā smṛtiḥ smṛtilambhe sarvagranthīnāṃ vipramokṣastasmai mṛditakaṣāyāya tamasaspāraṃ darśayati bhagavānsanatkumārastaṃ skanda ityācakṣate taṃ skanda ityācakṣate || 7.26.2 || || iti ṣaḍviṃśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ || || iti saptamo'dhyāyaḥ ||*

A verse on this subject says: 'One who has realized the Self does not see death, and knows no disease or sorrow. Such a seer sees everything as it is and attains everything in whatever way he wishes.' Before creation he is one; after creation he takes three, five, seven, and nine forms, and again eleven, a hundred and ten, even a thousand and twenty forms. Eating pure food purifies the mind. A pure mind makes memory strong and steady. Good memory frees a person from all bondage. The revered Sanatkumāra freed Nārada from all his shortcomings and led him beyond darkness [that is, ignorance]. The wise say that Sanatkumāra is a man of perfect knowledge.

### 8.1.1

॥ अष्टमोऽध्यायः ॥ अथ यदिदमस्मिन्ब्रह्मपुरे दहरं पुण्डरीकं वेश्म दहरोऽस्मिन्नन्तराकाशस्तस्मिन्यदन्तस्तदन्वेष्टव्यं तद्वाव विजिज्ञासितव्यमिति ॥ ८.१.१ ॥

*|| aṣṭamo'dhyāyaḥ || atha yadidamasminbrahmapure daharaṃ puṇḍarīkaṃ veśma daharo'sminnantarākāśastasminyadantastadanveṣṭavyaṃ tadvāva vijijñāsitavyamiti || 8.1.1 ||*

Om. This body is the city of Brahman. In it is a dwelling shaped like a lotus [the heart], and inside that is a small space. One must search within this space and earnestly desire to know what is there.

### 8.1.2

तं चेद्ब्रूयुर्यदिदमस्मिन्ब्रह्मपुरे दहरं पुण्डरीकं वेश्म दहरोऽस्मिन्नन्तराकाशः किं तदत्र विद्यते यदन्वेष्टव्यं यद्वाव विजिज्ञासितव्यमिति स ब्रूयात् ॥ ८.१.२ ॥

*taṃ cedbrūyuryadidamasminbrahmapure daharaṃ puṇḍarīkaṃ veśma daharo'sminnantarākāśaḥ kiṃ tadatra vidyate yadanveṣṭavyaṃ yadvāva vijijñāsitavyamiti sa brūyāt || 8.1.2 ||*

Suppose the disciples ask: 'This body is the city of Brahman, in it is a dwelling shaped like a lotus [the heart], and inside that is a small space. What is to be searched for in this space, and what should one earnestly desire to know?' The teacher should answer:

### 8.1.3

यावान्वा अयमाकाशस्तावानेषोऽन्तर्हृदय अकाश उभे अस्मिन्द्यावापृथिवी अन्तरेव समाहिते उभावग्निश्च वायुश्च सूर्याचन्द्रमसावुभौ विद्युन्नक्षत्राणि यच्चास्येहास्ति यच्च नास्ति सर्वं तदस्मिन्समाहितमिति ॥ ८.१.३ ॥

*yāvānvā ayamākāśastāvāneṣo'ntarhṛdaya akāśa ubhe asmindyāvāpṛthivī antareva samāhite ubhāvagniśca vāyuśca sūryācandramasāvubhau vidyunnakṣatrāṇi yaccāsyehāsti yacca nāsti sarvaṃ tadasminsamāhitamiti || 8.1.3 ||*

[The teacher replies:] 'The space within the heart is as large as the space outside. Heaven and earth are both inside it, as are fire and air, sun and moon, lightning and stars. Everything is held within that space in the embodied self, whatever it has and whatever it does not have.'

### 8.1.4

तं चेद्ब्रूयुरस्मिंश्चेदिदं ब्रह्मपुरे सर्वं समाहितं सर्वाणि च भूतानि सर्वे च कामा यदैतज्जरा वाप्नोति प्रध्वंसते वा किं ततोऽतिशिष्यत इति ॥ ८.१.४ ॥

*taṃ cedbrūyurasmiṃścedidaṃ brahmapure sarvaṃ samāhitaṃ sarvāṇi ca bhūtāni sarve ca kāmā yadaitajjarā vāpnoti pradhvaṃsate vā kiṃ tato'tiśiṣyata iti || 8.1.4 ||*

Suppose the disciples ask the teacher: 'If this body [brahmapura] contains all this, all things and all desires, what remains when the body grows old or perishes?'

### 8.1.5

स ब्रूयात्नास्य जरयैतज्जीर्यति न वधेनास्य हन्यत एतत्सत्यं ब्रह्मपुरमस्मिकामाः समाहिताः एष आत्मापहतपाप्मा विजरो विमृत्युर्विशोको विजिघत्सोऽपिपासः सत्यकामः सत्यसंकल्पो यथा ह्येवेह प्रजा अन्वाविशन्ति यथानुशासनम् यं यमन्तमभिकामा भवन्ति यं जनपदं यं क्षेत्रभागं तं तमेवोपजीवन्ति ॥ ८.१.५ ॥

*sa brūyātnāsya jarayaitajjīryati na vadhenāsya hanyata etatsatyaṃ brahmapuramasmikāmāḥ samāhitāḥ eṣa ātmāpahatapāpmā vijaro vimṛtyurviśoko vijighatso'pipāsaḥ satyakāmaḥ satyasaṃkalpo yathā hyeveha prajā anvāviśanti yathānuśāsanam yaṃ yamantamabhikāmā bhavanti yaṃ janapadaṃ yaṃ kṣetrabhāgaṃ taṃ tamevopajīvanti || 8.1.5 ||*

The teacher will answer: 'Old age may wear down the body, but the space within [brahmapura] never wears out, and it does not perish when the body dies. This is the true abode of Brahman. All our desires are gathered in it. It is the Self, free from all sins and from old age, death, bereavement, hunger, and thirst. It gives rise to love of Truth and dedication to Truth. If a person strictly obeys every command of his country's ruler, he may be rewarded with some land or even an estate.'

### 8.1.6

तद्यथेह कर्मजितो लोकः क्षीयत एवमेवामुत्र पुण्यजितो लोकः क्षीयते तद्य इहात्मानमनुविद्य व्रजन्त्येतांश्च सत्यान्कामांस्तेषां सर्वेषु लोकेष्वकामचारो भवत्यथ य इहात्मानमनिवुद्य व्रजन्त्येतंश्च सत्यान्कामांस्तेषां सर्वेषु लोकेषु कामचारो भवति ॥ ८.१.६ ॥ ॥ इति प्रथमः खण्डः ॥

*tadyatheha karmajito lokaḥ kṣīyata evamevāmutra puṇyajito lokaḥ kṣīyate tadya ihātmānamanuvidya vrajantyetāṃśca satyānkāmāṃsteṣāṃ sarveṣu lokeṣvakāmacāro bhavatyatha ya ihātmānamanivudya vrajantyetaṃśca satyānkāmāṃsteṣāṃ sarveṣu lokeṣu kāmacāro bhavati || 8.1.6 || || iti prathamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Everything perishes, whether earned through hard work in this world or a place in the next world earned through meritorious deeds. People who depart from this world without knowing the Self and the Truths they ought to know are not free, wherever they go. Those who depart knowing the Self and those Truths are free, wherever they are.

### 8.2.1

स यदि पितृलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य पितरः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन पितृलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.१ ॥

*sa yadi pitṛlokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya pitaraḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena pitṛlokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.1 ||*

If that person wishes to be with his forefathers, they appear before him as he wishes. Joining them in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.2

अथ यदि मातृलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य मातरः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन मातृलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.२ ॥

*atha yadi mātṛlokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya mātaraḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena mātṛlokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.2 ||*

If he then wishes to be with mothers, they appear before him as he wishes. Joining them in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.3

अथ यदि भ्रातृलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य भ्रातरः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन भ्रातृलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.३ ॥

*atha yadi bhrātṛlokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya bhrātaraḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena bhrātṛlokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.3 ||*

If he then wishes to be with brothers, they appear before him as he wishes. Joining them in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.4

अथ यदि स्वसृलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य स्वसारः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन स्वसृलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.४ ॥

*atha yadi svasṛlokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya svasāraḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena svasṛlokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.4 ||*

If he then wishes to be with sisters, they appear before him as he wishes. Joining them in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.5

अथ यदि सखिलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य सखायः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन सखिलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.५ ॥

*atha yadi sakhilokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya sakhāyaḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena sakhilokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.5 ||*

If he then wishes to be with friends, they appear before him as he wishes. Joining them in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.6

अथ यदि गन्धमाल्यलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य गन्धमाल्ये समुत्तिष्ठतस्तेन गन्धमाल्यलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.६ ॥

*atha yadi gandhamālyalokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya gandhamālye samuttiṣṭhatastena gandhamālyalokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.6 ||*

If he then wishes for a world of fragrant flower garlands, they appear before him as he wishes. Having those garlands in that world, he becomes great.

### 8.2.7

अथ यद्यन्नपानलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्यान्नपाने समुत्तिष्ठतस्तेनान्नपानलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.७ ॥

*atha yadyannapānalokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsyānnapāne samuttiṣṭhatastenānnapānalokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.7 ||*

Should he desire a world of food and drink, it comes to him by his wish alone. Supplied with food and drink in that world, he grows great.

### 8.2.8

अथ यदि गीतवादित्रलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य गीतवादित्रे समुत्तिष्ठतस्तेन गीतवादित्रलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.८ ॥

*atha yadi gītavāditralokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya gītavāditre samuttiṣṭhatastena gītavāditralokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.8 ||*

If he then wishes for a world of music, that world appears to him as he wishes. Enjoying music there, he becomes great.

### 8.2.9

अथ यदि स्त्रीलोककामो भवति संकल्पादेवास्य स्त्रियः समुत्तिष्ठन्ति तेन स्त्रीलोकेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.९ ॥

*atha yadi strīlokakāmo bhavati saṃkalpādevāsya striyaḥ samuttiṣṭhanti tena strīlokena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.9 ||*

Should he desire the company of women, they come to him by his wish alone. In their company in that world, he grows great.

### 8.2.10

यं यमन्तमभिकामो भवति यं कामं कामयते सोऽस्य संकल्पादेव समुत्तिष्ठति तेन सम्पन्नो महीयते ॥ ८.२.१० ॥ ॥ इति द्वितीयः खण्डः ॥

*yaṃ yamantamabhikāmo bhavati yaṃ kāmaṃ kāmayate so'sya saṃkalpādeva samuttiṣṭhati tena sampanno mahīyate || 8.2.10 || || iti dvitīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Any region he longs for, any good thing he wants to have, comes to him exactly as he wishes. When he gains it, he grows great.

### 8.3.1

त इमे सत्याः कामा अनृतापिधानास्तेषां सत्यानां सतामनृतमपिधानं यो यो ह्यस्येतः प्रैति न तमिह दर्शनाय लभते ॥ ८.३.१ ॥

*ta ime satyāḥ kāmā anṛtāpidhānāsteṣāṃ satyānāṃ satāmanṛtamapidhānaṃ yo yo hyasyetaḥ praiti na tamiha darśanāya labhate || 8.3.1 ||*

But all these true desires lie beneath a false covering. Though they rest on the Self, they are all false. That is why, when a relative dies, one does not see him again in this world.

### 8.3.2

अथ ये चास्येह जीवा ये च प्रेता यच्चान्यदिच्छन्न लभते सर्वं तदत्र गत्वा विन्दतेऽत्र ह्यस्यैते सत्याः कामा अनृतापिधानास्तद्यथापि हिरण्यनिधिं निहितमक्षेत्रज्ञा उपर्युपरि सञ्चरन्तो न विन्देयुरेवमेवेमाः सर्वाः प्रजा अहरहर्गच्छन्त्य एतं ब्रह्मलोकं न विन्दन्त्यनृतेन हि प्रत्यूढाः ॥ ८.३.२ ॥

*atha ye cāsyeha jīvā ye ca pretā yaccānyadicchanna labhate sarvaṃ tadatra gatvā vindate'tra hyasyaite satyāḥ kāmā anṛtāpidhānāstadyathāpi hiraṇyanidhiṃ nihitamakṣetrajñā uparyupari sañcaranto na vindeyurevamevemāḥ sarvāḥ prajā aharahargacchantya etaṃ brahmalokaṃ na vindantyanṛtena hi pratyūḍhāḥ || 8.3.2 ||*

Further, his relatives who are alive and those who are dead, and whatever else a person cannot get even by wishing, he gets all of these by going within his heart. All of a person's true desires are in the heart, though hidden. It is as if gold were buried somewhere and people unaware of it walked over that spot again and again, knowing nothing of it. In the same way all these beings go to Brahmaloka every day and know nothing of it, because ignorance covers them.

### 8.3.3

स वा एष आत्मा हृदि तस्यैतदेव निरुक्तं हृद्ययमिति तस्माद्धृदयमहरहर्वा एवंवित्स्वर्गं लोकमेति ॥ ८.३.३ ॥

*sa vā eṣa ātmā hṛdi tasyaitadeva niruktaṃ hṛdyayamiti tasmāddhṛdayamaharaharvā evaṃvitsvargaṃ lokameti || 8.3.3 ||*

The Self dwells in the heart. The word hṛdayam comes from hṛdi + ayam, 'it is in the heart', and that is why the heart is called hṛdayam. Whoever knows this goes daily to the heavenly world [that is, in dreamless sleep he is one with Brahman].

### 8.3.4

अथ य एष सम्प्रसादोऽस्माच्छरीरात्समुत्थाय परं ज्योतिरुपसम्पद्य स्वेन रूपेणाभिनिष्पद्यत एष आत्मेति होवाचैतदमृतमभयमेतद्ब्रह्मेति तस्य ह वा एतस्य ब्रह्मणो नाम सत्यमिति ॥ ८.३.४ ॥

*atha ya eṣa samprasādo'smāccharīrātsamutthāya paraṃ jyotirupasampadya svena rūpeṇābhiniṣpadyata eṣa ātmeti hovācaitadamṛtamabhayametadbrahmeti tasya ha vā etasya brahmaṇo nāma satyamiti || 8.3.4 ||*

The teacher said: 'Then this person, the embodiment of happiness, rises out of the body, reaches the highest light, and takes on his real nature. This is the Self. It is immortal and fearless. It is Brahman. Another name for Brahman is satya, Truth.'

### 8.3.5

तानि ह वा एतानि त्रीण्यक्षराणि सतीयमिति तद्यत्सत्तदमृतमथ यत्ति तन्मर्त्यमथ यद्यं तेनोभे यच्छति यदनेनोभे यच्छति तस्माद्यमहरहर्वा एवंवित्स्वर्गं लोकमेति ॥ ८.३.५ ॥ ॥ इति तृतीयः खण्डः ॥

*tāni ha vā etāni trīṇyakṣarāṇi satīyamiti tadyatsattadamṛtamatha yatti tanmartyamatha yadyaṃ tenobhe yacchati yadanenobhe yacchati tasmādyamaharaharvā evaṃvitsvargaṃ lokameti || 8.3.5 || || iti tṛtīyaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Sa, tī, and yam are the three syllables [that represent Brahman]. Sa stands for the immortal, ti for the mortal, and yam for what controls both. Because it controls both the mortal and the immortal, it is called yam. Whoever knows the meaning of these three syllables enjoys divine bliss every day in dreamless sleep.

### 8.4.1

अथ य आत्मा स सेतुर्धृतिरेषां लोकानामसंभेदाय नैतं सेतुमहोरात्रे तरतो न जरा न मृत्युर्न शोको न सुकृतं न दुष्कृतं सर्वे पाप्मानोऽतो निवर्तन्तेऽपहतपाप्मा ह्येष ब्रह्मलोकः ॥ ८.४.१ ॥

*atha ya ātmā sa seturdhṛtireṣāṃ lokānāmasaṃbhedāya naitaṃ setumahorātre tarato na jarā na mṛtyurna śoko na sukṛtaṃ na duṣkṛtaṃ sarve pāpmāno'to nivartante'pahatapāpmā hyeṣa brahmalokaḥ || 8.4.1 ||*

Next, this Self is like a dam. It holds up the worlds and keeps them from running together. Neither day nor night can cross this dam, nor old age, death, bereavement, good actions, or bad actions. All sins turn back from it, because this Brahmaloka is free of evil.

### 8.4.2

तस्माद्वा एतं सेतुं तीर्त्वान्धः सन्ननन्धो भवति विद्धः सन्नविद्धो भवत्युपतापी सन्ननुपतापी भवति तस्माद्वा एतं सेतुं तीर्त्वापि नक्तमहरेवाभिनिष्पद्यते सकृद्विभातो ह्येवैष ब्रह्मलोकः ॥ ८.४.२ ॥

*tasmādvā etaṃ setuṃ tīrtvāndhaḥ sannanandho bhavati viddhaḥ sannaviddho bhavatyupatāpī sannanupatāpī bhavati tasmādvā etaṃ setuṃ tīrtvāpi naktamaharevābhiniṣpadyate sakṛdvibhāto hyevaiṣa brahmalokaḥ || 8.4.2 ||*

So once you cross this dam, if you are blind you do not feel blind. Likewise, if you are hurt you do not feel hurt, and if you are mentally upset the sorrow is gone. That is why, once you cross this dam, even night becomes like day, for this world of Brahman is always full of light.

### 8.4.3

तद्य एवैतं ब्रह्मलोकं ब्रह्मचर्येणानुविन्दन्ति तेषामेवैष ब्रह्मलोकस्तेषां सर्वेषु लोकेषु कामचारो भवति ॥ ८.४.३ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्थः खण्डः ॥

*tadya evaitaṃ brahmalokaṃ brahmacaryeṇānuvindanti teṣāmevaiṣa brahmalokasteṣāṃ sarveṣu lokeṣu kāmacāro bhavati || 8.4.3 || || iti caturthaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Those who reach this Brahmaloka through brahmacarya become its masters and can visit all worlds as they wish.

### 8.5.1

अथ यद्यज्ञ इत्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तद्ब्रह्मचर्येण ह्येव यो ज्ञाता तं विन्दतेऽथ यदिष्टमित्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तद्ब्रह्मचर्येण ह्येवेष्ट्वात्मानमनुविन्दते ॥ ८.५.१ ॥

*atha yadyajña ityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tadbrahmacaryeṇa hyeva yo jñātā taṃ vindate'tha yadiṣṭamityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tadbrahmacaryeṇa hyeveṣṭvātmānamanuvindate || 8.5.1 ||*

What is known as yajña [sacrifice] is really brahmacarya, because the knower of the Self reaches Brahmaloka through brahmacarya. What is known as iṣṭa [worship] is also brahmacarya, since the desired Self is reached through brahmacarya.

### 8.5.2

अथ यत्सत्त्रायणमित्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तद्ब्रह्मचर्येण ह्येव सत आत्मनस्त्राणं विन्दतेऽथ यन्मौनमित्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तब्ब्रह्मचर्येण ह्येवात्मानमनुविद्य मनुते ऽ॥ ८.५.२ ॥

*atha yatsattrāyaṇamityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tadbrahmacaryeṇa hyeva sata ātmanastrāṇaṃ vindate'tha yanmaunamityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tabbrahmacaryeṇa hyevātmānamanuvidya manute || 8.5.2 ||*

What is known as 'Sattrāyaṇa' [a long sacrifice] is brahmacarya, since through brahmacarya the individual self is liberated [attains union with the Cosmic Self]. What is called 'mauna' [silence] is brahmacarya too, since through it one realizes the Self and, having realized It, stays absorbed in thought of It.

### 8.5.3

अथ यदनाशकायनमित्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तदेष ह्यात्मा न नश्यति यं ब्रह्मचर्येणानुविन्दतेऽथ यदरण्यायनमित्याचक्षते ब्रह्मचर्यमेव तदरश्च ह वै ण्यश्चार्णवौ ब्रह्मलोके तृतीयस्यामितो दिवि तदैरं मदीयं सरस्तदश्वत्थः सोमसवनस्तदपराजिता पूर्ब्रह्मणः प्रभुविमितं हिरण्मयम् ॥ ८.५.३ ॥

*atha yadanāśakāyanamityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tadeṣa hyātmā na naśyati yaṃ brahmacaryeṇānuvindate'tha yadaraṇyāyanamityācakṣate brahmacaryameva tadaraśca ha vai ṇyaścārṇavau brahmaloke tṛtīyasyāmito divi tadairaṃ madīyaṃ sarastadaśvatthaḥ somasavanastadaparājitā pūrbrahmaṇaḥ prabhuvimitaṃ hiraṇmayam || 8.5.3 ||*

What is known as 'anāśakāyana' [the path of fasting] is brahmacarya, since through it one reaches the immortal Self. What is called 'araṇyāyana' [life in the forest] is brahmacarya as well. For in Brahmaloka, the third world from the earth, are two oceans named Ara and Ṇya. There too is a lake called Airammadīya [so named because its waters intoxicate], a peepal tree ever dripping soma juice, Brahmā's city called Aparājitā [the Invincible], and a golden canopy made specially by the Lord.

### 8.5.4

तद्य एवैतवरं च ण्यं चार्णवौ ब्रह्मलोके ब्रह्मचर्येणानुविन्दन्ति तेषामेवैष ब्रह्मलोकस्तेषां सर्वेषु लोकेषु कामचारो भवति ॥ ८.५.४ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चमः खण्डः ॥

*tadya evaitavaraṃ ca ṇyaṃ cārṇavau brahmaloke brahmacaryeṇānuvindanti teṣāmevaiṣa brahmalokasteṣāṃ sarveṣu lokeṣu kāmacāro bhavati || 8.5.4 || || iti pañcamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

When they reach the two oceans Ara and Ṇya in Brahmaloka through brahmacarya, that Brahmaloka belongs to them, and they can then move freely through all the worlds.

### 8.6.1

अथ या एता हृदयस्य नाड्यस्ताः पिङ्गलस्याणिम्नस्तिष्ठन्ति शुक्लस्य नीलस्य पीतस्य लोहितस्येत्यसौ वा आदित्यः पिङ्गल एष शुक्ल एष नील एष पीत एष लोहितः ॥ ८.६.१ ॥

*atha yā etā hṛdayasya nāḍyastāḥ piṅgalasyāṇimnastiṣṭhanti śuklasya nīlasya pītasya lohitasyetyasau vā ādityaḥ piṅgala eṣa śukla eṣa nīla eṣa pīta eṣa lohitaḥ || 8.6.1 ||*

These veins connected with the heart are each filled with a thin liquid, reddish-yellow, white, blue, yellow, and red. The sun there has these same colours: reddish-yellow, white, blue, yellow, and red.

### 8.6.2

तद्यथा महापथ आतत उभौ ग्रामौ गच्छतीमं चामुं चैवमेवैता आदित्यस्य रश्मय उभौ लोकौ गच्छन्तीमं चामुं चामुष्मादादित्यात्प्रतायन्ते ता आसु नाडीषु सृप्ता आभ्यो नाडीभ्यः प्रतायन्ते तेऽमुष्मिन्नादित्ये सृप्ताः ॥ ८.६.२ ॥

*tadyathā mahāpatha ātata ubhau grāmau gacchatīmaṃ cāmuṃ caivamevaitā ādityasya raśmaya ubhau lokau gacchantīmaṃ cāmuṃ cāmuṣmādādityātpratāyante tā āsu nāḍīṣu sṛptā ābhyo nāḍībhyaḥ pratāyante te'muṣminnāditye sṛptāḥ || 8.6.2 ||*

As a long, broad road joins one village to another far away, so the rays go out from the sun and reach a person, linking the two. They enter that person's veins, come out of them, and return to the sun.

### 8.6.3

तद्यत्रैतत्सुप्तः समस्त्ः सम्प्रसन्नः स्वप्नं न विजानात्यासु तदा नाडीषु सृप्तो भवति तं न कश्चन पाप्मा स्पृशति तेजसा हि तदा सम्पन्नो भवति ॥ ८.६.३ ॥

*tadyatraitatsuptaḥ samastḥ samprasannaḥ svapnaṃ na vijānātyāsu tadā nāḍīṣu sṛpto bhavati taṃ na kaścana pāpmā spṛśati tejasā hi tadā sampanno bhavati || 8.6.3 ||*

When a person is sound asleep, all his organs are quiet and inactive. He is free from all worry and has no dreams. The organs then vanish into the veins. No sin can touch him at that time, for the sun's rays surround him.

### 8.6.4

अथ यत्रैतदबलिमानं नीतो भवति तमभित आसीना आहुर्जानासि मां जानासि मामिति स यावदस्माच्छरीरादनुत्क्रान्तो भवति तावज्जानाति ॥ ८.६.४ ॥

*atha yatraitadabalimānaṃ nīto bhavati tamabhita āsīnā āhurjānāsi māṃ jānāsi māmiti sa yāvadasmāccharīrādanutkrānto bhavati tāvajjānāti || 8.6.4 ||*

When a person grows weak, his relatives sit around him and keep asking, 'Do you know me? Do you know me?' He can recognize them as long as he has not left the body.

### 8.6.5

अथ यत्रैतदस्माच्छरीरादुत्क्रामत्यथैतैरेव रश्मिभिरूर्ध्वमाक्रमते स ओमिति वा होद्वा मीयते स यावत्क्षिप्येन्मनस्तावदादित्यं गच्छत्येतद्वै खलु लोकद्वारं विदुषां प्रपदनं निरोधोऽविदुषाम् ॥ ८.६.५ ॥

*atha yatraitadasmāccharīrādutkrāmatyathaitaireva raśmibhirūrdhvamākramate sa omiti vā hodvā mīyate sa yāvatkṣipyenmanastāvadādityaṃ gacchatyetadvai khalu lokadvāraṃ viduṣāṃ prapadanaṃ nirodho'viduṣām || 8.6.5 ||*

When a person leaves the body, he rises with the help of these rays. If he dies meditating on Om, his ascent is assured; otherwise it is not. In the time his mind takes to pass from one thought to another, he can reach the realm of the sun. The sun is the gateway to Brahmaloka. Those who know the meaning of Om and think of it at death enter Brahmaloka; those ignorant of it have no chance of entering.

### 8.6.6

तदेष श्लोकः । शतं चैका च हृदयस्य नाड्यस्तासां मूर्धानमभिनिःसृतैका । तयोर्ध्वमायन्नमृतत्वमेति विष्वङ्ङन्या उत्क्रमणे भवन्त्युत्क्रमणे भवन्ति ॥ ८.६.६ ॥ ॥ इति षष्ठः खण्डः ॥

*tadeṣa ślokaḥ | śataṃ caikā ca hṛdayasya nāḍyastāsāṃ mūrdhānamabhiniḥsṛtaikā | tayordhvamāyannamṛtatvameti viṣvaṅṅanyā utkramaṇe bhavantyutkramaṇe bhavanti || 8.6.6 || || iti ṣaṣṭhaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

A verse says this: A hundred and one arteries are connected with the heart. One of them rises to the crown of the head. Whoever goes up by this artery attains immortality. The others go in various directions and lead a person out of the body in other ways.

### 8.7.1

य आत्मापहतपाप्मा विजरो विमृत्युर्विशोको विजिघत्सोऽपिपासः सत्यकामः सत्यसंकल्पः सोऽन्वेष्टव्यः स विजिज्ञासितव्यः स सर्वांश्च लोकानाप्नोति सर्वांश्च कामान्यस्तमात्मानमनुविद्य विजानातीति ह प्रजापतिरुवाच ॥ ८.७.१ ॥

*ya ātmāpahatapāpmā vijaro vimṛtyurviśoko vijighatso'pipāsaḥ satyakāmaḥ satyasaṃkalpaḥ so'nveṣṭavyaḥ sa vijijñāsitavyaḥ sa sarvāṃśca lokānāpnoti sarvāṃśca kāmānyastamātmānamanuvidya vijānātīti ha prajāpatiruvāca || 8.7.1 ||*

Prajāpati once said: 'The Self is free from sin, old age, death, sorrow, hunger, and thirst. It gives rise to the desire for Truth and to commitment to Truth. This Self must be sought and thoroughly known. Whoever has sought and known the Self attains all worlds and all desires.'

### 8.7.2

तद्धोभये देवासुरा अनुबुबुधिरे ते होचुर्हन्त तमात्मानमन्वेच्छामो यमात्मानमन्विष्य सर्वांश्च लोकानाप्नोति सर्वांश्च कामानितीन्द्रो हैव देवानामभिप्रवव्राज विरोचनोऽसुराणां तौ हासंविदानावेव समित्पाणी प्रजापतिसकाशमाजग्मतुः ॥ ८.७.२ ॥

*taddhobhaye devāsurā anububudhire te hocurhanta tamātmānamanvecchāmo yamātmānamanviṣya sarvāṃśca lokānāpnoti sarvāṃśca kāmānitīndro haiva devānāmabhipravavrāja virocano'surāṇāṃ tau hāsaṃvidānāveva samitpāṇī prajāpatisakāśamājagmatuḥ || 8.7.2 ||*

The gods and the demons both heard from people what Prajāpati had said. They said, 'We will search for that Self, by knowing which we can attain all the worlds and whatever we desire.' With this aim, Indra from among the gods and Virocana from among the demons went to Prajāpati with fuel in their hands, neither telling the other of his plans.

### 8.7.3

तौ ह द्वात्रिंशतं वर्षाणि ब्रह्मचर्यमूषतुस्तौ ह प्रजापतिरुवाच किमिच्छन्तावास्तमिति तौ होचतुर्य आत्मापहतपाप्मा विजरो विमृत्युर्विशोको विजिघत्सोऽपिपासः सत्यकामः सत्यसंकल्पः सोऽन्वेष्टव्यः स विजिज्ञासितव्यः स सर्वांश्च लोकानाप्नोति सर्वांश्च कामान्यस्तमात्मानमनुविद्य विजानातीति भगवतो वचो वेदयन्ते तमिच्छन्ताववास्तमिति ॥ ८.७.३ ॥

*tau ha dvātriṃśataṃ varṣāṇi brahmacaryamūṣatustau ha prajāpatiruvāca kimicchantāvāstamiti tau hocaturya ātmāpahatapāpmā vijaro vimṛtyurviśoko vijighatso'pipāsaḥ satyakāmaḥ satyasaṃkalpaḥ so'nveṣṭavyaḥ sa vijijñāsitavyaḥ sa sarvāṃśca lokānāpnoti sarvāṃśca kāmānyastamātmānamanuvidya vijānātīti bhagavato vaco vedayante tamicchantāvavāstamiti || 8.7.3 ||*

The two lived there as brahmacarins for thirty-two years. One day Prajāpati asked them, 'Why are you staying here?' They answered: '"The Self is free from sin, old age, death, sorrow, hunger, and thirst. It gives rise to the desire for Truth and to commitment to Truth. This Self must be sought and thoroughly known. Whoever has learned of the Self and known It attains all worlds and all desires." Sir, this is your message. We wish to know that Self, and that is why we are here.'

### 8.7.4

तौ ह प्रजापतिरुवाच य एषोऽक्षिणि पुरुषो दृश्यत एष आत्मेति होवाचैतदमृतमभयमेतद्ब्रह्मेत्यथ योऽयं भगवोऽप्सु परिख्यायते यश्चायमादर्शे कतम एष इत्येष उ एवैषु सर्वेष्वन्तेषु परिख्यायत इति होवाच ॥ ८.७.४ ॥ ॥ इति सप्तमः खण्डः ॥

*tau ha prajāpatiruvāca ya eṣo'kṣiṇi puruṣo dṛśyata eṣa ātmeti hovācaitadamṛtamabhayametadbrahmetyatha yo'yaṃ bhagavo'psu parikhyāyate yaścāyamādarśe katama eṣa ityeṣa u evaiṣu sarveṣvanteṣu parikhyāyata iti hovāca || 8.7.4 || || iti saptamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Prajāpati told the two, 'What is seen in the eyes is the Self.' He added, 'This Self is immortal and fearless. It is Brahman.' They then asked, 'Lord, we see something when we look in water and again when we look in a mirror. Which is it?' Prajāpati answered, 'The Self is seen in all of these.'

### 8.8.1

उदशराव आत्मानमवेक्ष्य यदात्मनो न विजानीथस्तन्मे प्रब्रूतमिति तौ होदशरावेऽवेक्षांचक्राते तौ ह प्रजापतिरुवाच किं पश्यथ इति तौ होचतुः सर्वमेवेदमावां भगव आत्मानं पश्याव आ लोमभ्यः आ नखेभ्यः प्रतिरूपमिति ॥ ८.८.१ ॥

*udaśarāva ātmānamavekṣya yadātmano na vijānīthastanme prabrūtamiti tau hodaśarāve'vekṣāṃcakrāte tau ha prajāpatiruvāca kiṃ paśyatha iti tau hocatuḥ sarvamevedamāvāṃ bhagava ātmānaṃ paśyāva ā lomabhyaḥ ā nakhebhyaḥ pratirūpamiti || 8.8.1 ||*

[Prajāpati said:] 'Look at yourselves in a vessel of water. If you have any doubts about the Self, tell me.' They looked at themselves in the water, and Prajāpati asked, 'What do you see?' They answered, 'We see a reflection of our entire self, even our hair and nails.'

### 8.8.2

तौ ह प्रजापतिरुवाच साध्वलंकृतौ सुवसनौ परिष्कृतौ भूत्वोदशरावेऽवेक्षेथामिति तौ ह साध्वलंकृतौ सुवसनौ परिष्कृतौ भूत्वोदशरावेऽवेक्षांचक्राते तौ ह प्रजापतिरुवाच किं पश्यथ इति ॥ ८.८.२ ॥

*tau ha prajāpatiruvāca sādhvalaṃkṛtau suvasanau pariṣkṛtau bhūtvodaśarāve'vekṣethāmiti tau ha sādhvalaṃkṛtau suvasanau pariṣkṛtau bhūtvodaśarāve'vekṣāṃcakrāte tau ha prajāpatiruvāca kiṃ paśyatha iti || 8.8.2 ||*

Prajāpati told them, 'Dress well, put on fine clothes, make yourselves neat and clean, and then look into the pan of water.' So the two dressed well in fine clothes, made themselves neat and clean, and looked into the water. Prajāpati asked, 'What do you see?'

### 8.8.3

तौ होचतुर्यथैवेदमावां भगवः साध्वलंकृतौ सुवसनौ परिष्कृतौ स्व एवमेवेमौ भगवः साध्वलंकृतौ सुवसनौ परिष्कृतावित्येष आत्मेति होवाचैतदमृतमभयमेतद्ब्रह्मेति तौ ह शान्तहृदयौ प्रवव्रजतुः ॥ ८.८.३ ॥

*tau hocaturyathaivedamāvāṃ bhagavaḥ sādhvalaṃkṛtau suvasanau pariṣkṛtau sva evamevemau bhagavaḥ sādhvalaṃkṛtau suvasanau pariṣkṛtāvityeṣa ātmeti hovācaitadamṛtamabhayametadbrahmeti tau ha śāntahṛdayau pravavrajatuḥ || 8.8.3 ||*

The two said, 'Revered sir, just as we are well dressed in fine clothes, neat and clean, so are these two reflections well dressed in fine clothes, neat and clean.' Prajāpati said, 'That is the Self. It is immortal and fearless. It is Brahman.' The two then went away, happy in mind.

### 8.8.4

तौ हान्वीक्ष्य प्रजापतिरुवाचानुपलभ्यात्मानमननुविद्य व्रजतो यतर एतदुपनिषदो भविष्यन्ति देवा वासुरा वा ते पराभविष्यन्तीति स ह शान्तहृदय एव विरोचनोऽसुराञ्जगाम तेभ्यो हैतामुपनिषदं प्रोवाचात्मैवेह महय्य आत्मा परिचर्य आत्मानमेवेह महयन्नात्मानं परिचरन्नुभौ लोकाववाप्नोतीमं चामुं चेति ॥ ८.८.४ ॥

*tau hānvīkṣya prajāpatiruvācānupalabhyātmānamananuvidya vrajato yatara etadupaniṣado bhaviṣyanti devā vāsurā vā te parābhaviṣyantīti sa ha śāntahṛdaya eva virocano'surāñjagāma tebhyo haitāmupaniṣadaṃ provācātmaiveha mahayya ātmā paricarya ātmānameveha mahayannātmānaṃ paricarannubhau lokāvavāpnotīmaṃ cāmuṃ ceti || 8.8.4 ||*

Watching them go, Prajāpati said to himself: 'They are leaving without having realized or understood anything about the Self. Whoever among them, god or demon, takes the teaching this way [believing the body to be the Self] will be lost.' Virocana, the king of the demons, returned to the demons with a contented mind and explained this upaniṣad to them:

### 8.8.5

तस्मादप्यद्येहाददानमश्रद्दधानमयजमानमाहुरासुरो बतेत्यसुराणां ह्येषोपनिषत्प्रेतस्य शरीरं भिक्षया वसनेनालंकारेणेति संस्कुर्वन्त्येतेन ह्यमुं लोकं जेष्यन्तो मन्यन्ते ॥ ८.८.५ ॥ ॥ इति अष्टमः खण्डः ॥

*tasmādapyadyehādadānamaśraddadhānamayajamānamāhurāsuro batetyasurāṇāṃ hyeṣopaniṣatpretasya śarīraṃ bhikṣayā vasanenālaṃkāreṇeti saṃskurvantyetena hyamuṃ lokaṃ jeṣyanto manyante || 8.8.5 || || iti aṣṭamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

That is why, even today, people in this world say, 'Oh, he is a demon,' of someone who has no feeling of charity, shows no respect for others, and never bothers to perform a sacrifice, for the demons hold that the body is the Self. When someone dies, they adorn the body with all kinds of offerings, new clothes, and jewelry, believing that by this the person will conquer the other world.

### 8.9.1

अथ हेन्द्रोऽप्राप्यैव देवानेतद्भयं ददर्श यथैव खल्वयमस्मिञ्छरीरे साध्वलंकृते साध्वलंकृतो भवति सुवसने सुवसनः परिष्कृते परिष्कृत एवमेवायमस्मिन्नन्धेऽन्धो भवति स्रामे स्रामः परिवृक्णे परिवृक्णोऽस्यैव शरीरस्य नाशमन्वेष नश्यति नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीति ॥ ८.९.१ ॥

*atha hendro'prāpyaiva devānetadbhayaṃ dadarśa yathaiva khalvayamasmiñcharīre sādhvalaṃkṛte sādhvalaṃkṛto bhavati suvasane suvasanaḥ pariṣkṛte pariṣkṛta evamevāyamasminnandhe'ndho bhavati srāme srāmaḥ parivṛkṇe parivṛkṇo'syaiva śarīrasya nāśamanveṣa naśyati nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmīti || 8.9.1 ||*

But before Indra had got back to the gods, a doubt came to him: 'When the body is finely adorned, so is the reflection. When the body wears fine clothes, so does the reflection. When the body is neat and clean, so is the reflection. Then again, suppose a person is blind. The reflection will show a blind body. If the body is lame, the reflection will show a lame body. If the body is injured in some way, the reflection will show that too. And if the body is destroyed, the reflection disappears. I can't see any good coming from this.'

### 8.9.2

स समित्पाणिः पुनरेयाय तं ह प्रजापतिरुवाच मघवन्यच्छान्तहृदयः प्राव्राजीः सार्धं विरोचनेन किमिच्छन्पुनरागम इति स होवाच यथैव खल्वयं भगवोऽस्मिञ्छरीरे साध्वलंकृते साध्वलंकृतो भवति सुवसने सुवसनः परिष्कृते परिष्कृत एवमेवायमस्मिन्नन्धेऽन्धो भवति स्रामे स्रामः परिवृक्णे परिवृक्णोऽस्यैव शरीरस्य नाशमन्वेष नश्यति नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीति ॥ ८.९.२ ॥

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punareyāya taṃ ha prajāpatiruvāca maghavanyacchāntahṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ sārdhaṃ virocanena kimicchanpunarāgama iti sa hovāca yathaiva khalvayaṃ bhagavo'smiñcharīre sādhvalaṃkṛte sādhvalaṃkṛto bhavati suvasane suvasanaḥ pariṣkṛte pariṣkṛta evamevāyamasminnandhe'ndho bhavati srāme srāmaḥ parivṛkṇe parivṛkṇo'syaiva śarīrasya nāśamanveṣa naśyati nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmīti || 8.9.2 ||*

Indra came back with fuel in hand. Prajāpati asked: 'Indra, you went away with Virocana in a contented frame of mind. What has brought you back?' Indra answered: 'Lord, when the body is finely adorned, so is the reflection. When the body wears fine clothes, so does the reflection. When the body is neat and clean, so is the reflection. Then again, suppose a person is blind. The reflection will show a blind body. If the body is lame, the reflection will show a lame body. If the body is injured in some way, the reflection will show that too. And if the body is destroyed, the reflection disappears. I can see no good in this.'

### 8.9.3

एवमेवैष मघवन्निति होवाचैतं त्वेव ते भूयोऽनुव्याख्यास्यामि वसापराणि द्वात्रिंशतं वर्षाणीति स हापराणि द्वात्रिंशतं वर्षाण्युवास तस्मै होवाच ॥ ८.९.३ ॥ ॥ इति नवमः खण्डः ॥

*evamevaiṣa maghavanniti hovācaitaṃ tveva te bhūyo'nuvyākhyāsyāmi vasāparāṇi dvātriṃśataṃ varṣāṇīti sa hāparāṇi dvātriṃśataṃ varṣāṇyuvāsa tasmai hovāca || 8.9.3 || || iti navamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Prajāpati said: 'Indra, that is so. I will explain the matter to you again. Stay here for thirty-two more years.' Indra stayed there for another thirty-two years. Then Prajāpati said to him:

### 8.10.1

य एष स्वप्ने महीयमानश्चरत्येष आत्मेति होवाचैतदमृतमभयमेतद्ब्रह्मेति स ह शान्तहृदयः प्रवव्राज स हाप्राप्यैव देवानेतद्भयं ददर्श तद्यद्यपीदं शरीरमन्धं भवत्यनन्धः स भवति यदि स्राममस्रामो नैवैषोऽस्य दोषेण दुष्यति ॥ ८.१०.१ ॥

*ya eṣa svapne mahīyamānaścaratyeṣa ātmeti hovācaitadamṛtamabhayametadbrahmeti sa ha śāntahṛdayaḥ pravavrāja sa hāprāpyaiva devānetadbhayaṃ dadarśa tadyadyapīdaṃ śarīramandhaṃ bhavatyanandhaḥ sa bhavati yadi srāmamasrāmo naivaiṣo'sya doṣeṇa duṣyati || 8.10.1 ||*

'The one who moves about being worshipped in dreams is the Self. It is immortal and fearless. It is Brahman.' Indra went away with a contented mind. But before he had got back to the gods, a doubt came to him: 'A person may be blind, yet in a dream he is not blind. He may be lame, yet in a dream he is not lame. His body may have certain defects, yet his dream body is untouched by them.'

### 8.10.2

न वधेनास्य हन्यते नास्य स्राम्येण स्रामो घ्नन्ति त्वेवैनं विच्छादयन्तीवाप्रियवेत्तेव भवत्यपि रोदितीव नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीति ॥ ८.१०.२ ॥

*na vadhenāsya hanyate nāsya srāmyeṇa srāmo ghnanti tvevainaṃ vicchādayantīvāpriyavetteva bhavatyapi roditīva nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmīti || 8.10.2 ||*

'The body may be killed, but the other is not killed. Nor is the dream body lame when the body is lame. Even so, in dreams it may seem that people are killing him or chasing him, or that something unpleasant is happening. He may even seem to be crying. I can see no good in this.'

### 8.10.3

स समित्पाणिः पुनरेयाय तं ह प्रजापतिरुवाच मघवन्यच्छान्तहृदयः प्राव्राजीः किमिच्छन्पुनरागम इति स होवाच तद्यद्यपीदं भगवः शरीरमन्धं भवत्यनन्धः स भवति यदि स्राममस्रामो नैवैषोऽस्य दोषेण दुष्यति ॥ ८.१०.३ ॥

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punareyāya taṃ ha prajāpatiruvāca maghavanyacchāntahṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ kimicchanpunarāgama iti sa hovāca tadyadyapīdaṃ bhagavaḥ śarīramandhaṃ bhavatyanandhaḥ sa bhavati yadi srāmamasrāmo naivaiṣo'sya doṣeṇa duṣyati || 8.10.3 ||*

Indra came back with fuel in hand. Prajāpati asked: 'Indra, you went away with a contented mind. What has brought you back?' Indra answered: 'Lord, a person may be blind, yet in a dream he is not blind. He may be lame, yet in a dream he is not lame. His body may have certain defects, yet his dream body is untouched by them.'

### 8.10.4

न वधेनास्य हन्यते नास्य स्राम्येण स्रामो घ्नन्ति त्वेवैनं विच्छादयन्तीवाप्रियवेत्तेव भवत्यपि रोदितीव नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीत्येवमेवैष मघवन्निति होवाचैतं त्वेव ते भूयोऽनुव्याख्यास्यामि वसापराणि द्वात्रिंशतं वर्षाणीति स हापराणि द्वात्रिंशतं वर्षाण्युवास तस्मै होवाच ॥ ८.१०.४ ॥ ॥ इति दशमः खण्डः ॥

*na vadhenāsya hanyate nāsya srāmyeṇa srāmo ghnanti tvevainaṃ vicchādayantīvāpriyavetteva bhavatyapi roditīva nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmītyevamevaiṣa maghavanniti hovācaitaṃ tveva te bhūyo'nuvyākhyāsyāmi vasāparāṇi dvātriṃśataṃ varṣāṇīti sa hāparāṇi dvātriṃśataṃ varṣāṇyuvāsa tasmai hovāca || 8.10.4 || || iti daśamaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

'The body may be killed, but the other is not killed. Nor is the dream body lame when the body is lame. Even so, in dreams it may seem that people are killing him or chasing him, or that something unpleasant is happening. He may even seem to be crying. I can see no good in this.' Prajāpati said: 'Indra, that is so. I will explain the matter to you again. Stay here for thirty-two more years.' Indra stayed there for another thirty-two years. Then Prajāpati said to him:

### 8.11.1

तद्यत्रैतत्सुप्तः समस्तः सम्प्रसन्नः स्वप्नं न विजानात्येष आत्मेति होवाचैतदमृतमभयमेतद्ब्रह्मेति स ह शान्तहृदयः प्रवव्राज स हाप्राप्यैव देवानेतद्भयं ददर्श नाह खल्वयमेवं सम्प्रत्यात्मानं जानात्ययमहमस्मीति नो एवेमानि भूतानि विनाशमेवापीतो भवति नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीति ॥ ८.११.१ ॥

*tadyatraitatsuptaḥ samastaḥ samprasannaḥ svapnaṃ na vijānātyeṣa ātmeti hovācaitadamṛtamabhayametadbrahmeti sa ha śāntahṛdayaḥ pravavrāja sa hāprāpyaiva devānetadbhayaṃ dadarśa nāha khalvayamevaṃ sampratyātmānaṃ jānātyayamahamasmīti no evemāni bhūtāni vināśamevāpīto bhavati nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmīti || 8.11.1 ||*

Prajāpati said: 'When the self is asleep, with all its organs at rest, it has no worry and no dreams. This is what the Self is like [that is, spotless]. It is immortal and fearless. It is Brahman.' Indra went away with a contented mind. But before he had got back to the gods, a doubt troubled him: 'In deep sleep the self cannot recognize itself as "I am so-and-so," as it does when awake. What is more, it does not even recognize the beings around it. It is as though the self has been wiped out. I can't see any good coming from this.'

### 8.11.2

स समित्पाणिः पुनरेयाय तं ह प्रजापतिरुवाच मघवन्यच्छान्तहृदयः प्राव्राजीः किमिच्छन्पुनरागम इति स होवाच नाह खल्वयं भगव एवं सम्प्रत्यात्मानं जानात्ययमहमस्मीति नो एवेमानि भूतानि विनाशमेवापीतो भवति नाहमत्र भोग्यं पश्यामीति ॥ ८.११.२ ॥

*sa samitpāṇiḥ punareyāya taṃ ha prajāpatiruvāca maghavanyacchāntahṛdayaḥ prāvrājīḥ kimicchanpunarāgama iti sa hovāca nāha khalvayaṃ bhagava evaṃ sampratyātmānaṃ jānātyayamahamasmīti no evemāni bhūtāni vināśamevāpīto bhavati nāhamatra bhogyaṃ paśyāmīti || 8.11.2 ||*

Indra came back with fuel in hand. Prajāpati asked: 'Indra, you went away with a contented mind. What has brought you back?' Indra answered: 'Lord, in deep sleep the self cannot recognize itself as "I am so-and-so," as it does when awake. What is more, it does not even recognize the beings around it. It is as though the self has been wiped out. I can see no good coming from this.'

### 8.11.3

एवमेवैष मघवन्निति होवाचैतं त्वेव ते भूयोऽनुव्याख्यास्यामि नो एवान्यत्रैतस्माद्वसापराणि पञ्च वर्षाणीति स हापराणि पञ्च वर्षाण्युवास तान्येकशतं सम्पेदुरेतत्तद्यदाहुरेकशतं ह वै वर्षाणि मघवान्प्रजापतौ ब्रह्मचर्यमुवास तस्मै होवाच ॥ ८.११.३ ॥ ॥ इति एकादशः खण्डः ॥

*evamevaiṣa maghavanniti hovācaitaṃ tveva te bhūyo'nuvyākhyāsyāmi no evānyatraitasmādvasāparāṇi pañca varṣāṇīti sa hāparāṇi pañca varṣāṇyuvāsa tānyekaśataṃ sampeduretattadyadāhurekaśataṃ ha vai varṣāṇi maghavānprajāpatau brahmacaryamuvāsa tasmai hovāca || 8.11.3 || || iti ekādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Prajāpati said: 'Indra, that is so. I will explain the matter to you again. Stay here for five more years.' Indra stayed there for another five years. In all, Indra spent one hundred and one years in this way. This is what sages mean when they say, 'Indra lived with Prajāpati for one hundred and one years practicing brahmacarya.' Then Prajāpati said to him:

### 8.12.1

मघवन्मर्त्यं वा इदं शरीरमात्तं मृत्युना तदस्यामृतस्याशरीरस्यात्मनोऽधिष्ठानमात्तो वै सशरीरः प्रियाप्रियाभ्यां न वै सशरीरस्य सतः प्रियाप्रिययोरपहतिरस्त्यशरीरं वाव सन्तं न प्रियाप्रिये स्पृशतः ॥ ८.१२.१ ॥

*maghavanmartyaṃ vā idaṃ śarīramāttaṃ mṛtyunā tadasyāmṛtasyāśarīrasyātmano'dhiṣṭhānamātto vai saśarīraḥ priyāpriyābhyāṃ na vai saśarīrasya sataḥ priyāpriyayorapahatirastyaśarīraṃ vāva santaṃ na priyāpriye spṛśataḥ || 8.12.1 ||*

Indra, this body is mortal. Death has taken hold of it. Yet it is the base of the Self, which is immortal and has no form. Whoever has a body is subject to both happiness and unhappiness. Indeed, as long as one has a body, there is no end to happiness and unhappiness. But when a person is free of the body, neither good nor bad can touch him.

### 8.12.2

अशरीरो वायुरभ्रं विद्युत्स्तनयित्नुरशरीराण्येतानि तद्यथैतान्यमुष्मादाकाशात्समुत्थाय परं ज्योतिरुपसम्पद्य स्वेन रूपेणाभिनिष्पद्यन्ते ॥ ८.१२.२ ॥

*aśarīro vāyurabhraṃ vidyutstanayitnuraśarīrāṇyetāni tadyathaitānyamuṣmādākāśātsamutthāya paraṃ jyotirupasampadya svena rūpeṇābhiniṣpadyante || 8.12.2 ||*

Air has no form. Neither do clouds, lightning, or thunder. All of these rise from the sky and take on their particular forms through the heat of the sun.

### 8.12.3

एवमेवैष सम्प्रसादोऽस्माच्छरीरात्समुत्थाय परं ज्योतिरुपसम्पद्य स्वेन रूपेणाभिनिष्पद्यते स उत्तमपुरुषः स तत्र पर्येति जक्षत्क्रीडन्रममाणः स्त्रीभिर्वा यानैर्वा ज्ञातिभिर्वा नोपजनं स्मरन्निदं शरीरं स यथा प्रयोग्य आचरणे युक्त एवमेवायमस्मिञ्छरीरे प्राणो युक्तः ॥ ८.१२.३ ॥

*evamevaiṣa samprasādo'smāccharīrātsamutthāya paraṃ jyotirupasampadya svena rūpeṇābhiniṣpadyate sa uttamapuruṣaḥ sa tatra paryeti jakṣatkrīḍanramamāṇaḥ strībhirvā yānairvā jñātibhirvā nopajanaṃ smarannidaṃ śarīraṃ sa yathā prayogya ācaraṇe yukta evamevāyamasmiñcharīre prāṇo yuktaḥ || 8.12.3 ||*

In the same way, the joyful self rises from the body and, reaching the light of the Cosmic Self, appears in his own form. This is the Paramātman, the Cosmic Self. He then moves about freely, eating, playing, or enjoying himself with women, carriages, or relatives, with no memory at all of the body in which he was born. As horses or bullocks are harnessed to carriages, so prāṇa [life] stays harnessed to the body [because of karma].

### 8.12.4

अथ यत्रैतदाकाशमनुविषण्णं चक्षुः स चाक्षुषः पुरुषो दर्शनाय चक्षुरथ यो वेदेदं जिघ्राणीति स आत्मा गन्धाय घ्राणमथ यो वेदेदमभिव्याहराणीति स आत्माभिव्याहाराय वागथ यो वेदेदं शृणवानीति स आत्मा श्रवणाय श्रोत्रम् ॥ ८.१२.४ ॥

*atha yatraitadākāśamanuviṣaṇṇaṃ cakṣuḥ sa cākṣuṣaḥ puruṣo darśanāya cakṣuratha yo vededaṃ jighrāṇīti sa ātmā gandhāya ghrāṇamatha yo vededamabhivyāharāṇīti sa ātmābhivyāhārāya vāgatha yo vededaṃ śṛṇavānīti sa ātmā śravaṇāya śrotram || 8.12.4 ||*

Next, this organ of sight lies within the space in the eyes. That is where the deity presiding over the eyes [the Self] resides. The eye is the instrument the Self sees with. Next, the one who knows 'I am smelling this' is the Self. The organ of smell is the instrument it smells with. Next, the one who knows 'I am speaking this' is the Self. The organ of speech is the instrument it speaks with. Next, the one who knows 'I hear this' is the Self. The organ of hearing is the instrument it hears with.

### 8.12.5

अथ यो वेदेदं मन्वानीति सात्मा मनोऽस्य दैवं चक्षुः स वा एष एतेन दैवेन चक्षुषा मनसैतान्कामान्पश्यन्रमते य एते ब्रह्मलोके ॥ ८.१२.५ ॥

*atha yo vededaṃ manvānīti sātmā mano'sya daivaṃ cakṣuḥ sa vā eṣa etena daivena cakṣuṣā manasaitānkāmānpaśyanramate ya ete brahmaloke || 8.12.5 ||*

Then, it is the Self that knows 'I am thinking this.' The mind is its divine eye. Now free, the Self delights in seeing all that it wishes to see in Brahmaloka through that divine mental eye.

### 8.12.6

तं वा एतं देवा आत्मानमुपासते तस्मात्तेषां सर्वे च लोका आत्ताः सर्वे च कामाः स सर्वांश्च लोकानाप्नोति सर्वांश्च कामान्यस्तमात्मानमनुविद्य विजानातीति ह प्र्जापतिरुवाच प्रजापतिरुवाच ॥ ८.१२.६ ॥ ॥ इति द्वादशः खण्डः ॥

*taṃ vā etaṃ devā ātmānamupāsate tasmātteṣāṃ sarve ca lokā āttāḥ sarve ca kāmāḥ sa sarvāṃśca lokānāpnoti sarvāṃśca kāmānyastamātmānamanuvidya vijānātīti ha prjāpatiruvāca prajāpatiruvāca || 8.12.6 || || iti dvādaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

The gods worship this Self. That is why all worlds and all desirable things are within their reach. Whoever fully understands and realizes this Self [with the help of teachers and the scriptures] can reach whatever worlds and gain whatever desirable things he wants. This is what Prajāpati taught Indra.

### 8.13.1

श्यामाच्छबलं प्रपद्ये शबलाच्छ्यामं प्रपद्येऽश्व इव रोमाणि विधूय पापं चन्द्र इव राहोर्मुखात्प्रमुच्य धूत्वा शरीरमकृतं कृतात्मा ब्रह्मलोकमभिसंभवामीत्यभिसंभवामीति ॥ ८.१३.१ ॥ ॥ इति त्रयोदशः खण्डः ॥

*śyāmācchabalaṃ prapadye śabalācchyāmaṃ prapadye'śva iva romāṇi vidhūya pāpaṃ candra iva rāhormukhātpramucya dhūtvā śarīramakṛtaṃ kṛtātmā brahmalokamabhisaṃbhavāmītyabhisaṃbhavāmīti || 8.13.1 || || iti trayodaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

From the dark may I reach the diverse. From the diverse may I reach the dark. As a horse shakes its coat [to get rid of dirt], I will shake off any stain on my character. As the moon frees itself from the mouth of Rāhu [and regains its brightness], so I, having accomplished everything, will lay down this body and reach that eternal Brahmaloka.

### 8.14.1

आकाशो वै नाम नामरूपयोर्निर्वहिता ते यदन्तरा तद्ब्रह्म तदमृतं स आत्मा प्रजापतेः सभां वेश्म प्रपद्ये यशोऽहं भवामि ब्राह्मणानां यशो राज्ञां यशोविशां यशोऽहमनुप्रापत्सि स हाहं यशसां यशः श्येतमदत्कमदत्कं श्येतं लिन्दु माभिगां लिन्दु माभिगाम् ॥ ८.१४.१ ॥ ॥ इति चतुर्दशः खण्डः ॥

*ākāśo vai nāma nāmarūpayornirvahitā te yadantarā tadbrahma tadamṛtaṃ sa ātmā prajāpateḥ sabhāṃ veśma prapadye yaśo'haṃ bhavāmi brāhmaṇānāṃ yaśo rājñāṃ yaśoviśāṃ yaśo'hamanuprāpatsi sa hāhaṃ yaśasāṃ yaśaḥ śyetamadatkamadatkaṃ śyetaṃ lindu mābhigāṃ lindu mābhigām || 8.14.1 || || iti caturdaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

What is described as space brings forth names and forms. These names and forms exist within Brahman. Brahman is immortal; it is the Self. May I come to the court of Prajāpati. May I gain the fame of a brāhmin, and also that of a prince and a merchant. I wish for real fame. I want to be famous among all who are famous. May I never again have to be born with a body covered in blood and dirt, toothless and yet always wanting to eat.

### 8.15.1

तधैतद्ब्रह्मा प्रजापतयै उवाच प्रजापतिर्मनवे मनुः प्रजाभ्यः आचार्यकुलाद्वेदमधीत्य यथाविधानं गुरोः कर्मातिशेषेणाभिसमावृत्य कुटुम्बे शुचौ देशे स्वाध्यायमधीयानो धर्मिकान्विदधदात्मनि सर्वैन्द्रियाणि सम्प्रतिष्ठाप्याहिंसन्सर्व भूतान्यन्यत्र तीर्थेभ्यः स खल्वेवं वर्तयन्यावदायुषं ब्रह्मलोकमभिसम्पद्यते न च पुनरावर्तते न च पुनरावर्तते ॥ ८.१५.१ ॥ ॥ इति पञ्चदशः खण्डः ॥

*tadhaitadbrahmā prajāpatayai uvāca prajāpatirmanave manuḥ prajābhyaḥ ācāryakulādvedamadhītya yathāvidhānaṃ guroḥ karmātiśeṣeṇābhisamāvṛtya kuṭumbe śucau deśe svādhyāyamadhīyāno dharmikānvidadhadātmani sarvaindriyāṇi sampratiṣṭhāpyāhiṃsansarva bhūtānyanyatra tīrthebhyaḥ sa khalvevaṃ vartayanyāvadāyuṣaṃ brahmalokamabhisampadyate na ca punarāvartate na ca punarāvartate || 8.15.1 || || iti pañcadaśaḥ khaṇḍaḥ ||*

Brahmā taught this knowledge of the Self to Prajāpati, Prajāpati taught it to Manu, and Manu in turn taught it to all human beings. A young man goes to live in his teacher's house and serves him, and in his free time he studies the Vedas in the prescribed way. Having finished all his studies, he returns home and marries. But he goes on studying the scriptures in a sacred place. He also teaches his children and disciples so that they will be religious. He keeps all his senses under control and avoids violence except at a holy place. In this way he lives his whole life. After death he goes to Brahmaloka, and he is not born again, he is not born again.
